# AGENTS.md

Rules for AI agents and people changing Ooga Booga Land. Read it before changing anything. The bar: dependency-free JavaScript at engine quality, correct, measured, allocation-conscious and consistent with the modules around it.

## What this is

A WebGL2 floating island whose cliff caves are projects. The page lands on the hub. The open caves are the EntropyLab lab, where donated bananas feed voxel cavemen who stand for its contributors; B.C.A. (Banana Certified Accounting) at 10 o'clock, an office inside the hub where the gorillas put on suits through its veil and count bananas at two desks; the Lightning Factory, a Lightning node at work, at 2 o'clock; and Ooga Arcade at 3 o'clock, whose cabinets open the games (Cloud Hopper, Ooga Drop, Ooga Orbit, Ooga Mine; Block Frenzy is reached down the upper hall by its zipline; Rally is beside Invaders across the fire bowl, under repair and out of order), which have no other entrance on the island. A vine bridge at 4 o'clock reaches the Mempool island, a rainforest round a lake that stands as deep as the transaction backlog, with a chamber under the lake, walked into down a tunnel, whose wall paintings read the chain out. ₿IFRÖST is a gateway islet past an arch over the north pass, whose chamber has a window through to DSB Land and mirrors for the worlds still to come. The Timechain Sphere off the southwest rim is one doorway with a keypad to ten rooms, each its own kind of place under a dome that is all screen (0 the Planetarium, 1 the Conference Room of live block and mining charts and the one that locks, 2 Satoshi's Temple, 3 the Block Party, 4 the Fireside Lounge, 5 Proof of Work-out, 6 the Observatory, 7 the Mining Museum, 8 the Lecture Hall, 9 the Movie Theater), a scene of their own, shared signed in and solo signed out. A stone bridge from the top of the south stairs, under the Oogatron, reaches Repo Island: a carved totem for each of 170 open-source Bitcoin and Bitcoin-adjacent repositories, in sections, as tall and lit as the meadow totems' own rule says and thicker as they matter more, the island slowly turning under a stone lip its bridge meets. Pile, crew, effects and loot crates are shared systems, and the pile level is shared everywhere.

The page is static with five live feeds: the mempool.space socket (`mempool.js`), the chain snapshot (`chain.js`: REST from mempool.space or Esplora, plus the Coinbase price socket and its REST fallbacks), the oogatron stats poller (`oogatron-live.js`: the jumbotron every minute) and the totems' GitHub poller (`totem-feed.js`: when Bitcoin Core, BTCPay Server, LND and CLN last merged a PR into main/master, through the Worker's `/api/totems/<i>` where there is one, scanning with its optional `GITHUB_TOKEN`) and Repo Island's (`repo-feed.js`: every island repository's latest merge into its default branch in one reply from the Worker's `/api/repo-totems` every five minutes, which needs that token); and one read that is not live, the Lightning Jet Hangar's boards' look at their repository on GitHub, once a page life (`jet-feed.js`). Personal saves live in localStorage; shared game high scores live in the Worker's D1 database (see `docs/highscores.md`). Donations are simulated unless the page's API is in use: bananapayserver (its contract is that repository's `docs/protocol.md`), reached at the page's own address once its Worker says so (`/api/me`'s `donations`, from the Worker's `DONATIONS` binding to bananapayserver's `PageApi`): the Worker passes `/donations/*` on and names the donor from its own sign-in. Real mode goes through `donations.js` alone, labels donations on a test network (signet on staging) as tests, and in it a demo tip (L) does nothing. Simulated donations start at 69 sats; real donations follow the API's `minSats` (69 when unset). A message rides only a tip of at least 1,000 sats (`MESSAGE_MIN_SATS`, or the API's `messageMinSats`), so tiny tips cannot speak; the Bananas panel's recent list shows every amount, with a checkbox (on until a visitor turns it off, remembered in localStorage `ooga-recent-bar`) that keeps it to tips at or above the bar. Served from Cloudflare, the page sits behind the Worker in `worker/`, which adds GitHub sign-in (`docs/auth-and-presence.md`); served anywhere else it finds no backend and runs as it always has. Player controls are in the README.

## Ground rules

- Read a file before editing it.
- Never run git commands. The maintainer commits. No branches, stashes or `.gitignore` changes unless asked.
- Vanilla JavaScript only: no frameworks, TypeScript, bundler, npm dependencies, external scripts or fonts. `package.json` holds only the build and test scripts.
- Keep the content policy strict. `src/index.html` allows `self` scripts and styles and `connect-src 'self' https: wss:` (an owner decision so the page can poll its feeds; `'self'` lets local `wrangler dev` reach the Worker over http); the build pins inline blocks by hash, and the Worker's pages (`--page-assets`) load the main script and stylesheet as same-origin files instead. Never add `unsafe-inline`, `unsafe-eval`, or a non-`self` script or style origin.
- Smallest change that works. No refactors, reformatting or renames the task does not need. Match the surrounding style.
- No console noise. The suite fails a check if the console is not clean.
- Testing follows the Testing section. Never weaken or skip a check.

## Repository

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `src/index.html`, `src/style.css`, `src/js/` | the sources; open `src/index.html` directly after one `npm run build`, which writes `src/js/characters.gen.js` |
| `src/characters/` | one file per contributor with a custom look, voice or alias |
| `oogaboogaland.html` | the built single-file page; gitignored, built with `npm run build`, committed back by CI's `artifact` job after merges to `rock`. Never commit it by hand |
| `scripts/characters.mjs` | joins `src/characters/*.js` into the gitignored `src/js/characters.gen.js`; the build and the suite run it first |
| `scripts/build.mjs` | inlines `src/` in script order and pins the content-policy hashes |
| `scripts/site.mjs`, `scripts/serve.mjs` | `npm run site [dir]` builds and stages the deployed site (default `untracked/site`). Search engines and link crawlers judge a page by the HTML at its address, so every route gets the whole built page under its own head (title, description, canonical address, preview card): `index.html`, and `<route>.html`, which GitHub Pages answers at `/<route>` with a 200. Plus `404.html` (`noindex`; sends a miscased or slash-ended route to its address, anything else home), `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, `cards/`, and the favicons as files (`scripts/distribution-icons.mjs`: the page's own 32px and 64px PNGs at `/favicon-32.png` and `/favicon-64.png`, which every staged page links in place of its embedded copies, both unchanged in `/favicon.ico`, and the existing 180px `/apple-touch-icon.png` kept unchanged with its `-precomposed` twin), so no address a browser or crawler asks for unprompted answers 404. `--audio-assets` writes the DSB entrance recordings as same-origin files under `audio/` and points the page at them (`scripts/distribution-audio.mjs`); `--image-assets` does the same under `art/` for the pictures in `PICTURE_MODULES` (`externalizePictures` in `scripts/distribution-modules.mjs`): the DSB rooms' as they enter the deferred `dsb` bundle, which drops that bundle from about 1.7 MB (0.97 MB gzip) to 0.6 MB (0.2 MB gzip), and Cloud Hopper's title and result art as it enters `cloud-hopper`, which keeps 1.2 MB of barely compressible base64 out of every first load. `npm run build:site` and `npm run serve` pass both, and either flag can be used locally. `--page-assets`, the last step, moves the page's own script and stylesheet out of every route page into `js/main-<hash>.js` and `css/style-<hash>.css`, with the jumbotron's snapshot, which every deploy rebakes, in its own `js/jumbotron-data-<hash>.js` loaded just before the code (`externalizePage`), so the 24 pages share one cached copy and a data-only deploy keeps the code's; the policy keeps `'self'` and the `data-early` hashes. `--headers <file>` writes that file as `_headers` plus one immutable rule per file under `js/` and `css/`, by exact path, so a 404 there is never cached. `npm run build:site` passes both; `npm run serve` keeps the page's script inline. `npm run serve` stages it and serves it as Pages does; test path routes there |
| `scripts/cards.mjs`, `cards/` | `npm run cards` captures every route's 1200x630 preview card with the suite's Chrome driver (from disk, through `?scene=` and the hub's `view=`) into `cards/<image>.jpg`, with the clock frozen at noon UTC so no card shows when or where it was taken (committed; hand-made art may replace any). The Mempool route's card keeps the live feeds on, so it waits for a chain snapshot and for the simulator's first donation toast to pass. a route without an `image` in `routes.js` shows home's card, and `site.mjs` refuses to stage when a named card is missing |
| `scripts/emoji-data.mjs` | bakes `src/js/emoji-data.js` for reactions: pulls Unicode's `emoji-test.txt` (Emoji 15.1, or a path given) and keeps every fully-qualified emoji up to Emoji 15.0 in CLDR order with its name and, where it takes a skin tone, its light-skin form, checking the other four tones are listed. Run by hand; on any failure it keeps the committed file |
| `.github/workflows/pages.yml` | deploys nothing (the site is the Cloudflare Worker; github.io only redirects there). On pushes to `rock` and the character bot's `merge_sha` dispatches, the `build` job reconciles contributors, refreshes the jumbotron snapshot and builds the portable page, and the `artifact` job tests that and commits it to `rock`. The build job's token stays read-only with `persist-credentials: false`; only the separate `artifact` job takes `contents: write`, and only on merges to `rock`. Keep that split. A commit that loses the race to a later merge ends as superseded, since that merge's own run commits the page, and the `automation/site-artifact-*` staging branch is deleted however the job ends |
| `test/run.mjs`, `test/browser.mjs` | the suite and its headless Chrome driver, the whole of `test/` |
| `worker/` | the Cloudflare Worker: GitHub sign-in, D1 sessions and `/api/*` in `src/`, the room, `/game/<kind>/<venue>` (`game-host.js`: `GameHost`, one Durable Object a hosted game's instance, apart from the room, named only from `games.js`'s registry, its rules on the server's clock, every hit decided there; `games/ghost-hunt.js`, HALLOWEEN, the ghost hunt; see `docs/game-host.md`), `/api/arcade-plays` (`arcade-plays.js`: every cabinet opened to a run, counted per game per UTC day with no identity, atomic upserts, `0010_cabinet_plays.sql`, read over the last 30 days through the edge cache), `/donations/*` passed on to bananapayserver (`donations.js`: only the page's body and type, the signed-in donor, the visitor's address; `closed` when it can't be reached), `/api/tess` (`tess.js`: Tess's answers from Claude Haiku 5.5 with `tess-rules.js` and the fact sheet `build:site` writes from the page's own data (`scripts/tess-facts.mjs`, the gitignored `worker/src/tess-facts.gen.json`), kept in D1 by the folded question, limited by `TESS_LIMITER` per address and `TESS_DAILY_CENTS` a day, `closed` without `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, when `/api/me`'s `tess` is false and the page doesn't ask; her rules make her a girl and a Bitcoin maximalist), `/api/totems/<i>` (`totems.js`: the four totems' latest main/master merge, one GitHub scan per repository shared through the edge cache, authorized by the optional `GITHUB_TOKEN` secret, one per Worker, which never reaches a log, the cache or a reply; a 401 finishes the scan unauthenticated), `/api/repo-totems` (`repo-totems.js`: Repo Island's merges, every repository in `repo-island-data.js` asked of GitHub's GraphQL API a few dozen a query with the same token, which it needs (closed without one), one scan a six-minute edge cache shared and the last good one answering a refusal for a day), `/api/cloud-hopper-replays/<board>/*` (`cloud-hopper-replays.js`: authenticated session-bound run tickets and binary finishes, public immutable downloads; `cloud-hopper-replay-store.js`: current-winner coherence and scheduled orphan/retired-object cleanup through the optional `CLOUD_HOPPER_REPLAYS` R2 binding; migration `0006_cloud_hopper_replays.sql` adds the bounded registry, tickets and pointers, with setup in `docs/cloud-hopper-replays.md`), `eval/` (Tess's eval, run by hand with `npm run eval` on every change to her rules, the fact sheet or the model: `tess-questions.jsonl` by category, graded by code and by Claude Sonnet 5.5 against a rubric, each run kept in `untracked/tess-eval/`; never part of `npm test`), migrations, `node --test` checks. Its own `package.json` holds `wrangler`, `@anthropic-ai/sdk` and the deploy scripts, so the root one keeps only build and test scripts. `npm run build:site` at the root stages the site with the DSB recordings, the DSB rooms' pictures and the page's own script and stylesheet as same-origin files and `worker/_headers` (plus an immutable rule for each hashed file) into the gitignored `_site/` it serves, marking those pages as Worker-served so a failed account lookup cannot open the demo donation popup |
| `wrangler.staging.jsonc`, `wrangler.production.jsonc` | the two Workers, `oogaboogaland-staging` and `oogaboogaland-production`, in one account: the same code, each with its own D1, room, hosted games' objects (`GAMES`), OAuth App, Realtime app and secrets. Staging binds `bananapayserver-staging` as `DONATIONS`, production `bananapayserver-production` |
| `.github/workflows/cloudflare-staging.yml`, `cloudflare-production.yml` | both by hand, production only on `rock`: a fresh jumbotron snapshot, `build:site`, the Worker's locked dependencies (`npm ci --prefix worker --omit=dev`), the Worker's checks, D1 migrations, `wrangler deploy`. Read-only token and no cron, because every deploy drops live sockets. Setup in `docs/cloudflare-setup.md` |
| `docs/` | contracts and runbooks: activity, sign-in, the networking inventory, Cloudflare setup |
| `untracked/` | local planning notes, ignored by git |

Nothing to install. `npm test` needs Node 22 or newer and Chrome; the driver looks at the macOS application path, so elsewhere set `CHROME` to the binary. `npm run test:unit` needs neither Chrome nor a build. Deploy only the staged site: the workflows run `npm run build:site` and upload `_site` (the page as `index.html` and each `<route>.html`, `404.html`, `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, `cards/`), never the source tree, and does not run the browser suite.

## Modules and load order

Classic scripts, each an IIFE with `"use strict"`, sharing `window.BL`. A file exposes `BL.<name>` after its file name (`gl-renderer.js` is `BL.glRenderer`), a scene registers on `BL.scenes.<id>`, and `director.js` exposes `window.__ooga` under debug only. The `<script>` tags in `src/index.html` are the dependency and build order: a new module goes after everything it uses, and `director.js` loads last.

| File | Job |
|---|---|
| `controls-help.js` | Native controls dialog beside the speaker. Each scene supplies `controlsHelp(touch)` rows and optional `controlsHelpHold(on)` input cleanup. The pilot reads its live equipment; games reuse coaching copy. Movement bindings and discrete shortcuts come from `controls.js`, shared with handlers. The director holds local simulation while the dialog is open (a scene with a live session sets `runsBehindPanels` to keep stepping, with its player's input ignored); a pad keeps working in it through the Menus context, its Controller settings button opens the controller screen at any time, and when the controller was the last input the panel lists the scene's pad context with the player's own button names. Network connections stay live. Keep key practice isolated, clear holds on close/blur/mode changes, and preserve focus return. |
| `sound.js` | Page-wide environment mute (`oogaboogaland.audio`), shared by the banner speaker and, while voice is off, M in every scene. Audio modules subscribe and unsubscribe on disposal; short-lived effects use `output(context)`. While voice is enabled or joining, the director reserves M for microphone mute before scene handlers, except in input fields and editable content; `voice.setMuted` never acquires a microphone (a visitor back in voice with the microphone closed, `stats.returning`, gets `voice.toggle` from M instead) |
| `qr.js` | QR code for the donation link |
| `banana-icon.js` | The Bananas panel's nine-pixel banana as painted canvas pixels and solid sign geometry, shared by the Factory donation board and kiosk |
| `donation-rate.js` | One shared 2140data socket while a real kiosk or the Bananas panel is open, paused while hidden, for live estimates only; `watch(onRate)` optionally receives price and stale updates, and the server prices receipts independently. Off under `nosim` and `rate=0` (`stop`), as the other live feeds are |
| `donation-popup.js` | The mainland donation dialog: the kiosk flow, real (Lightning/on-chain invoices) or simulated (`open(true)`: the page's `/donate` address as its QR, a simulated payment handed to the director as a donation), opens without changing scenes, handles seen payments, and closes on settlement so the current scene shows the delivery. The name field remembers this browser's donor names (`game.state.recentHandles`): typing shows the prefix matches, the arrow the whole list, Down previews a match, Tab or leaving the field accepts it, Escape restores the typed prefix, and a tap picks one. Plain Tab preserves a typed name and moves to the message. It owns the name and message fields both kiosks share: hidden but focusable from script alone (out of the tab order), so a phone's keyboard opens on them, kept to the characters a donation carries, their text, focus and selection passed to the kiosk to draw (`typing`), `focusDetails(field)` putting the typing in one (null takes it out, and gives the mainland popup's canvas the focus back), Enter going from the name to the message, Tab between the two, and Enter or Escape finishing like the screen's DONE. Owned and disposed by the director; available signed in or out |
| `math.js` | `mat4`, unit `quat`, easing, damping, hashing, `rayFromView`, stable `sortByKey` with caller-owned `sortScratch` |
| `daylight.js` | the local solar clock: sun, moon and star frame, sky and light factors, six phases; `sample`, `createClock` |
| `scene.js` | nodes (a node with a `quaternion` turns by it instead of its Euler `rotation`), world transforms, camera, bounds cache, tweens |
| `mirror-ripples.js`, `mirror-body.js` | the unbreakable mirror's impact waves and the cave entrances' body-contact glyph atlas |
| `character-visibility.js` | shared geometry bakes and visibility scratch for partly visible characters |
| `compact-geometry.js` | on by default (`?compactGeometry=0` turns it off): packs finished, immutable render-only meshes (voxel character bakes, foliage LODs, the grass tuft, cutaway caps) into typed columns; readers walk faces through `createCursor`/`readFace`, builders copying faces into a new mesh (`merge`, a track's sector bake) take `copyFace`, and writing a packed mesh's faces or reassigning its `faces` or `verts` throws (its typed positions cannot refuse an element write). Turned off, `pack` returns the mesh untouched |
| `terrain-cache.js`, `terrain-snapshot.js`, `terrain-worker.js` | On in the staged site where the hub's keep rule keeps the hub (`TERRAIN_CACHE` in `director.js`: a memory report of at least 8 GiB, or a fine pointer without one; phones and smaller devices would pay for the fill's second island build and never read the cache for a released hub's rebuild). `?terrainworker=1` forces it and `?terrainworker=0` stops it; the portable page and the source tree carry no Worker, so they build as before: a boot of the hub, Drop or Orbit restores its island from IndexedDB when this build has stored it. A miss builds the island on the main thread as usual, and once the page is ready, at idle and never during a transition, `fill` has a same-origin classic Worker (`npm run site` writes it as `js/terrain-worker-<hash>.js` with `scripts/distribution-terrain.mjs`, `terrain-cache.js` inside it) build a data-only snapshot and store it itself, so the snapshot never crosses to the page. The cache is read only at boot, keeps two snapshots of this build with the Worker's build CPU (the restore's `credit` to `tierFromBoot`), and is skipped when storage is missing or throws. `terrain-snapshot.js` rehydrates a snapshot (no code is sent, and a query-source checksum refuses a stale build); structured clone returns every plain array with generic elements, so the road tile's `verts`, which the renderer reads beside every model's, is rebuilt as a double array, or V8 boxes every model's vertices. Any failure falls back to the ordinary build |
| `gl-renderer.js` | WebGL2: instancing, culling, shadows, sky, sea (distance haze blends into the sky at the same viewing angle; an angular fade keeps the horizon continuous even near sea level; with `seaFog` and `fog` it blends into the fog's own colour over `fogNear` to `fogFar` instead, as the meshes do, and the sky just over the horizon fades into that colour too, so no line shows between them), a geometry's `fogPierce` (0 to 1) taking that share of the fog off it so a glowing thing shows through haze (Thunderhead's tornadoes and lightning; the canvas renderer honours it too), clouds, point lights (`POINT_LIGHT_CAPACITY` 32; tiers draw 32/20/10), wind sway, water, lava and road faces (roads cut to marching-squares edges from the 8-neighbour mask `terrain.js` writes into each path tile), `smooth` geometry (averaged vertex normals) and explicit per-vertex `normals` (zeros mean unset), voxel block detail from a geometry's `voxel` grid, `projective` geometry (its instance matrices may carry a last row, and the world position is divided per fragment: emissive, `castShadow: false`, no `lines`, drawn as a batch with a world `cullSphere`, never picked) and `clipSlab` / `clipSlab2` (`[nx, ny, nz, d]` in the world; drops what lies where `|n·p + d| > 1`), both honoured by the canvas renderer too, an `imageSurface` with `cutout` (a sprite: a hard edge where its picture's alpha crosses a half, or with `soft` its alpha, times the node's `smokeOpacity` as ordered coverage, `bright` its bloom; with `blend` ("add" or "alpha") truly blended in its own pass after glass, depth read but not written; the Canvas 2D renderer draws it without a face behind it), a canvas-backed `imageSurface` (`canvas`, bumped `version`, `rect`, optional `background`, `curve`, `tessellated` and `smooth` (drawn up with linear filtering on both renderers, for a small canvas spread wide): the jumbotron and the Mempool paintings) kept as one retained texture re-uploaded only when its `version` or size moves (Canvas 2D shades it once a surface a frame on 8-bit light, in a bounded cache), a depth prepass on tiers that set `prepass` except on a named Apple silicon GPU (tile-based; `?debug=1&prepass=0|1` overrides it), `screenCull` on a node (an opted-in prop leaves the camera pass under about two pixels across, never shadows or reflections), `foliageLods` medium and far render meshes picked by screen size with hysteresis (collision and Canvas 2D keep the original), glossy reflectors (a `reflector` geometry with `gloss`, its strength: a polished floor that adds its reflection, softened and stronger toward grazing, over what is drawn there, with no face of its own; its reflection taken at half the tier's mirror size, every frame on High (a reused one trails the floor while the eye moves), every other frame on Medium and on Low's own cadence), lower-tier reflections (the hub mirror stays live at every visible size, Low capped at 256 pixels and one capture per four frames, distant captures up to eight frames apart; other planar reflectors stay live at every visible size, with the same tier caps and capture cadence; environment cubes on every tier) that keep only large instances and every geometry marked `characterPart`, bloom and grade |
| `canvas-renderer.js` | the Canvas 2D fallback with the same API |
| `models.js` | procedural geometry and shared builders (`box`, `bevelBox` for chunky timber, `lathe`, `merge`, `cutCylinder` for a build-time bore through flat meshes, `makeVox`, `voxelGeometry`, `cached`; `moved`, `turnedX`, `turnedY`, `turnedZ` move and turn a built part in place, so a model file composes these rather than writing its own; the interior kit the cave interiors share: `beam`, `turn` and `shaded` for smooth turned parts, `torus`, `chain`, `deckParts` (its rails where `deckRailSpans` lays them, which a floor shares to stop walkers at them), `railParts`, `columnParts`, `stairParts` (rungs across a flight, or `stepped` deep plank treads with a riser under each, at `stairTreads`' heights, so a floor can stand walkers on the treads drawn), `lanternParts`), the lab room, `caveman`, props, crates, `SWAG`. The caveman's belt and loincloth never draw from `k.rand`. Every Ooga is authored on sixteenths of its height and drawn at `FINE` (4) times that detail, as Kortik and his sister are: once every hook has run, the head is split cell for cell and rounded at the back, over the crown and under it, its face (the front plane from the brow down) kept cell for cell, and the face button is cut from that same head, and a hair cell the ragged-hair pass cut from the middle of a flat stretch behind the face goes back, so no hair shows a hole; the torso is `TORSO_RISE` (1) taller than authored, its chest rows stretched and the head and arms lifted with it, narrower at the waist over the belt for a slim build (the others' lower torso keeps its width) and fuller through the chest, whose top rows run out into each arm's shoulder (`CHEST`), with its edges rounded (`rounded`, which spares anything too thin to round); its hip band is refitted to the thighs (`hips`) and tapers in to the waist (`BAND_IN`), and the authored loin flaps become a close wrap round the thighs (only on an Ooga whose own model wore flaps, so trousers, shorts or a suit drawn instead stay bare), a broad panel before and behind and a shorter one down each hip, hemmed ragged in `k.loin`'s colours; arms and legs are drawn anew from smooth profiles in authored units (`ARM_PROFILE`, `LEG_PROFILE`: a deltoid swelling into the shoulder and rolling over level with the torso's top; a shin easing into the thigh), every shape given in units so any `FINE` draws it, and speckle and weave hashed on half-unit grains (`grainMix`) over plain block fists and feet a size under the authored ones, with no finger nubs or toes (the finger nodes hold one cell hidden in the fist), and the legs stand `LEGS_IN` (half a unit) closer; colours come from the authored part region by region, keeping whatever a hook built out past it, and cloth a part wears widely gets a weave of shades (`weaves`, which a tint shades from its own palette); what a hook hung on the torso or the root over the hip keeps its place on the longer torso (`keepOn`: a piece beside an arm rises with it, one before the hip band comes back with its front, one on the chest's front comes out with the deeper chest); legs run `LEG_UNITS` (7) from hip to sole, `FOOT_UNITS` (1.5) of it the foot, so `baseY` and `headOffset` follow them. Anything that stands an Ooga somewhere reads its hip from the build (its root's `position.y`), never a fixed share of its height; `BODY` gives the hip and the torso's rise as shares of height for what is laid out before an Ooga is built (Heimdall). Nothing there draws from `k.rand`. Fine bakes merge into same-coloured rectangles (`voxelGeometry`'s `rects`). `characterGeometry` marks a geometry `characterPart`, which lower-tier reflections keep whatever its size: the caveman marks its whole build (weapon skins, head swaps, tint twins), and swag, the Agent's forms and clothing and the worn jetpack and flame are marked where they are built, so a new worn or carried piece is marked too. `foliageLods(high, coarse)` gives a foliage mesh its own medium and far render meshes |
| `blaze.js` | the page's fires: the arcade hall's smooth `blaze` geometry and its one flicker clock (`flare`), the island's voxel flame rig (`createVoxelRig`: four bands of glowing blocks rising off a coal bed, a slow gust, a click flare read from the root's `flare`, an optional ember/spark pool, a `light` level for the scene's lamp, and `boxes` for fire contact), and the ember models (`emberModel`, `buildEmberModel`, `emberGeometry`) the hub's critters and a rig's own nodes draw. The hub's lamp loop hands a rig `lampK`, `lampGlow` and `lampBase` so the lamp's lit glow and click flare reach the blocks. The hub pauses a rig while its pit is far, advancing the rig's own clock only nearby. Nothing here allocates per frame; a rig's blocks and ember pool are built once |
| `convex.js` | `sweptCylinder`: GJK of a body's swept cylinder against rock pieces, for `terrain.js` |
| `terrain.js` | voxel grid, greedy meshing, the island: `heightAt`, `surfaceAt`, `onLand`, `isPath`, mouths |
| `hub-models.js` | cached hub props, `caveSign`/`postSign`, `SIGN_ICONS` and the signs' `SIGN_BADGES`. Hub trees and bushes use closed, faceted `clumped` foliage: both mesh levels keep every lobe, with fewer facets at medium, and flowers and berries sit on the same tips. Other dressing keeps `leafy` clumps of two-sided leaf cards lit by canopy normals. `puff`, `flower` and `limb` are shared builders; tree `collisionGeometry` keeps the voxel trunk and roots, while the actual canopy lobes are clipped and capped above the walking headroom plane. Their upper surfaces match the visible crown; lower leaves provide landing support without blocking walkers. Both detail levels share that collision mesh. `flatInto` appends flat-shaded parts (timber, boxes) to a `smooth` geometry and `padNormals` fills zeros after `limb`, so one mesh mixes both. The jungle kit dresses a built body in its own mesh: `withJungle(body, grow)` copies it into a smooth geometry with normals for `grow` to write `liana` vines (`sagPoints` for a slung one), `sprig` leaves and leafy `garland`s into; `bamboo`, `lashing` and `log` are round parts for `shaded`, `thatch` a flat one. `SIGN_GLYPHS`, the 3x5 alphabet every carved sign uses, must stay complete: a missing key silently drops a letter; `CARVED_M` is the five-wide M carved signs and the arena's gargoyles use, since a three-wide M reads as an H |
| `hub-hammock.js` | one face-up resting place between palms in the same grass patch beside Ooga Arcade. `plan` rejects any path crossing along the full anchor span and keeps the bed's swing and stand-up point clear; a companion palm is planted only if no existing pair fits. The wraps are children of the palms with matching local heights and renderer sway; direct suspension ends follow the animated trunk transforms and share the frame wind clock with the renderer. Each end fan converges on the line from its spreader center to the tree, keeping the fan centerline and suspension collinear as they sway, using fixed cord meshes and node transforms. The crew seat has `lying: true`; `enter` reserves it through the crew contract and blends the standing position and orientation into rest over 0.9 seconds. `reachable` covers both ground beside the cloth and its walkable top; `leave` blends back to the validated standing spot over the same duration, holding occupancy until the crew completes a safe release. Normal Space/movement requests animated standing; forced cleanup remains immediate. The pilot reuses its bed camera limits and restores normal view handling on exit. Occupancy retains the existing pose inference and lower-room-id tie-break. A body walking into the bed (`bump`: the visitor's speed from the hub's position changes, another player's from successive synced poses) and a banana round crossing its swing (`crosses`) each add to the sway with `kick(strength)`, a walk-in by its speed toward the bed (a body going along or away from it, such as an Ooga just rolled out of it, never pushes), a banana a third of a hard one; each page rocks its own copy from the same events, nothing is sent, and none of it allocates. The bed hangs where its original planned swing (`PLAN_SWAY`, 0.11) fits, so the bigger swing never moves it; pushes swing it up to `SWAY_MAX` (0.4), and that outline (`swing`) is what pushes, rounds and a tipped Ooga's landing spot go by. Pushes and bananas climb the sway in steps the bed remembers: every hit adds to one `count` shared by everyone (a run or plain walk into the side is 1 however long it is held against it, one contact being one push until the body has stepped 0.8 m clear, a burst of the rifle 1 however short (rounds with no gap over 0.4 s) and each further round of a held trigger 3/19 more, so a held trigger tips an occupant out by itself at the 20th round of a magazine of 30, and a gentle push under half a hit only eases the swing up), the count forgets itself after `RESET_S` (a minute) without a hit or when the bed is left, and each hit lifts the swing to the count's height whatever it has eased down to since; a counted push also shoves the bed along (`LEAN`, about 11 degrees at a walk, eased in over `LEAN_RISE` and then easing out) and plays the pusher's `crew.pushPose`, both arms easing out and back (this page only: the room's pose carries no push flag), a hit also sets the phase so the bed sets off away from the pusher or shooter that very frame (a round along its shot across the bed), an occupied bed is a wall to walk into (`blocks`, checked in the hub's `walkable`; an empty one is stepped onto), and one timed with the swing adds up to `MOMENTUM` more height, never to the count. The count reaching `TIP_COUNT` (four) while someone lies in the bed is `spilling`, and only the occupant's own page tips them out: `tip` rolls the body over the edge on the side the bed was last knocked toward (`fallSide`), onto the ground beyond the stand-up spot, lies it there and gets it up on the spot over `FALL_SECONDS` (1.5 s; `stateFor` reports it no longer rests from the start), then the hub stands it up through the usual forced get-up, so another page's pose never empties the bed from here; each page counts the hits it saw. Climbing in settles the bed to the occupied rest with the count at zero, and the seat is free at once. Lying in it, the visitor cannot rock the bed: every direction, left and right included, gets them up. A knock or gentle push that lifts the swing past `FALL` without reaching the count tips the visitor's own Ooga out the same way; the swing stays per page, so other pages see the body move, not the bed. Removing either palm or leaving vacates the bed and clears remote pose markers. |
| `dressing.js` | the one set-dressing kit (see Set dressing) |
| `lifehash.js` | LifeHash v2 from Blockchain Commons (BSD-2-Clause-Patent; keep the header notice) |
| `jumbotron-data.js`, `jumbotron.js` | baked oogatron stats (schema 3; the Worker's pages load it as its own file before the main script, so it reads nothing at load but `window.BL`) and the hub board. `refreshData` takes live payloads; the module never fetches. The board draws through one canvas-backed `imageSurface`, repainted in place with its `version` bumped, its layouts' chrome as prepared alternative geometries. Its 5x7 `FONT` is shared with the Mempool chamber's wall paintings; an unknown character draws as a box |
| `headquarters-models.js`, `headquarters-sleep.js` | HQ and basement geometry (17 bed slots reused across visits: five HQ rooms and two nooks, ten basement rooms) and the validated waypoint graph to the beds: `route`, `plan` (the same route a leg at a time; the crew spreads bed trips over frames, a few milliseconds each), `clearSegment`; `outdoorNavigation` joins rainforest beds to the existing bridge, ridge, nest and chamber corridors |
| `hole-guides.js`, `wall-apertures.js`, `slope-guides.js`, `cave-guides.js`, `rock-guides.js`, `object-guides.js`, `sight-guides.js`, `pile-guides.js`, `banana-cover.js`, `mirror-guides.js`, `camera-cover.js` | the visibility stack, below |
| `caves.js` | the eight cave slots (clock, status, scene, name, repo, `theme`, `soon`, `link` for a sign's repository that is no work site yet (the Arcade's), and `place` with `title` for a room inside the hub, B.C.A. at c10) and the gate |
| `characters.js`, `contributors.js` | the registry `src/characters/` calls (`add`, `get`, `all`; rejects duplicate handles), and the roster built from it: oogatron activity (schemas 1-3), working, chilling and sleeping, hashed traits |
| `donations.js` | the event contract, `sanitize`, `MESSAGE_MIN_SATS` (1,000: the fewest sats a tip needs to carry a message; `messageMinSats` is the API's bar once its status names one), the simulator, and real mode: the API's address (the page's own once the director passes on its Worker's word, `useOrigin`, even after the simulator has started), the network the socket's status names (`testNetwork`, "" on mainnet), `invoice` (its `bolt11` null when the API couldn't make the Lightning half in time), `note` and `onchain` (an address to pay that invoice on-chain) checked against its contract, `waitFor`, `waitingOn` and `waited` (the invoices the kiosk let a visitor go from with an on-chain address shown, at most 16 for the page life, so the director can say one was theirs when it is seen and when it confirms), and `subscribe`'s socket, which sends nothing, reconnects with backoff from the last donation it played and skips one already played, and passes on each donation with the API's banana count, the pile, the leaderboard, each on-chain payment seen before its block (`onSeen(id, sats, due, left)`) and `state.open`, keeps the newest twenty donations of any amount and the newest twenty at or above the message bar on `state.recent` (`keepRecent`, which the demo's list shares, so the recent list fills twenty rows whichever way its checkbox is set), and keeps the API's latest tally on `state.tally` (every donation it holds, all time, the last seven days in UTC hours and its current rate) |
| `mempool.js` | the mempool.space socket: `block`, `fees`, `stats`, backoff, `STALL_MS` redial. Never `want` `track-mempool` (about 225 MB an hour). Off under `nosim` and `mempool=0` |
| `oogatron-live.js` | polls `/v2/stats` once a minute while visible. The director applies each stats snapshot to contributors for the page life, including outside the hub; the hub subscribes separately for its jumbotron. The HUD follows contributor changes and refreshes row ages each visible minute and on tab return. The `contribution` baseline is the first live poll, never the baked snapshot. Off under `nosim` and `oogatron=0` |
| `totem-feed.js` | the latest PR merged into main/master within 24 hours for the four totems' repositories (`REPOS`: `number`, `url`, `changedAt`) served by the Worker from its `/api/totems/<i>` summary (`worker/src/totems.js`: one scan shared through the edge cache, authorized by its optional `GITHUB_TOKEN`, the GitHub limit's cooldown shared too, with each repository's last good scan of the day answering while GitHub refuses; a 403, 429 or 503 there is waited out by its `retry-after`, reading api.github.com meanwhile only for a repository the page has no reading of yet; a 404, 405 or non-JSON reply there, as on a page with no Worker, falls back to api.github.com directly, unauthenticated, for the page life): all four on start, then one every 90 s in turn while visible, waiting out a spent GitHub limit until its reset (behind the Worker, still asking the Worker). A `change` event is `fresh` only when a new merge arrives during the page life, never on the first reading. Off under `nosim` and `totems=0` |
| `repo-island-data.js`, `repo-feed.js` | Repo Island's list and its merges. The data, shared unchanged by the page and the Worker (`repo-totems.js` imports it, as `protocol.js` does `voice-blend.js`): `SECTIONS` in the order their wedges run round the island (its sign's `name` and `badge`) and `REPOS`, each its canonical `repo`, `name`, carved `word` (2 to 7 letters, A to Z without X, never another's) and `importance`, its height as a share of Bitcoin Core's on the meadow's own scale (Core 1, LND 0.7, CLN 0.62, BTCPay Server 0.52, the same here). Adding a repository is one row; the island lays itself out again. The feed asks `/api/repo-totems` FIRST_MS after `start`, then every five minutes while visible (ETag revalidated, a refusal waited out by its `retry-after`), and a page with no Worker (404, 405, not JSON) stops for good, its totems dark, since asking GitHub for each would spend a visitor's own limit many times over; `apply` checks a reply at the boundary, `state.repos[i]` keeps `REPOS[i]`'s merge (`number`, `url`, `changedAt`), `state.version` counts changes, and subscribers hear `change` events, `fresh` only for a merge first seen during the page life. Off under `nosim` and `repos=0` |
| `chain.js` | the chain `snapshot`, mutated in place; `derive` sets `gale` (arrivals, which make the weather) and `soak` (the paying backlog, kept as data). `backlogAt`, `feesAt`, `heightAt` and `priceAt` stamp each category's last observation this session. Off under `chain=0` |
| `weather.js` | the Mempool island's weather: six `STEPS`, the cloud deck and the sky from arriving volume (`gale`, averaged over `STORM_TAU` and expired by `socketAt` age inside `update`, when it reads unavailable and eases to calm), wind from the same arrivals as they come, block strikes; `update(dt, opts, shelter)` muffles it under the island's ground; `create` returns the instance; `rainDrop` is also Cloud Hopper's storm rain |
| `weapon-targets.js` | cached triangle BVHs, weapon rays and melee contacts; the nearest contact does not depend on traversal order |
| `gamepad.js` | one controller, read once a frame at the top of the director's `step`: the last one pressed afresh while the pad in play has been still for a few reads (a held button never takes over, and a press Steam's twin mirrors in the same read or the next never swaps them), else the first connected, so a second pad or Steam's idle virtual twin never takes over; nothing is read while the window is unfocused. Three layers. **Normalize:** a standard-mapping pad passes through; any other goes through the profile learned for it in the controller screen's guided setup (`learn(id, { map, axes })`: each of the 18 places a button, an axis with its rest and direction, or a hat value; the four stick axes with their signs), keyed by vendor and product (`profileKey`); a pad with neither is not read and is named in `state.unknown`. **Bind:** `CONTEXTS` holds one table per context (`foot`, `race`, `drop`, `orbit`, `mine`, `arcade`, `menus`), each entry an `ACTION` bit read natively, a `key` the pad presses for the player, a `menu` verb, or a `stick` row; a scene names its context in `padContext` (default `foot`) and the director passes it to `setContext`. `bind(action, buttons, context)`, `bindings`, `resetBindings(context)`, `snapshot`/`restore` (the screen's Undo), saved per controller family (`familyOf`: Xbox, PlayStation, Switch, Stadia, other; `setFamily` overrides) with the feel (`setFeel`: dead zone, look speed (applied by `controls.js` after its clamp, so it raises the top turn rate), invert X and Y, swap sticks, run hold, aim toggle) in localStorage `ooga-pad-v1`, validated on load like `game.load`: a bad piece falls back to its default. Home and the touchpad are reserved, Menu always pauses (its row is fixed) and sticks swap rather than rebind; each throws. **Deliver:** `state.actions` (`held`, `pressed`, `released` over `ACTION` bits) for the native readers; a `key` entry dispatches that key's keydown on press and keyup on release on `document.body`, marked `pad` (`"shift+s"` with Shift), so a scene's own key handling serves the pad; a context with `stickKeys` turns the left stick into the d-pad's places; while `setMenu`'s function says a menu layer is open (`pad-focus.js`) the Menus context sends its verbs there and nothing reaches play. View and Menu together emit `settings`; losing the controller in a context with a `pauseKey` taps it (`tap`) and emits `disconnected`; `subscribe` also hears `device`, `unknown`, `bindings`, `family` and `feel`. `glyph(place)` and `nameOf(id)` name things the player's way. The physical bits `BUTTON` stay in `held`, `pressed` and `released`, with `physical` and `rawLx`… for the screen and `listen(fn)` handing it raw presses. `navigator.getGamepads` allocates, so it is read only while a pad is connected; a delivered key allocates its one event per press. By default `controls.js` takes `act` (A) as Space, `sprint` (left-stick click) as a run until the stick lets go (or while held), and `descend` (d-pad down) as X held; `pilot.js` takes `fire` (RT), `aim` (LT, down the sights, or a toggle), `reload` (X), `swapWeapon` (Y), `bash` (B) and `view` (View); the ghost hunt's lobby takes `act` (Ready) and `back` (B). A new native pad action is a bit in `ACTION` and an entry in `CONTEXTS.foot`, read from `state.actions`, never from a physical bit; a new game key is an entry in its context. `hold(on)` publishes neutral input while polling physical state, and held buttons require release/repress afterwards, as they do across a change of context. `rumble(strong, weak, ms)` vibrates the pad being read when it can (Chrome and Edge's `vibrationActuator`, Firefox's `hapticActuators`; nothing elsewhere; `canRumble`) scaled by `strength` (localStorage `ooga-vibration`, `setStrength`), `stop` ends it; a reader calls it a few times a second, never every frame. The rally, the drop and the orbit rumble on their collisions, boosts, landings and staging |
| `pad-focus.js` | a controller in menus: the topmost open layer (`LAYERS`: a modal dialog, a DSB menu, a title, results, pause or mine card, a garage, the orbit builder, the hangar's sound panel) takes the pad (`BL.gamepad.setMenu(open)`, answered from a cache a `MutationObserver` clears). `verb` moves focus to the nearest control in that direction (a range slider steps instead), A presses it or the layer's main button (`PRIMARY`), B is the layer's own Escape sent where its handler listens (a dialog that ignores it closes as the browser's Escape would), the bumpers page its tabs, a board's chevrons or its arrows, View opens the controller screen, and the right stick scrolls it (`scroll`). A card counts only while it has something to press, so cards that only show (the ghost hunt's round and results cards) leave the pad to play. The ring is `.pad-focus`; any pointer or real key takes it away. A new overlay that should take the pad joins `LAYERS` |
| `pad-settings.js` | the controller screen, `#pad-settings`, made by the director (`create`, `install`): the player's controller drawn in its family's layout, lit as pressed, sticks against the dead zone; a tab per context with each action's buttons; Change waits for every input to come up, then takes the next press (Menu, Escape or six seconds cancel), and a button already used asks Swap or Keep with Swap focused; Undo for five seconds and Reset this tab; the feel and vibration; the guided setup for a pad the browser doesn't map (a snapshot at rest, the first input well away from it answers each of 20 steps, nothing heard until all is back at rest). Opened from the controls panel's Controller settings, View in a menu, View and Menu together, and by itself for an unknown pad. It holds the scene as the controls panel does and runs a frame loop only while open |
| `interact.js`, `controls.js`, `cursor.js` | pointer gestures and picking; held keys, sticks and chords folded into one axes object per frame, R and F pitching the view unless a scene borrows them (`borrowPitch`, read as `keyPitch`); the virtual cursor and pointer lock. A wheel reports a zoom factor, including with Shift held; a plain sideways swipe stays inert |
| `pilot.js` | the visitor's view in any scene: the Ooga they drive (there is no view without one: Escape frees a captured cursor or ends a look but never lets go, and `release` is for teardown alone), orbit camera, third person, the act button, and Ooga Mine's camera-only flight (`fly`, `onFreeAction`); presets are looks (`look(place)`, `endLook`, `looking`): the camera goes to the place while the visitor keeps the Ooga, whose own camera (its view and carry or combat) is kept once as the first look begins, and any movement (keys, a stick, a pad's stick or A, `controls.moving`), camera input, X or Escape gives it all back and the movement goes on to the Ooga; `setActive(on, keepInput)` hands held movement on (a tour a movement ended); `possess` asks the scene's optional `mayPossess(cave)` first and toasts its refusal, keeping the Ooga already driven when the new one cannot be taken; a pilot made with `carry` (the hub, the arcade, the Factory, the hangar, ₿IFRÖST and DSB Land) keeps `BL.pilot.carried` current while it drives an Ooga (its view, combat, look as an angle from the Ooga's heading, pitch, zoom, the weapon in hand and its rounds), and its scene arrives with `arrive(cave)`, which possesses with that, puts the weapon and rounds back where the scene allows and has the next `navigate` keep the look, pitch and zoom from the heading the scene gives; a scripted crossing through a shield passes `framed` to `navigate` and keeps its own camera, the Sphere's doorway keeps its own exit view, and scenes that restrict the view or weapons carry nothing (#421); the visitor's last chosen view (carry or combat, the view, look, pitch, zoom, shoulder side and weapon in hand, never its rounds, nor a view a game's `combatOnly` forces) is written to localStorage `ooga-view` when the page hides or closes, and a page's first arrival before anything is carried comes back in it (the hub's first claim when it lands on its own view, which also stands the Ooga at the spot it last stood free in this browser, localStorage `ooga-spot` from the hub's `noteSpot`, or at random round the pile with none, never at its NPC); `debug` pages neither read nor write it; `view` names the view being kept, and `exitClose(shoulder)` leaves first person for the shoulder, or with `shoulder` false the orbit; `turnToward(x, y, z)` (page-level as `BL.pilot.turnToward`, the live pilot's) eases the view round to face a point at the view's own rate, tipping it in first person and turning the map from overhead, moving nothing (in first person and the aiming views the Ooga turns on the spot with the view); refused while something holds the camera or the Ooga lies down, and the visitor's own look input takes over at once; `viewState()` reads the view and mode of the Ooga driven now (view, combat, the look from its heading, pitch, zoom, overhead height, shoulder side) and `adopt(state)` gives them back to it later without letting go of it, blending from the view shown (first person keeps its look), as the Timechain doorway does; a seated Ooga's head follows the view only as far as the view is its own eyes (`seatedLook`, by `closeMix`), so seen from outside it sits looking straight out (a lying or armed seat aside); combat goes overhead to the bird's-eye view only where the scene offers it with its optional `birdsEye` (the ghost hunt's, for a hunter while a hunt runs; `clanker-play.js` takes the same for a gorilla) and does not keep it out with its optional `noOverhead` (Cloud Hopper's, while it runs); anywhere else X enters combat at the shoulder, leaving first person or scrolling out stays at the shoulder, a double right-click in combat does nothing, a carried or saved bird's-eye view arrives at the shoulder, and a view still overhead when the offer ends comes down to the shoulder; a scene's optional `primaryAction(down)` takes the left button's press in combat and the pad's RT first (true takes it, and its release then goes there too: Cloud Hopper's grappling hook) |
| `game.js` | loot tiers, deterministic loot, inventory, localStorage, `formatLarge` |
| `highscore-rules.js`, `highscores.js` | the page/Worker score registry and shared records: 26 boards, permanent machine IDs for duplicates, one D1 JSON dictionary per board. Public reads; only a run begun and finished in the same signed-in GitHub account submits. Cloud Hopper keeps the top 1,024 attributed times for each level on its own board (`cloud-hopper-l1` to `l4`, migration `0005_cloud_hopper_levels.sql`); its single board from before the levels is `retired`, read but never written. Drop, Orbit, Mine and the eight retro cabinets also show attributed top-ten boards backed by at most 1,024 finishes per board (`0008_cabinet_runs.sql`), preserving their existing machine records. The remaining games keep unattributed machine records (Rally separates track, mirror and lap/race). With `/api/me`'s `highscoreSnapshots` capability, the 17 Arcade machine boards share one `/api/highscores/arcade` snapshot: a public-only Cache API entry at the current Cloudflare data center lasts 30 seconds, while browser responses stay `no-store`; signed-in pages use the fresh `/api/highscores/arcade-live` route. Older Workers keep individual board reads. Guests refresh only while visible and watching a board, using the snapshot's remaining cache lifetime rather than stacking another TTL; hiding, leaving the last board or disposal cancels the timer, and failed reads retain the last good scores. Signed-in pages use the room's `highscore` pushes without score polling, discard stale per-game revisions and refresh watched boards on reconnect. Migration `0004_highscore_notifications.sql` records one pending revision per game in the score transaction; committed improvements notify the room through binding-only `refreshHighscores`, which reads D1 itself, and the existing room sweep recovers missed notifications. No guest socket or per-frame fetch; the client disposes with the director. Local bests stay local; old saves and restored Mine runs never upload. `docs/highscores.md` holds the API and rollout; Ghost Hunters, poker, DSB and the ticket wheel are excluded |
| `zone-map.js` | the zones signed-in players report, as the panels read them: `landOf` (a zone's land), `groupOf` and `heading` (the live players tab's groups: one a zone, every cave entrance the island's, DSB Land's venues apart, every game with Ooga Arcade's hall and only Ghost Hunter's Arena apart), `line` (the banner's line under its title by those same names, the main island keeping its own), `parentOf`, `routeFrom` and `next(from, to)` (zones are a tree rooted at `outside`; the first step toward another zone is down toward it or up toward the island, and a level or the land between starts as the land over it does), and `arrange(rows, mine)`, the tab's order. Pure, loaded in Node by the suite |
| `hud.js`, `fx.js` | the DOM panel (roster, meter, the Bananas panel's leaderboard and recent list (a row for every ranked donor, made as the board first reaches it, and twenty pooled recent rows, filled in place, every amount or only the message bar's by its checkbox, a banana before each amount while they count bananas, the recent rows' orange check centred on the name's line; the donors scroll inside a window five donor rows tall and the recent rows inside one at least as tall that takes the slab's spare room, while the slab scrolls above Feed the Oogas, which stays at the panel's foot), locker, toasts, tooltip; `toast` is the page's one, shared by every scene's HUD and the director; `holdRow` holds a game's Ooga list to one row and `leaveTo` labels a game's exits; wherever a place's banner has a Leave button, the wordless way out (`#leave-hud`, an arrow out of a bracket) stands left of the Ooga's face in the equipment row, as large as the face's button and named by the Leave button it stands in for, which is kept out of sight (`body[data-leave-hud]`); `showAccount` fills the account controls in Contributing Oogas; `voiceIcon` and `voiceWords` are the voice mark the live players tab shares; `showVoices` puts each signed-in player's host dot (centred behind the online dot) and voice mark, a click-to-mute button (the visitor's own mutes their microphone), on their Ooga's roster row; every roster name is a button opening a darker strip under its row with the contributor's stats (`openStats`: their Ooga's face, drawn once a page, and beside it their total across the five kinds and their first contribution from the character's `joined`, its year shortened to ’26 on every strip at once when the roster's longest total and longest date would not fit side by side there (`fitSince`), then across the strip's whole width, under the face too, the kinds and a chip linking each repository they contributed to (not the org's `.github`) with their count there, from the newest oogatron reading or the baked one, refilled while open as readings arrive), `showMic` keeps the microphone's note up while muted in voice, or alone in voice ("Alone · mic off"), and marks the rows of every signed-in player's own and driven Ooga online, whichever scene's roster is up; the roster stays ordered by its Sorted by dropdown over the list (latest contribution by default, or most contributions in the newest oogatron reading, this browser's choice in localStorage `ooga-roster-sort`), regardless of online presence); the particle pool, world speech, zzz marks, overlay drawing. All world speech goes through `fx.say(speaker, text, dur, hold)` and `fx.sayAt`: a line of any length (a `\n` starts a new bubble) is said a bubble at a time, each one or two rows (never more), shown whole (never typed out), with "..." (unless it already ends in punctuation) and a down arrow while another follows, going on after a readable pause or sooner on the place's `next`, a `hold` line keeping its last bubble up; `more`, `said` and `hush` let a place drive dialogue. A speaker's bubble holds still against small sway and follows only real movement. Never split, trim or fit a line to the bubble by hand. Arcade games use `sheet.setPlaying` to fold the shared panels temporarily while leaving tabs and local player-mute controls usable; ordinary panel choices return on exit. Floating scoreboards keep their separate gameplay hiding behavior. |
| `voice-blend.js` | shared page/Worker voice by level and crossing (#392): the main world (`WORLD`, every zone the island scene reports: `outside` with its cave entrances, `between` for its bridges and the air off its edge, `rainforest` and its cave `rainforest-under`, `sphere`, `bifrost`, Repo Island's `repos`, `hq` and its `hq-basement`, all shown to each other and in one set of coordinates, `world`, `sameFrame`) goes by height, not zone: all of it on or over the island's plane is one place, the surface, and each level below hangs from the one over it (`ABOVE`: `hq`, `hq-basement`, and `rainforest-under` under the rainforest's top), so a player there reports only the level it is heading down to and how far, wherever it stands (`crossable`; the hub's `levelOf` works it out). Elsewhere a crossing joins two places (`PAIRS`: Ooga Arcade into Ghost Hunter's Arena along the haunted hallway, each a whole group, `place`, #422, the arena's older names its `ALIASES`). The 20 steps the room and other pages get (`STEPS`), equal-power weights (`weight`, whose `at` is a place, as `near` and `far` name them), how loud a speaker plays to a listener (`gain`) and whether the room lets them hear each other (`shares`) |

A granted `cabinetAnchor` holds `setBody`, `setZone` and `setBlend` unchanged, and rewrites `sendPose` to the cabinet's hall position/heading with no jet or weapon, keeping proximity voice and visible presence there. The room is a Durable Object billed while awake: nothing there may run on a timer while idle (see `docs/auth-and-presence.md`). Its existing one-minute sweep also queries D1 for pending highscore revisions while players are connected, reads and broadcasts changed boards, and conditionally acknowledges only delivered revisions so a concurrent newer commit survives. never while in DSB Studio), `stop` on sign-out or `replaced` (only `leave`, the Leave voice button, forgets the visitor was in voice), a microphone whose track ends (`micLost`) and a restart whose calls fail coming back as a speaker on a bounded retry, `stats` (`enabled`, `muted`, `alone`, `speaking`, `joining`, `peers`, `hearing`, `error`, and `micWanted`, `publishing`, `ready`, `receiveState`, `micError` among the Studio panel's), `inspect` for debugging; a restart keeps the mute. Alone (#181): while the room's list is empty nobody can hear this page, so the mic track is held off as a mute holds it (`stats.alone`; still published so others can find it, the room not told, the visitor's own mute untouched) and comes back when the room lists someone. In DSB Studio an audience listens without publishing, so the room's `voice` message there also carries `listeners`, the other receive sessions, and that count holds the gate. An AudioContext made inside the Join click and closed in `stop` meters the mic and every received voice every 150 ms while voice is on (`stats.speaking`, `speaking(id)`); `muteLocal(id, on)`/`mutedLocally` and `setVolume(id, level)`/`volumeOf` save stable player IDs under the listener's account ID in localStorage (`oogaboogaland.voice-players.<listener-id>`), usable before voice joins; login callers resolve current room records and the old unscoped list is not imported. A voice link's partner plays at full whatever the crossing or the hearing range, and `inEarshot(id)` says whether a player can be heard where both stand. `setRadius` (#380) sets this visitor's hearing range (`stats.radius`, 0 for no limit, else 1–60 metres in 1-metre steps (`RADIUS_MIN`..`RADIUS_MAX`), kept in localStorage `oogaboogaland.voice-radius`): each received voice plays full inside the range and is silent at or beyond it (`falloff`, a hard cutoff), measured from `net.state.self` to the remote in the same place, a voice in another place of the group or not yet placed counting as past it and a page with no position of its own playing everyone; movement applies the cutoff on each existing 150 ms meter read, and `setRadius` applies it immediately without a fade or volume ramp (on iOS, cancelling the gain's scheduled ramp before setting its value), so it costs no new timer, and it changes only this page's playback volume, never the microphone, the room or what is pulled; where Web Audio is available (`GAIN_PLAY`), voices play through gains with peak compression above 100% and pass-through compression at normal levels, straight to the context output independently of environment mute; elements stay muted to keep streams flowing. Gains carry the receive level, crossing share, hard range cutoff, local volume and mute. Without Web Audio element playback offers quieting through 100%. Nodes are created when tracks land and disconnected when voices go (`plug`, `unplug`); there a context Safari suspends or interrupts marks the voices `blocked` and the next trusted `pointerup` or `keydown` resumes it (`wake`), and `fades` says whether a range can be heard at all.
