The hub's `claimOwnOoga` hands every visitor their own Ooga once a visit: a signed-in contributor theirs, a guest their local likeness (`preferredCharacter`), and again whenever the one driven stops being theirs; every other scene possesses the Ooga carried in, `character=`, or that same one, and leaves toward the island when an account change takes it away. Who may drive which Ooga is `net.mayDrive`, checked in `pilot.possess` through the scene's `mayPossess` (hub and lab); the room keeps its own copy of the ownership rules over the cast `build:site` writes to the gitignored `worker/src/characters.gen.json` (see `docs/auth-and-presence.md`) |
| `crew.js` | cavemen: work trips, weapons, pile reloading, sleep, strolls, possession, jetpack, swag, pets (at the heel, flying with a jetpack owner, or with `petRidesHat` riding the hat: `hatRide`, `poseHatRide`, shared with remote copies); a guest's local likeness (`contributors.addLocal`, marked `local`) is the visitor's `avatar`, never an NPC: `away` while nobody drives it, so it neither works, sleeps nor leaves a ghost; a seat for `sitPlayer` may take the act itself (`seat.act`, true when it did) before Space stands the Ooga up; `tint` colourways follow the day's price. A `puppet` Ooga (npc-sync.js on a following page) gets no AI or spacing in `update`; `ctx.onShot` reports every shot (the driven Ooga's also go to the room through `net.sendShot`, and its strikes through `net.sendSwing`), `showShot` replays one visually (no hits; a passive NPC's work rounds fly only as such a replay while it is a ghost track, in the scene's `shotLook`), `weaponFlags` packs a cave's weapon for the room as `jetFlags` does its jetpack, `setTint` sets a colourway; the online dot counts a local driver only while `net.state.me` is present. Other players (`outsideActors` with `player`) hold the driven Ooga off jumping and flying; on foot they give way instead: it walks on into their gap down to `PUSH_DEPTH` of it, each page moves its own Ooga out of any player's gap (`giveWay`, never off a drop) unless it is walking into them or the scene holds it in place (`holdsPlace`: the Timechain Sphere's keypad and crossing), and one that has not given way after `PUSH_PATIENCE` of pushing is walked through, so players never block one another. `outsideSupportAt` is their top as ground for it inside that same gap, which the hub adds to its support; standing there it rides their move through the scene's `carryCharacter`, as a passenger rides the crew |
| `npc-paths.js` | the surface path graph: 4096 fixed nodes in typed arrays with a binary heap, rebuilt when `path.version` changes. `fixed(island)` plans passive Hub NPC meadow work legs along the authored spokes and pile ring only, in the same walker state |
| `pile.js` | the banana pile: surface layer, then a layered shell over a mound; `MAX_BANANAS`; level on the shared `world` |
| `crates.js`, `critters.js`, `breakables.js`, `solid-props.js` | loot crates; instanced butterflies, fireflies and embers by phase and tier; capped destructible props with safe respawns; props whose render mesh is their collision shell (`segmentClear`, `supportAt`, `ceilingAt`; `prepare(geometry)` builds a mesh's shared collision tree ahead of its first `add`) |
| `agent.js` | the Agent, a voxel gorilla NPC: `create`, `createPlay` |
| `bca-models.js` | B.C.A.'s office behind the c10 veil, in the mouth's frame: two walnut desks, swivel chairs the gorillas turn, chunky monitors whose ledger faces (IN, OUT, TOTAL in tallies) are cached variants swapped by each desk's count, keyboards, abacuses whose beads slide under the hand (`stroke(progress, out)`, then `commit()`), ledger shelves, filing cabinets and the motto plaque. `office(mouth)` returns the nodes, solids and the world-space stations and wait spots the hub hands to `clankers.create` as `offices`, and `update` turns the chairs |
| `sleep-beam.js` | Pooled mirror-green glyph streams for the gorilla bed fallback: eight shared instanced batches, at most 20 bed slots, 90 ms upward departure and 90 ms downward arrival driven by `clankers.js`. Fits a short glyph cap to the posed body; the controller temporarily uses the existing green code form and restores it on completion/cancel. Reduced motion suppresses glyphs and color. No collision, network or camera ownership; `hide`, `clear`, `liveGeometry` and `dispose` follow the visit. |
| `npc-status-beams.js` | The Hub's passive NPC status relocations, presented on a 20-slot `sleep-beam.js` pool: it observes each roster Ooga's and companion's state after the scene applies it, and shows a 0.55 s departure at the old pose then an arrival at the new one. It never moves an actor, grants control or delays a state; controlled and remote bodies are skipped. `update`, `clear`, `liveGeometry` and `dispose` follow the visit. |
| `npc-ghosts.js` | The Hub's NPC ghost tracks (NPC ghost tracks in `docs/auth-and-presence.md`): while a signed-in player drives an Ooga or gorilla as a separate body (remote-players.js's `ghost` hook, the hub's `hideGorilla`), its roster NPC keeps its own track and is drawn in Cloud Hopper's replay look (`cloudHopperGhost.style`), its harmless work rounds too (crew `shotLook`). Marks only `ghostTrack`, which the tooltip's status reads as the NPC's own working, chilling or sleeping, never online (`hud.statusFor`; a gorilla's by its Ooga, `tooltipOwner`; the driver's live body keeps the online dot), and the crosshair as no target; hovered, a ghost Ooga or gorilla shows its name and that activity dot by the overlay's exact test (the hub's `showGhostTooltip`, rechecked each frame, a live body in front taking the pointer); never moves an actor, reserves or frees a seat or bed, or sets `humanControlled`/`remoteControlled`. The hub calls `restore` at the top of its update so every system reads the real models, and `paint(dt)` once poses are final; one twin per distinct source geometry, released a second after the last ghost and on `dispose`; at most 32 ghosts. Only a contributor's character is a ghost, its driver signed in or out: a guest's local likeness (`contributors.addLocal`: the default Ooga, or a remembered unbundled login's) is the visitor's own avatar (crew.js `avatar`), with no NPC track at all, so it never comes here. `set`, `shotLook`, `liveGeometry`, `stats` |
| `clankers.js`, `clanker-play.js` | the gorillas, one for each Ooga, and the visitor's control of one. In an office (`ctx.offices`, B.C.A.) a gorilla rises upright past the veil into a suit (`agent.suitClip`, three colourways hashed from its name), walks the office's authored points, claims one of two desks or a wait spot, sits, swivels and works in turn (type 5–9 s, look 1–2 s, abacus 3–5 s, spans hashed from its name), and lets go of its desk the moment anything takes it over. A gorilla whose Ooga sleeps (by `crew.stateOf`, its standing state, never the moment it stirs for a donation) goes to bed where the scene keeps beds (`ctx.sleep`: `slots`, `path(slot, out)`, `mark`): it frees its bench, leaves its room, reserves the bed dealt to it for the day by a hash of the current sleepers' names (`bedsToday`: occupied beds stay fixed across activity changes and midnight; only sleeping owners receive new reservations, while a driven gorilla keeps its existing bed until release), walks the path in phase `sleep` (`updateSleep`: go, settle, asleep), and walks home when its Ooga wakes (`updateWake`). Two short walking backsteps share two seconds without forward waypoint progress; five centimetres of new progress resets the timer; jumps, climbs and getting up pause that timer. Exhaustion enters beamOut then beamIn, retaining the bed and checking its landing pose before departure and again before transfer. A blocked landing waits and retries without flashing or moving a player. Possession, waking, remote control and visit cleanup cancel the beam. A hand on it wins. The bed is freed when waking starts. The 20 authored beds remain the limit: excess sleepers stay hidden until a bed becomes available; an Ooga that is away keeps its gorilla hidden. Ending an island event reconciles hidden gorillas immediately against their owners' current states. Afloat in the Mempool island's water (`motion.swim`, set by the hub) the rig dog-paddles (`SWIM` in `agent.js`). The gorilla the visitor drives meets other players as a driven Ooga does: on the island `clankerClear` holds it off them and `clankerSupportAt` adds their tops, both on its rig's posed envelope (the cylinder their pages see), its climbs ask the hub's `playersClear` (in `climbClear`, `climbWalkClear` and the opening path's `clankerOpeningClear`), and standing on one it rides their move (`rideClankerPlayer`); NPC gorillas still run under players |
| `cloud-hopper-replay-codec.js`, `cloud-hopper-replays.js` | Shared winning replay data and client I/O. The codec is also imported by the Worker: bounded versioned binary metadata, appearance geometry and Float32 poses, with finite-value, identity, level, course-version, duration and sample checks. The client requests session-bound tickets, submits eligible recordings, verifies download hashes and lazily caches at most four immutable recordings. Its controller freezes only the active attempt's ghost pointer; numeric boards always reach the timer/result HUD, and ending a run releases the pointer for fresh results. Replay defaults off, missing/offline data never falls back to a browser personal ghost, and stale async downloads cannot resurrect a departed level. No per-frame network work. Client poses are not authoritative physics proof; `docs/cloud-hopper-replays.md` specifies publication, recovery, retention and rollout |
| `cloud-hopper-course.js`, `cloud-hopper-levels.js`, `cloud-hopper-models.js`, `cloud-hopper-art.js`, `cloud-hopper-hud.js`, `cloud-hopper-island.js`, `cloud-hopper-ghost.js`, `cloud-hopper-music.js`, `cloud-hopper-audio.js`, `cloud-hopper-sky.js`, `cloud-hopper-tornado.js`, `cloud-hopper-gust.js`, `cloud-hopper.js` | Cloud Hopper uses the hub's cached white voxel clouds and Ooga Arcade's carved cabinet builder, marquee and animated pixel screens. A solo randomized descent at one of four levels (`cloud-hopper-levels.js`: plain data, loadable in Node). A level's route R runs from the launch cloud to the final cloud 12 m off the coast: Level 1's is 340.1 m, 21% under the old 430.5 m course, and each level's 42% longer than the last (482.9, 685.8 and 973.8 m). It starts R out along the Arcade mouth's heading and `slope` × R above the final cloud; its `layout` and `timing` override the course's `LAYOUT` and `TIMING`, whose defaults are the old course's shape and Level 2's timing. A run flies under its level's own fixed sky (its `sky`: an hour sampled on the equinox, `cloudHopperSky.DAY`, and how much painted cloud), which the hub samples once in place of its clock while the run plays, its lamps lit by the sky shown and its phase toasts still by the clock: Floating Isles at 08:15 among voxel isles with flowers, palms, trees and a hut, strata, drips and vines under them, waterfalls of the Mempool island's moving lake water (its `WATER` blue in seven strands of `face.lake` with their own `lakeFlow`) into mist, smaller isles on rope bridges, two drifting Ooga balloons, wheeling gulls and a double rainbow centred over the island as seen from the course, a bright one nearer the island (`INNER_RAINBOW`) inside a greater, fainter one beyond it with its colours turned round (`RAINBOW`); Amber Spires at 17:27 with towering cumulus coloured for the low sun, every one at one scale (`SPIRE_SCALE`, so their blocks match and the near ones tower over the course): ten far round the island, four of which grow, and six young ones nearer off to either side of the course (`YOUNG`; on Canvas 2D the young ones are left out and the far ones stand grown), the growing ones starting at the Play press, one after another, in about 17 s each: billow after billow puffs up out of the one below and flows up the column, then the top spreads out over it (`spirePieces`: each billow and the top a mesh of its own, uploaded as the run is built and eased into place), banks along the horizon, rising sky lanterns and geese in a V; and Aurora Heights at 00:30 under the moon with an aurora as a real one stands and moves, drawn in pixel art: eight long curtains (`CURTAINS`): toward the island two far ones whose lower edges stand over the sea rather than the eye (`sea`), so from high on the course they hang low over the sea behind the island and rise into the sky as a rider comes down to it, a long low arc and a shorter one off to one side; three high overhead off to either side, their rays drawing together toward the top of the sky; and two behind, toward the start; each kept where it stands round the eye (the far ones' heights over the sea aside), as something a hundred kilometres up stays put however far a rider falls, its lower edge never under the sea's height, straight but for its folds so perspective bends it into an arc settling toward the horizon at its ends; each pixel columns standing edge to edge on its lower edge, coloured as a real aurora is, brightest and sharpest at that lower edge in green, its rays rising from it through teal into faint violet and red, each column its own height so the rays are streaked and their tips step as staircases, with a skirt under the edge of a thin pink fringe and a soft green glow on the sky (`ray`, `skirt`, one batch each); and moving as one does, never twisting about: its folds drift along it and change over a minute or two, its lower edge rises and dips a little, bright stretches and faint ones come and go along it, its rays shimmer as pleats of bright and dim rays run along it a few degrees a second, a wave of brightness runs along it every 16–24 s, and every two minutes or so (40 s into the run first) it breaks up (`BREAKUP`), brightening fast with its folds and rays quickening and its pink fringe glowing, then easing back over twenty seconds; the tiers draw fewer columns, Canvas 2D the two farthest toward the island coarser and without their skirts, shooting stars, dark isles with glowing moss, crystal columns and vines dressed with crystal clusters, glowing mushrooms and rune stones, crystals hanging from their keels, a fall of light off every third one's rim and fireflies drifting round them, and great faceted crystal clusters on rock turning in the air with a ring of shards orbiting each and a glow turned to the eye, and its clouds, the launch cloud's too, given a night fill (`cloudMesh`'s `dark`), and two gusts of wind a run (its layout's `gusts`, `cloud-hopper-gust.js`), each coming on at a seeded share of the way down (one in each half) from a seeded side straight across the course: it warns for 2.5 s (a band down the edge of the screen it comes from with chevrons pointing the way it blows and GUST, `#cloud-hopper-gust` from the HUD's `setGust`, by the view's right; a rising whoosh, `cloudHopperAudio.gustWarn`; a few faint streaks drifting across), the wind already rising through it to 30% of its push (`LEAD`), then blows for 3 s, easing up to the whole of it and out (pale glowing streaks racing past in a box ahead of the eye, one instanced batch written in place, and its wind's rush and whistle, `cloudHopperAudio.gust`), pushing an airborne Ooga across at up to `GUST_PUSH` (22 m/s², a drift of about 15 m/s against the air's drag, more than steering makes up) and sliding one planted on a cloud more gently, easing toward `GUST_SLIDE` (6 m/s), so one that just stands there is carried off the edge and leaves it at the slide's speed while one walking against it holds; and Thunderhead at 18:36 in a storm (its sky's `storm` greys the sample once, `cloudHopperSky.grade`, before the hub's stash keeps it; its `fog` [40, 160] hazes the clouds and, through `seaFog`, the sea into the haze's own grey, so the course beyond and the island stay hidden; its clouds take the night fill too) with rain round the eye (`weather.rainDrop`, 360, 240 or 120 thin streaks by tier, none on Canvas 2D, none within 3 m of the eye) and forked lightning 60 to 110 m off every 4 to 10 s whose flash the hub adds to the sky and haze for its frames (`cloudHopperSky.flash`, from the controller's `flash`) and whose thunder arrives at the speed of sound (`onStrike`), and storm-dark isles (`isle` variants 8–11) well out to either side of the course at about its height, each with a lighthouse banded white and Bitcoin orange (`lighthouse`) and a keeper's hut, sweeping two beams round (`beam`), landmarks far off rather than anywhere to land; the haze takes them as it takes everything, and past its start a lighthouse is only its light, a soft warm glow over the lamp (`hazeLight`, a blended picture the sky module fades itself, falling to nothing well inside its square) that rises as the haze takes the tower, flares as a beam sweeps toward the eye and dies away by 420 m (`GONE`). Thunderhead also brings tornadoes (its sky's `tornado`, `cloud-hopper-tornado.js`): each run has three spots down the course, seeded with it (`SPOTS`: 8 to 36 m off to one side of the main landings a run at the expected pace reaches somewhere within 10% of 22%, 50% and 78% of the way down, `SPREAD`), and a tornado for each, any of them up at once (the controller's `tornadoes`, each `start`ed with its own spot, `only`, drawing its own copies of the slabs and spray, `which`, since the renderer batches instances by geometry and tornadoes sharing one would draw only one of them, and writing its own whirl: the one that has the Ooga, else the nearest down, else the nearest brewing, is the `tornado` that matters now, whose whirl the course takes and which whirls the Ooga, draws its outline and roars). From the run's start the storm turns over every spot, each tornado's white cap gathering, glowing and spinning over the clouds there (4.5 s; its top `TOP`, 75 m over that landing's top, low enough that a rider coming down sees it ahead, a few degrees under the eye as it comes out of the haze), lightning striking round it every 1 to 2 s with its flash and thunder, and brews there, so a rider sees ahead where each will come down (tornado and lightning both piercing most of the haze, `fogPierce`, but a tornado only near the eye: fully within 200 m, easing off by 260 m, past which the haze hides it as it does a cloud and it is not drawn, its lightning neither, `SEEN`, so those further down the course come out of the storm only as the rider nears them); a spot within 180 m of the rider, or behind them, moves on down to the main landing that far ahead, none in the last tenth of the way (`AHEAD`, `LAST`). Once its cap has gathered it waits until the rider comes within 220 m of it across the course (`TRIGGER`) or passes it; then, with a gathering whoosh, the funnel lowers slowly from it through the course (20 s, its tip reaching the course about halfway), a thin rope under the cap, 30% of its full girth and 35% of its full hold as its tip reaches the course (`THIN`, `WEAK`), growing to both as its tip goes on down to the bottom (the cap keeps its size and the funnel blends from it, `TAPER`), roaming meanwhile, and roams about the course there until the rider is 50 m nearer the island than it (`GONE`; its heading wandering at about 4 m/s, pulled back toward the landing it brewed over, its tail curling back from where it heads), its lightning striking round it until it ropes out and draws up (3 s), so a run at any pace meets all three fully down, two at once now and then. It is a giant voxel tornado, 122 m tall: 66 stepped slabs (one instanced batch for each of six bands of colour, white at its flared 19 m cap through blue to deep blue at its tail, which curls back from its drift), each turning and swaying at its own speed, with streaks of spray whipped round it; a view that comes in at its side cuts away the slabs round its own height, and its roar rises with how near and strong it is (`cloudHopperAudio.tornado`). While it is down it writes the course's `whirl`: clouds within 22 m of its side (`REACH`) turn round it as a vortex does (10 m/s at its side, slower further out), are drawn in toward it at up to 3 m/s and lifted at up to 2.5 m/s (each cloud's `drawn` and `lift`, at most 30 m up), each keeping its own angle, blended from its lane by the tornado's strength, so they ease back out and down into their lanes as it dies or moves off, and a rider planted on one is carried round and up. An Ooga within 18 m of its side is taken (the controller's `whirlOoga`, `WHIRL_*`): lifted off whatever it stands on, drawn in toward it (up to 7 m/s), carried round it (up to 12 m/s) and lifted (up to 5 m/s), harder the nearer it comes and all of it easing as the tornado dies, its grip easing the Ooga's flight toward the whirl's over time (`WHIRL_GRIP`, 10 a second at its side at full strength, the air's own drag easing it there too and the grip aimed past the whirl's to make up for it) rather than taking it over at once, so speed brought in, a boost cloud's, carries a rider on through a young one, until it does or the Ooga steers out past 22 m, keeping its speed when let go and an air jump; a hook biting a cloud holds it out. Whenever the funnel holds the Ooga or stands between it and the view, the hub draws the Ooga's outline through it (`hiddenByTornado`, the hidden-walker outline of `camera-cover.js` with a dark key so it reads on the white). A run is never lost while it is whirled, the pull growing a little inside the wall, unless the Ooga is wholly inside the funnel (its body's radius inside the funnel's at its feet) 0.15 s on end (`SWEPT_DWELL`, `sweep`), so a quick pass through it goes on: a young one, its tip just through the course, sweeps most of those run through it but lets go one boosted from a pink cloud just short of it, half grown about half even of those, and once grown nearly every pass. Then the controls let go and the Ooga is drawn in to the axis from where it was caught (`SWEPT_DRAW`, 0.5 s) as it is whirled up the core inside the funnel (`SWEPT_CLIMB`, 2.4 s) while the view draws back 12 m outside the funnel's side and rises after it, 6 m under it and never nearer the top than 4 m under it (`SWEPT_OUT`, `SWEPT_BEHIND`, `SWEPT_UNDER`), turning up to follow it but never so far that the frame's top edge looks over the funnel's near rim, so it sees tornado and never past its top; near the top it widens into a ring 6 m round the axis 10 m under the top, inside the funnel and hidden but for its outline, circling, spinning and tumbling there (`SWEPT_SINK`, `SWEPT_RING`, `SWEPT_SPIN`) behind the DQ card until a replay or exit, the tornado held down meanwhile (`hold`) and the view tilting up behind the card so the Ooga circles at 75% of the screen's height; 1.2 s after it gets there (`SWEPT_HOLD`) the run ends in a DQ of its own, without the fall into the water (`setDq("tornado")`: its own heading and line, and its own picture, `cloudHopperArt.swept`, shown once it is supplied). `cloud-hopper-sky.js` lays that scenery for each run from a fixed seed a level, decoration alone (`sightHidden`, no collision, each piece kept off every cloud's swept lane), its moving parts instanced batches written in place (an instance's glow, its 17th float, set to 1 so emissive faces light) and a few node transforms, nothing allocated a frame; Canvas 2D gets a thinner aurora without the flocks, lanterns and shooting stars. The camera's far plane widens to take it in. The controller widens the camera's far plane to keep the island in view from far starts and restores it on exit, and its world bound follows the start. Its front-left Ooga Arcade cabinet zooms to the glass and shows level selection inside it. Play closes the card and rides the visible Ooga up from beside the island while a uniform cabinet zoom reaches fullscreen. Results and level selection stay immersed; only explicit exit reverses the zoom to the hall without reshaping the card or game image. The exterior dock and entry trigger are removed; the controller's floating cabinet stays hidden throughout Arcade mode. Weapons are hidden before selection and play, with the prior loadout restored on cancellation or exit. The arcade opens a title card with a picture card a level (`BL.cloudHopperArt.levels`, from `src/art/cloud-hopper-level-*.webp`): the level's sky over the cloud a run starts from, with the driven Ooga painted standing on it once the player has finished that level (painted once per Ooga, `hud.renderBodyIcon`), the level's number in the picture's corner, its name alone on a row, its route and the player's best, or a lock and what unlocks it; the card is up to 1120 px wide. A tap or the arrows choose, Play (or a fresh Space or Enter on a level card, which plays that card's level) begins the lift for the chosen open level, and Not now (or Escape) leaves the player in place; the two share one row, on a phone too. Under the cards the chosen level's shared board shows its ten places in two columns of five, with empty/loading/unavailable/stale states and no local-score fallback. Browser personal bests are labeled separately. A visit starts on the level played last, else the highest open level the player has not finished. Only Level 1 starts open: a clean finish of the highest open level opens the next, signed in or out, online or not, and every clean finish records the player's best for its level in the save (`game.state.cloudHopper`: `unlocked` for the browser, `players` the eight most recent logins' bests per level, "" signed out; `recordCloudHopper`, `cloudHopperBest`). At the starting cloud, the first play in each browser session gets four illustrated guide cards for ordinary clouds, pink boost clouds, jetpacks and the banana-pile finish, followed by a card for each thing the run carries that the session has not met: purple bounce clouds, which come in at Level 2, Aurora Heights' gusts (wind curling over a cloud, `gustStrokes`), Thunderhead's tornadoes (its funnel alone, `cloudHopperTornado.still`, its slabs stood as nodes for the icon renderer) and each power-up the level carries (sessionStorage `ooga-cloud-hopper-items-v1`; a Next level that brings a new one shows its card at the top first), every card of a guide held at its tallest card's size, measured as it opens and on a resize (`fitGuide`), so its buttons never move. The briefing holds player input and the race clock at zero while keeping the full-volume drumless score uninterrupted. Finishing the guide or choosing Skip tips stores `ooga-cloud-hopper-guide-v1` in sessionStorage, with a page-session fallback when storage is unavailable; later plays and reloads within that session skip it, and a new browser session shows it again. The old localStorage completion marker is ignored. Cancelling during the guide returns to the Arcade cabinet without marking it seen. The controller clears held input before releasing the player and stages the ghost only after the guide closes. Leave the launch cloud to start the clock; reach the banana pile to finish. As the run starts the help/key lines fold up into the clock, shrinking and fading over 0.7 s after a beat (`cloud-hopper-running`; none under reduced motion); the selected level's global record row stays visible, with its opt-in ghost button immediately left of the time and numeric updates independent of the active ghost. Hidden tabs pause the race clock, clouds and ghost with player physics while the soundtrack continues. A gold BANANA PILE / TOUCH TO FINISH label, with the live distance to the pile in metres centred over it, and a downward chevron track the live pile top during lift, ready and running, staying readable from the starting cloud and disappearing on results or exit. Donation thank-you tickers are discarded while Cloud Hopper is active; the optional `fx` ticker visibility predicate also clears a notice already on screen. Its low stone rim uses ordinary step assistance, so players can walk or run onto it in both the hub and Cloud Hopper without jumping. Early DQ requires 0.75 continuous seconds of falling with no conservatively reachable cloud, launch platform, island coast or falling pickup. Becoming recoverable, rising or landing resets that grace period; controls and the race clock stay live meanwhile. Touching water still disqualifies immediately. The reach envelope uses each cloud’s own height and full sinking/drift range, remaining air jump, sprint speed and a finite jetpack impulse budget from the crew’s actual acceleration, rise cap, fuel and tank size; having fuel never skips the reach check. Jet-first recovery spends fuel and loses height while arresting the current downward speed before crediting any remaining lift. A separate jump-first option consumes the one remaining air jump, so it cannot cancel the fall and be counted again after the fuel. The budget allows pulsed thrust and delayed air jumps, but cannot rescue a trajectory after water contact. Jetpack pickups only extend recovery when their release/fall trajectory can intersect the player’s reachable height before water, accounting for horizontal travel, remaining jump and fuel; a nearby pickup stranded overhead cannot postpone DQ. Results stop the timer, input and jet flame while a fixed-position camera tracks the Ooga’s continued ballistic fall through the water; the body disappears after full submersion and replay/exit restores its visibility. Result cards replace their status headings with matching still Ooga illustrations: underwater for DQ (a tornado's DQ has its own, `swept`, still to be supplied; until it is, that card shows none), resting on the banana pile for completion, and a golden-banana trophy for a new session best (`src/art/cloud-hopper-*.webp`, embedded by `cloud-hopper-art.js` for the portable page and written back out as files under `art/` for hosted pages by `--image-assets`, so they load only when a card shows them and a failed one hides), with descriptive alt text. The first clean finish establishes a best; only a strictly faster later run gets the best-time image, while ties use the completion image. The DQ card opens immediately below the top bar and above the lower third of the screen, up to 760 px wide with its three buttons in one row and a transparent backdrop, its board fitting without scrolling (a window up to 720 px tall leaves it its level's name for a heading and a smaller picture; only a phone on its side scrolls it); the held camera frames the falling torso at 75% screen height in either projection. Replay/exit restores the retained up vector and orthographic scale and rebases the pilot view before relocating. `pilot.syncHeadVisibility` keeps the actor’s head visible as it falls away from that fixed camera. From 29 main clouds and 16 side landings (Level 1) to 78 and 43 (Level 4), at most 128 in all, form a scattered field with frequent random turns, all entirely offshore; the last cloud is 12 m beyond the island radius. Main forward gaps target 11.5 m, with jitter bounded by the widest rotated cloud. Side landings occupy distinct randomized segments throughout the full descent, alternating left and right. Placement reserves at least 1.5 m between complete swept rectangular footprints, including both clouds’ full lateral travel; optional landings move outward when needed. A radial footprint bound keeps all puffs offshore without pushing the final clouds back together. Ordinary walk-offs fall without a ledge boost (`autoLedgeJump`); Space jumps. Seeded special clouds add pink boost clouds whose white chevrons scroll across their whole upper surface, and, from Level 2, purple bounce clouds using the same voxel shapes. Every course of a level carries the same number of each (Levels 1 to 4: 8, 13, 15 and 25 pink and 0, 10, 15 and 18 purple among 45, 67, 88 and 121 clouds), its `boost` and `bounce` shares of all but seven of its clouds (`SPECIAL_SPARE`, the most its start, finish and jetpacks ever keep) dealt at random among the eligible ones, no two bounces nearer in height than a hop and a half while the course allows; a special turned white for want of a fair place to fade is dealt again to an ordinary white cloud, whose clock already keeps every check a special needs. Every course cloud has an independent seeded orientation; landing contact, rider carry and offshore clearance follow the rotated shapes. The boost pattern follows the cloud's silhouette and tiers while pointing toward the mainland, independently of its long axis. Touching any part of a boost cloud launches toward the mainland with 28 m/s initial horizontal speed and a small 4 m/s lift. Touching a purple cloud launches at 12 m/s straight up, including with a worn jetpack. Just above it, five small translucent spring and upward pixel-arrow animations pulse gently with subdued glow and staggered phases: one at the centre and four spread across inset positions scaled to the cloud’s dimensions. They follow the cloud’s orientation, drift, sinking and opacity. These decorative markers have no collision and share three cached geometries included in the course’s geometry lifetime. Full-body swept contact includes the sides and underside, rising or stationary contact, and fast crossings; each cloud rearms only after separation. The contact bounds turn with the cloud, shrink with its sinking top, and never sweep through a hidden lane reset. Recovery includes reaching a special cloud with the head or body. Both leave one air jump, preserve ordinary steering, follow cloud fading/sinking, and are generated from that attempt’s course seed. The first two and final landings and jetpack clouds stay white. The optional crew `onJump` hook sounds successful explicit jumps; Cloud Hopper also sounds its automatic launches. `cloud-hopper-audio.js` opens one gesture-activated context with pooled jump and cloud voices, pooled tone and noise voices for the power-ups' cues (a pickup's chime, X-ray's rising and falling shimmer, the feather's whoosh, the hook's throw, bite, let-go and loss), two thunder voices (a crack and a rumble delayed by the strike's distance) and the storm's rain bed, and a retained jet loop made of the noise alone, no tones, so it sounds like a jetpack rather than a motor: a deep, steady rushing roar breathing gently with a slow drift of the same noise, and a thin hiss over it (`JET`, `JET_ROAR`, `JET_HISS`). The quiet jet whooshes open with thrust and closes as it fades on release; mute, results and exit silence it immediately. Cloud fade onsets use three-dimensional rider distance, full within 4 m and silent at 50 m. Effects share the environment mute, stop on hidden pages or results, reset between runs and release their context/listeners on disposal. `cloud-hopper-music.js` supplies one original score a level (`SONGS`), each 32 bars on the same plan (a soft lead with full-bar rests, slowly changing chords, offbeat answering notes and a bass, with tempo-synced stereo echoes) in its own style for its sky: Coconut Breeze (Level 1, 112 BPM tropical pluck-pop in G major), Golden Hour Groove (Level 2, 118 BPM sunset disco-synthwave in D major), Cloudbreak Sprint (Level 3, 128 BPM progressive trance in B minor, the original score unchanged) and Thunderhead Run (Level 4, 122 BPM dark deep house in D minor: a round kick on every beat, the bass and sub on the offbeats, open hats between, syncopated minor stabs and a haunting pluck through deep echoes). Their fixed voices sit inside that same context and noise loop, with no high arpeggio runs or repeating noise risers. `song(index)` picks the level's: up to three players share the output: the playing song fades out over 2.4 s from its next beat and the new one starts only 85% of the way through that fade, with the old one down to about a quarter, coming in over 1.6 s (`FADE_OUT_S`, `ENTER`, `FADE_IN_S`), so choosing another level card fades one song away before the next arrives; a song switched to that has not come in yet is swapped for the newer choice in its place. The picker plays the level chosen, and a run its own level; Arcade mode skips idle dock music. The chosen song's melody, chords and bass play continuously through lift, briefing, ready, race, results and Play Again; phase changes preserve the song position, held notes and echoes. A separate drum gate enters at full level on the next bar’s first beat when the race starts; after finish, session-best or DQ it lets the current bar finish and closes before the following downbeat. Pending transitions can be replaced by a quick replay without restarting the song, and queued kick ducking follows the same gate. Gameplay effects stop at results. The controller selects the score from its phase and ticks it before result/falling early returns. Scheduling uses audio time with bounded lookahead and skips missed notes; mute and suspended contexts hush it while retaining the song clock for return. Hidden tabs keep the score running through a 250 ms audio-only timer with a two-second lookahead, primed on hiding and stopped on return, mute, exit, disposal or context suspension; gameplay and rendering stay paused. The opening panel starts at 28% music volume, reaching full volume when play reaches the starting cloud. Every arcade start and replay uses the eased six-second climb and gradual volume rise. The ride frames the visible Ooga and cloud, then restores the chosen first-person or shoulder play view at the top. Replay retains the transport. Exit stops the score before returning to the Arcade; hub departure also stops scheduling, and disposal closes the shared context. Echo taps have no feedback and flush while inaudible. No music fetches. Each cloud swings smoothly across its own 10–16 m lateral lane on a seeded speed and phase, turning round at the ends and never jumping, so a planted rider is carried and a body sweep holds over a frame of any length. During a run the swing is a function of race time alone (a tornado's whirl aside); waiting before departure only wobbles each swing's phase, which the run's first 1.5 s eases away, so waiting can never move a cloud out of its lane. Falling jetpacks retain their exact first-disappearance position. Offshore clearance and recovery use the full lane range. Disappearances start only once the race clock does, and each is a closed-form function of race time (`cloud-hopper-course.js` `TIMING`, `docs/cloud-hopper-levels-plan.md` 2.4–2.8). Every cloud is permanent (never fades) or ordinary (one disappearance per seeded slot); the first two and last two landings are permanent. A level's timing sets its blend (`permanent`: 80%, 70%, 60% and 50% of its white clouds permanent, Levels 1 to 4, while every boost and bounce cloud is ordinary, since never fading is for white clouds alone), its ordinary clouds' slots (`ordinary`, log-uniform, so an ordinary cloud stands solid a median 22.5, 20, 17.5 and 15 s between disappearances), each warning and sinking fade (`warn` and `fade`: 3 s and 1 s, 2.5 and 0.875, 2 and 0.75, 1.5 and 0.625) and how long a gone cloud stays gone (`hidden`: 1.5–3, 2–3.5, 2.5–4 and 3–4.5 s). Every loss of support follows its warning at full support (`phase` WARNING, `warning` 0–1), which the controller shows as a dithered thinning pulse quickening from 2 to 6 a second (`thinning`) with a soft rising whistle (`cloudHopperAudio.warn`, three pooled voices, heard within 40 m), and then the sinking fade; warning and fade together outlast a jump. Built bottom-up, every cloud starts permanent and a white one, while the white clouds made ordinary so far fall short of the level's share of them (a seeded chance either way), is made ordinary if one of `PLACES` (21) places along its slot keeps its every warning fair (whenever it starts warning while a run may be on it, a cloud it reaches then is steady), its linked ordinary clouds' first two warnings 3 s apart and, for a main landing, a permanent cloud or the coast within its level's `spine` hops; otherwise it stays permanent and the next ones make up for it. Every boost and bounce cloud is made ordinary at the first of those places that keeps its linked ordinary clouds' first two warnings apart, since a touch launches a rider off it and none stands on it as it warns; one no place keeps so (about one in a hundred on Thunderhead, almost none elsewhere) turns white and stays permanent. None warns before 4 s. Level 4's fairness counts on 80% of a running jump rather than 70% (`reach`), or too few of its clouds could fade fairly. `solidLeft` reads the schedule. Three power-ups ride the clouds as pickups beside the jetpacks (plan 3; `course.pickups`' `kind`), placed from their own seeded stream after everything else, so no landing, special, pickup or clock moves: a level's layout sets how many (`xray`, `updraft`, `hook`: Amber Spires 2 X-ray; Aurora Heights 2 X-ray, 2 Updraft; Thunderhead 3 X-ray, 1 Updraft, 2 Hook), each kind spread over its own stretch of the main landings on plain clouds no pickup holds. They fall with their cloud as a jetpack does, turn in their own tinted glow (`cloudHopperModels.pickup(kind)`: carved stone goggles (the chosen C: chunky stone rims notched with glowing runes round dark lenses with glowing scanning rings, and a leather strap behind), a sky feather, a grappling hook on coils of vine rope), which fades into a level's haze with distance as the body does (`updateJetpackPickup`'s `haze`, since a blended picture takes no fog of its own), and the hub lights the two nearest to the view. `pickupAt(…, wants)` takes only what the rider may: a jetpack and X-ray always (another refreshes either to full, and one touched on a full tank is gone all the same), a feather or a hook only with none held. X-ray goggles, worn meanwhile over the Ooga's eyes (`cloudHopperModels.wornGoggles` on its face anchor, the lenses just in front of its face and the strap round the back of its head, one geometry for each size of head), show, for 30 s, the nearest clouds' solid time read from the schedule itself (`solidLeft`): at most 8 labels (5 on a coarse pointer or a narrow screen) within 60 m (120 in haze), a ring emptying over 20 s with whole seconds in it, red and pulsing while it warns, a gold ∞ diamond on a permanent cloud, a dashed ring and the seconds until a gone one is back, a dashed outline in haze past the fog's start, overlapping labels left out, a thin cyan screen edge, and a falling shimmer 2 s before the end; it allocates nothing a frame (labels made once, fixed insertion buffers). A sky feather held turns a Space press with both jumps spent and no worn pack into a gust (the crew's `spentJump` hook): 13 m/s straight up with the horizontal speed kept, a spiral of leaves and a whoosh, and `canRecover` counts it with the air jump as one reset as tall as both. A grappling hook held is thrown by the hook's button (`hookAction`: the left button in combat through the pilot's `primaryAction`, a pad's RT, or holding the hook's button in the banner on a touch screen) along the crosshair's ray from the Ooga's chest: the first cloud it meets within 40 m (`course.hit`, which a gold ring marks under the crosshair while the hook is in hand) is bitten where it was hit and followed as the cloud drifts and sinks, and a miss flies the hook out to its full reach and loses it. While the button stays down a bitten hook pulls the Ooga at up to 18 m/s toward a point just over the cloud's top at the bite, lifting it off whatever it stands on and leaving it one air jump; let go (or arrived within a metre, after 4 s, or with the cloud gone), the hook lets go and reels back to the hand while the Ooga flies on with its speed, the crew's air drag easing it. A hook held saves a fall while any cloud stands in its reach (`hookReach`), since its pull outruns gravity. Buttons in the banner's equipment row beside the jetpack's say them, each its pixel icon, a readout and its own edge colour: X-ray's goggles with its seconds (blinking red its last two), a feather held and a hook held or thrown (`setPowers`). The run is played in combat alone: the hub's `combatOnly` holds the pilot there from the ride up to the result card, so X and the portrait never switch to carry, and `noOverhead` keeps it off the bird's-eye view; an Ooga that came in carrying goes back to carrying when it leaves. The first jetpack is collected unequipped and toggled with J. Extra pickups refill an owned pack to full while preserving whether it is worn or stowed; a full tank takes the pickup too, and it is gone. Pickup refills happen before empty-pack removal and the recovery check. Fuel never regenerates, and an empty pack disappears. Floating pickups use a cached copy of the jetpack with a modest material fill so their full silhouette and colours stay readable at night; worn packs keep their original material. Their soft aura sits entirely behind the rotating model bounds instead of intersecting the tanks, with a restrained pool and neutral uplight below. In this game alone they provide vertical lift only while Space is held: WASD and Shift retain ordinary air control and sprint speed, lateral movement burns no fuel, and releasing thrust restores the normal falling pose and gravity with no sideways ceiling glide. The main island keeps its outdoor objects while NPCs, other islands and interiors are hidden and openings are sealed with matching solid stone. Cloud Hopper keeps the chosen camera distance and angle relative to the Ooga without follow lag or terrain correction: slopes, cliffs, trees, props, other Oogas and the ghost cannot shorten its boom or supply camera aim/zoom targets or ceilings. Manual view controls still change the chosen framing; the DQ water-fall sequence alone holds the camera independently of the Ooga. The island stays clear from the starting height with fog beyond the course. The floating cabinet stays hidden, and the starting cloud stays at the top when the run ends; exiting hides the launch cloud, restores equipment, parks the Ooga safely at the Arcade mouth, and returns it to the cabinet in the Arcade scene. The result card shows the selected level's shared top ten in two columns of five, with empty places and explicit loading/unavailable/stale states. Accepted finishes refresh that numeric board immediately; browser personal bests remain separately labeled. A clean finish offers Next level (when it is open), Replay, Level select (the level cards in the card, the line under its title saying what the race is in place of the played level's name, as the intro's does, with the chosen level's board under them, as on the opening menu: the shared board following the card chosen, with the browser personal best labeled separately; with Play level N and Back, which puts the played level back, the card free to use the window's height) and Return to arcade, and says what it earned (a session or personal best, the next level); a DQ offers Play again, Level select and Return to arcade. Panel buttons are coloured by what they do (their `data-role`): banana gold goes on (Play, Next, Next level, Replay, Play again), leaf green chooses (Level select), light gray steps back (Back) and stone leaves (Not now, Skip tips, Return to arcade). Panel buttons require a fresh unmodified Enter or Space press: held sprint/jump keys, repeats and orphan Space releases cannot trigger Play again, while pointer clicks and Tab navigation remain available. Eligible signed-in runs capture actual body, limb and jetpack poses at 20 Hz in a bounded 12,001-sample typed buffer, thinning in place for long runs, plus an immutable appearance snapshot. The global winner for each level supplies the sole playback source, scoped by course version; no personal or guest recording is retained as a fallback. The timer's small ghost control defaults off, enables only when a compatible recording is available, downloads lazily and highlights while enabled. The active attempt keeps its selected ghost while numeric scores remain live; results stop playback, release the selection and show any newly accepted record. The nameplate uses the board's current attribution for the recorded winner. Before departure it stands on the course-facing rim, looking outward, using the appearance root's model-local ground offset plus the current cloud height exactly once; its world-space motion samples are unchanged and its root blends into the recorded route over the first quarter-second without shifting the stored samples or race clock. Every new play, including retries and returning from the Arcade, draws a fresh course seed; the session remembers the previous seed to prevent an immediate repeat. The translucent, non-interactive ghost keeps the winning run’s original route, so its old landing spots may differ from the new clouds; the HUD labels that original route. Pre-start cloud drift continues from the current attempt’s own departure phase instead of synchronizing to the ghost. Ghost, course and race timer share elapsed time. D1 publishes the authoritative winner's immutable replay pointer; earlier winners keep ties and equal-time attempts cannot replace an existing recording. The ghost owns its tinted geometry copies, participates in liveGeometry and releases them on exit. Arcade replays return to the launch point for another ascent and preserve the original equipment for the eventual exit. During a cabinet visit, initial play, Replay, Next level and Play level N all ascend to the chosen course's start. Only initial play expands the cabinet; later starts remain fullscreen. The non-cabinet debug path keeps its earlier replay behavior. The HUD caches safe vertical framing on show/resize. `pilot.framePoint` uses a raised overview only for orbit; shoulder and first person retain their own framing, and exit clears the offset with `pilot.releaseFrame`. The course-facing view stays under player control after any guide cards close. Arcade cards are modeless so the player and voice tabs remain usable. Crew hooks `jetpackRefills`, `jetpackLiftOnly` and `npcPaused` isolate disposable fuel, vertical-only thrust and NPC simulation, and `spentJump` hands the sky feather a Space press with both jumps spent. The controller restores equipment on return and cancels synchronously on hub departure. The hub's `ensureCloudHopper` installs its controller, HUD, weather, sound and game-only meshes on first cabinet use, not on ordinary hub entry; the same hub visit reuses them and full `leave` disposes them. Hosted pages defer its code and art as `cloud-hopper`; `cloud-hopper-levels.js` stays inline for `game.js`, and the portable page stays self-contained. |
| `scene-hub.js`, `scene-lab.js` | the island (registers first, so it is the landing scene) and the lab. The plaza's library is a shallow nook the island cuts into the hillside left of the Timechain stairs (the `nook` option of `terrain.island`, dressed by `hubModels.libraryNook` and lit by `libraryLamp`): a short entry cut up from the stair terrace, a roofed stone mouth and three shelves of books (Learn Me A Bitcoin, Bitcoin.org and the whitepaper) whose picks open one book a page in the shared board window (`libraryBoard`), each page linking out in a new tab. GAME MODE (`setGameMode`, handed to an island event as `gameMode`) holds the island still for play while an event runs: every gorilla nobody drives away (clankers' `setAway`), the trees' sway held (`RENDER_OPTS.windTime`), no fireworks, the meadow's fire pit out, the act button and every message at the foot of the screen (toasts and the hint pill) hidden (`body.game-mode`), the top left's equipment controls hidden (the rifle kept on a touch screen, to fire), the crew's donation cheer held back (`celebrate`), a ring wall round the meadow well inside the bluff's foot (`GAME_RING`, 18.5 m, so a shoulder camera behind someone at the wall stays out of the rock, rising `ringDelay` seconds after the event begins and sinking as it ends) that refuses a step out across it while letting anyone outside walk in (and brings the visitor's own Ooga in to the pile if it stands outside as the ring starts up, `bringInsideRing`), unseen, `RING_PANELS` flat veils of B.C.A.'s kind that outline the driven Ooga in amber glyphs where it touches them, as B.C.A.'s veil does (`ringBodies`, each section's atlas made the first time someone reaches it), and ripple where another player does (`touchGameRing`), with paper lanterns floating along its top (`RING_LANTERNS`), the Timechain Sphere not turning, no clouds (the island's own and the Mempool island's deck, `weather.hold`), and the Mempool island hidden and not run; all of it back when the event lets go and in `leave`. The Mempool island, its tunnels and its chamber are part of the hub: `buildMempoolIsland` returns the handle everything else asks (`groundAt`, `rainAt`, which adds the tree crowns the rain lands on, `overAt`, `coveredAt`, `floatAt`, `walked`, `water`, `paintings`, `layout`, and `preview` for the maintainer). Its ground is solid-prop meshes, so the shoulder logic must know them as walked on (`walked`) or a rising terrace or the lake's bowl is taken for an obstacle to pass round. Its rock is kept out of the outline registry (`exclude`) and answers sight through the layout's grid instead: `poolSeam` is asked only for the walker's own rim there, drawn when the view stands in open air and rock wholly hides them, and never the full outline pass, since the island is one owner in the registry. Its tunnel and chamber lamps burn always but count as point lights only for a view under its ground (`poolShade`) or following a walker who is (`poolUnder`), fading out by distance from the lamp, or from the room's middle for the chamber's set (a lamp's `reach`, `fade` and `from`): every light costs every pixel, and from the home island they lit nothing in sight. World-height checks that mean the home island's rooms (`belowHome`, the cutaway's `subterranean`, the crew's `underHome`) are gated to the home island, since the chamber lies lower than its ground. The Library of Bitcoin has five rows of books and long scrolls, glowing sign letters and two lanterns. |

The sector `bake` carries each piece's shading normals (explicit, or averaged for `smooth` ones), so cartoon decor keeps its soft shading merged |
| `skydiver.js`, `drop-hud.js`, `drop-audio.js`, `drop-models.js`, `scene-drop.js` | Ooga Drop: the diver's physics, HUD, sound, the roof plane and course props, the scene |
| `haunt-models.js` | The Ghost Hunter's gargoyle and iron fence builders, outside the deferred arcade group so Block Frenzy's Haunted Hollow can use them |
| `grapple.js` | The grappling hook's arithmetic, no scene or state of its own: `toward` (the hook head's flight), `pullSpeed`, `march` (a ray through a game's solid test) and `tether` (a rope length constraint, so a pull swings). Cloud Hopper's hook and Block Frenzy's both call it |
| `block-sim.js`, `block-trivia.js`, `block-models.js`, `scene-block.js` | Block Frenzy: capture the flag on Fort Chaos with glowing Bitcoin blocks for flags. The rules as pure functions (two a side, paint, grenades, rockets and nets, a splatted carrier drops the block where it stands for either side to pick up, a carrier can throw it to a teammate (Tab), its own side carrying it home by hand, scoring only with your own block home, pushable crates you can also lift, stack three high and throw to stun a rival, a fortifying start, seeded weather of clear spells and storms with rain and lightning, quiz-trap pads that hold an Ooga in a bubble until a Bitcoin question is answered, a double jump and crouch, a second storey of stilt huts, a centre deck and fort walls to stand on, a sniper tower at each end whose rifle fires only from its platform through a zoom scope, a zipline from each tower into the other end, ammo caches that refill like a stand, the Ooga AK and the Golden Dual AKs, four maps, every one a floating island with open edges and a jetpack return, hub-style mounds, caves and outcrops, two hidden grappling hooks a round, three or four gorillas a map that drag an Ooga by the feet to the edge and throw it off, seeded bots), the question bank, the arena and props, the scene with a chase camera and first person. See `docs/block-frenzy.md` |
