| `mine-rigs.js`, `mine-sim.js`, `mine-models.js`, `mine-hud.js`, `mine-audio.js`, `mine-crew.js`, `scene-mine.js` | Ooga Mine: catalog arithmetic, the seeded allocation-free sim (`snapshot`, `save`, `load`), the cave and its `LAYOUT`, panels, sound, operators, and the scene, which is only a view of the sim |
| `factory-window.js` | The Factory entrance and roof tube views: the shared baked hall projected along the camera's rays into a shallow relief, driven by the one factory node. The roof view points down above the cooker and supports orthographic views; two clip slabs bound it to the lined bore. Terrain and real/stand-in cave ceilings have matching circular apertures. Both windows dispose their feed listeners on leaving the hub; Canvas 2D keeps the entrance's existing dark fallback. It also holds the walk through the shield both scenes share: `GLIDE`'s reach, pace and timings, and the frame held over the switch, one canvas a page: `grab(canvas)` takes the scene's frame right after it is drawn, and `still(ctx, k)` paints it over the next scene's overlay while it dissolves; and `crossing` and `arrival`, the same walk through a door turned any way (the Lightning Jet Hangar's, both sides): where a walker stands to the door's side and past its shield as it goes through, and the same on the far side coming in |
| `factory-feed.js`, `factory-mock.js`, `factory-kiosk.js`, `factory-models.js`, `scene-factory.js` | The Lightning Factory: the reader of a node's events under Foundry's public contract and the demo contract, with `node(world)`, the page's one factory node (its feed, the demo node playing into it, and where each line stands); the seeded demo node that stands in for a real one; the donation kiosk's screen flow, the simulated donation as the concepts' screens draw it (attract with a QR and START, the QR the visit's donation link, or in real mode this site's own Factory address, amount on a keypad, its quote in bananas, both showing who the tip is from (an editable name, the signed-in login or `anon` initially) and its message in a row that `A` or a tap opens on a screen of their own (`details`, back to whichever opened it; from the amount the next CONTINUE asks for the invoice with them, from the quote they update that invoice in place, the screen waiting for that save before it closes and staying up with the error when it fails, to TRY AGAIN or KEEP SAVED, which puts the details the invoice holds back in the fields): the name and the message drawn there in the screen's letters with a caret, typed into the two hidden fields it shares with the mainland popup (`typing()`), which save automatically and stay editable until settlement starts, an invoice with a fresh donation request as its QR and a five-minute expiry, or in real mode the API's invoice, asked for as soon as the amount is confirmed, its bolt11 as a `lightning:` QR and its own expiry, or, chosen on the quote beside it at any amount, ON-CHAIN: an address for the same invoice (`hooks.onchain`) as a `bitcoin:` QR for its amount, to send before the invoice expires, then a wait that says the bananas cook once it confirms and lets the visitor go, WAITING FOR A BLOCK once the API sees the payment (`seen`; a short one goes back to the address asking for what is `left`), an invoice left so waited on (`waitFor`) for its "on its way" and "Your on-chain tip confirmed" toasts in any scene, and an invoice without its Lightning half offering on-chain alone; the wait, the payment), painted in the jumbotron's letters on one square canvas, `still` painting the glass's own pictures, and a payment ending the wait only through the donation event the scene hands back (`receive(id, bananas, handle)`), as a real one will, its thanks naming the donor that event names; the hall's geometry, `LAYOUT` (the right-wall `platform` holding the rebalancer, the treasury and the banana cooker) and walkable floor (`supportAt`, `clearAt`, `walkable`, which on foot also holds a body off the decks' and walkways' rails, `railClear`), the cooker's, the kiosk's and the donations board's parts and their places (`COOK`, `KIOSK`, `BOARD`), the kiosk's and the board's timber built in voxels an eighth of a metre across (`PX`, `pixelTimber`) so the renderer's block detail gives it their concepts' pixel texture, each standing in a niche the hall leaves in its rock (`hall`'s recesses, which set the face back flat), `hubWindow`, the window's light stand-in of the hall with the hall's light baked into fully glowing colours (tuned in `WINDOW_MOOD`), and the light kit it and ₿IFRÖST's window bake their stand-ins with (`windowLights`, `bakeWindow`); and the scene, which cooks each donation in view: lime sat cubes from the core (through the exit shield while the node reads stopped) into the cooker's jaw, the jaw gulps them, chews, and spits the bananas down its chute and straight up into a ceiling intake, from fixed-capacity batches; the donation corner, the kiosk with its niche and the donations board, stands only where donations are real or on a debug page (`donationCorner`, decided once a page, after the account's look and before the first build), so a Worker without bananapayserver's binding shows the hall without it; the donation kiosk built into the right wall at the end of the walkway from the balcony (`LAYOUT.kiosk`: a timber frame bolted with iron on a timber platform, its posts rising past the beam to a cap, a framed banana medallion between them, two lanterns on long chains from the cap, a lit grille under the screen) shows its attract screen, or its thanks while the cooker works; Space beside it (the act button reads DONATE! within its reach) or a tap on it glides the view square on to its glass (the pilot under `setExternalControl`, the Ooga put away and the sheet still available), lays the flow over the glass in the overlay and gives it the taps and keys, on a real payment glides round to the `show` view as the cooker takes it (the demo holds the paid tip until they press to watch), and back once the bananas are out or the visitor moves. The kiosk keeps Escape over the sheet unless a panel has focus (`hud.create`'s `escapeHeld`). The island's button opens the same kiosk flow, simulated or real, in the mainland popup without changing scenes; the hub shows every settled donation leaving the Factory roof tube in a high arc with a vertical approach to the pile, for signed-out and signed-in viewers alike. The page's Worker can turn real mode on after a visit has begun: `showMode` then lays the real kiosk, its attract picture and the board's face in place, and a visitor at the kiosk starts over. The donations board on the right wall between the study hall and the kiosk (`LAYOUT.board`, in its own niche) shows, in real mode, the API's tally of every donation (`donations.state.tally`: the totals, today and this week counted from its UTC hours by this page's clock, its latest rate, the last donation's sats; NO READING YET before the first), the same in every browser, and simulated, this browser's tips from the game's tally (`game.tipsWithin`); exact sat totals and banana amounts to two decimals, naming no one or no time, its face labelled with which, its figures a picture repainted only when one changes. A tap opens it in the board dialog (`boardView`, the face and figures painted together by `paintBoard`). The tunnel behind the 2 o'clock mouth (`tunnelFrame`, shared by the island's `hubTunnel` and the hall's `exitTunnel`) is floored in the balcony's own boards, in its order, so it reads as one deck across the shield, stopping on the mouth's stone short of the grass and path; its island end and the balcony in the hub's window, built from the same boards, are coloured as the hall's lamps show them (`hallBoard`), with patch boards after Kortik's (`tunnelBoard`) let into its walls between the timber sets, behind the posts' inner faces so they need no collision. Its shield stands at `SHIELD_Z`, as far back toward the cave's rear wall as the hall's window leaves room for. The hub dresses the mouth with it, a tap on the mouth only saying to walk in, and walks the played Ooga in through its shield, as the Timechain Sphere's doorway and Ooga Arcade's doors do: in the tunnel's last metres, on foot and heading in, the Ooga walks itself down the middle (`factoryGlide`: the pilot reads input as always and the walk then sets the Ooga's steering) while the view swoops into its own eyes (`factoryWindow.eyes`), and as that eye reaches the shield the hall takes over from the same eye with an instant switch, the island's last frame held over its first and dissolving away, from the crossing it is handed (`world.factoryCrossing`: across, how far past; `walkTunnel` opens the tunnel's first steps to the walk alone). The Ooga walks on `stopPast` metres, far enough that the view handed back behind it clears the shield, then the view glides back out of its eyes and the controls return. Out through the gate does the same the other way, from no nearer the arrival's step than `OUT_SLACK`, the island taking over at its own shield. Nothing commits to the walk in before the hall is ready: from 15 m of the mouth or on the walk the island readies it behind the shield, as the Timechain Sphere's doorway readies a room (`door-ready.js`: the Factory's code, then the hangar's, then the scene's `warmSteps` building the hall and uploading its meshes in a few milliseconds a frame, all but the rock and the hangar door's window, whose single atomic costs would stumble a free walk and are left under the held frame, and which the hub keeps live until the hall's visit holds them; a frame spent on that is `warming` to the quality tier). Not ready by then, the Ooga stands in front of the shield (`factoryWait`) with the island running on, the shield beating and saying so after a moment; the visitor's own move away gives it straight back, and a failed download or a 15 s wait hands it back with a notice, this side of the shield, free to walk in and try again. The hall waits for the hangar's code at most `HANGAR_GRACE`, and not at all when walked into. Where the switch is still slow the Ooga stands a beat in the shield first, so it lands on a still frame; out to an island it must rebuild the hall fades rather than hold a frame for seconds (the crossing's `held`), as the Timechain Sphere's way out does. Each scene keeps the other's meshes on the GPU across the walk (`world.islandKeep`, `world.factoryKeep`, only each scene's own), and the island keeps the Factory's builders warm (`keeps: ["factory"]`, read by the director), so the walk in after the first uploads and rebuilds almost nothing. A walk done, a forward key held through it goes on walking (`controls.takeForward`, from the keys physically down, which a new scene's controls cannot see for themselves); with anything else held the Ooga stands until it is let go (`settle`), since the view has turned round behind it. An Ooga in the air crosses as before, through the fade; the hall keeps the island's driving rules and reports the Ooga driven to the room (`net.setBody`), so players in it share voice |
| `tess.js` | Tess, the flying guide, as a rig a place drives: her meshes in three detail tiers (each worn for one of her first frames, so the renderer uploads them behind the curtain or fade), the tumbling rings, her beam (a camera-facing ribbon, white-hot in the middle and amber at the edges) and its glint, flight along a path she steers (`clearPath`, `push`, `steer`, the last point following someone while `track`ed), her moods (`calm`, `steady`, `flare`, `nod`; and `uneasy`, 0 to 1, her rings faltering out of step, her eye narrowing and dimming and her trembling, `whirl`, her rings spun up that many times faster, brighter and throwing motes once fast, `tilt`, rad, a lean to one side she eases into, and `flip`, over backwards in the air with a little lift, or with a share of a turn only tipped back that far and back again, which a place sets and which leave her where she is), her speech through `fx` (`say`, `next`, `more`, `said`, `hush`), and her sparks and motes from her own fixed pool of 24 nodes, never the scene's particles, so a place's node count stays fixed while she works. `create` returns the rig; `factory-greeter.js` (the Factory's guide) and `hub-guide.js` drive it. Her root is `sightHidden`: the outline registry walks past her, and the hub shows her speech bubble without asking it |
| `hub-guide.js` | Tess on the island: she works the plaza's machines (`WORK`) and greets once a page (`world.tessGreeted`), only a visit that lands on the island's own view (no routed place, no `view=`, not walked back out of a cave or come from ₿IFRÖST) and only while that view has not moved; a visitor who moves first, or while she flies over, is left be, and a tap on her offers the island tour at any time (her rings flare at once). Every flight to the visitor or to a tour's stop takes about `COME_TIME`, 2.5 s, however far out the view is. A visitor not signed in gets the island tour (`STOPS`: ten landmarks, each an `eye`, a `look` and her lines) until this browser has answered it, Show me or Not now (localStorage `ooga-tess-tour`); a signed-in one gets what is new since she last offered them news in this browser (`NEWS`, dated, newest first, each at a stop or at none for what is everywhere: she keeps, for each login, the newest entry of the news they last answered, Show me or Not now, in localStorage `ooga-tess-visits`, and everything listed above it is new; news ignored waits for the next visit), and no greeting when nothing is. A feature worth showing a returning visitor is one `NEWS` entry and its line, added at the top. A tour holds the pilot inactive (`setActive(false)`, which drops held keys and stops the driven Ooga) and under `setExternalControl`, and glides the view stop to stop by itself; a movement meanwhile (`controls.moving`) ends it at once, the view the Ooga's own again and the movement going on to it (`setActive(true, true)`), and she goes back to work; she speaks only once she has arrived, a stop's lines going on a bubble at a time, and once she has said them the tour moves to the next stop, or home after the last. Her beam points at the stop's `look`, except through a line in `POINT_AT`, when she faces the middle of the points the scene hands her as `targets` (the totems' eyes) and points a beam at each at once (the rig's `aims`, up to four). Next (Enter) skips through the bubbles, then to the next stop. The view is the visitor's again when the tour ends. Under her offer a question box and three suggestions (`SUGGESTIONS`) ask her things the page answers by itself (#270): `answer(question, last)` folds case, accents and punctuation and returns the lines she says, a place to take the visitor (`go`, a one-stop trip there and back to her offer), the place it was about (`place`, for a following "take me there") or the island tour, and null for what the page can't answer. That goes to the Worker's `/api/tess` where the Worker says it answers them (`net.state.tess`, `/api/me`'s `tess`), with the place last asked about: she thinks aloud meanwhile, a `go` in its answer is a trip, an answer arriving after she has moved on is dropped, and a failure gets one of her own lines (`FAILED`); with no backend, or when that fails, she says she doesn't know that one yet. Her offer stands as long as a question is being written (the box focused or holding a draft), and Enter on her own buttons (Ask, a suggestion, Show me, Not now) presses that button rather than going on with the tour. While a question is out with the Worker, Ask and the suggestions are disabled and the box keeps the next question as a draft. On a touch screen the panel keeps to the room between the two sticks. Another place hands `create` its own tables as `guide` (its `stops`, `lines`, `work`, `answer`, `suggestions` and `pointAt`, its names in her offer and tour, the `world` flag and localStorage key she remembers it by, and what the Worker hears a question is about): it has no news and offers its tour to everyone until this browser has answered it, and its answers may carry their own `say` text instead of line ids. `lend(driver)` lets a place borrow her, the hangar while Kortik's Sis smashes the Jet (`lent` says whether one has), and `light(out, offset)` writes her light |
| `hangar-guide.js` | Tess in the Lightning Jet Hangar, on `hub-guide.js`'s machinery handed the hangar's own tables (`guide`): she works its machines (`WORK`), greets whoever walks in once a page (`world.tessHangarGreeted`) and offers its tour (`STOPS`: the Jet, Kortik's Sis, the static fire booth, the terminals and projector, the boards, the launch tunnel and the way back, her beam finding Sis wherever she is and the projector and its screen, `POINT_AT`) until this browser has answered it (localStorage `ooga-tess-hangar-tour`), a tap on her bringing it at any time. Under her offer she answers questions about the Jet (`answer`, three `SUGGESTIONS`): from her own lines what it began as and what Jet 2.0 is, an autonomous economic engine for LND routing nodes; from the repository's reading (`jetFeed.reading()`) who builds it, when it began, its releases, stars and forks, commits, and issues and pull requests; from her own lines Jet v2, rebalancing, HTLCs, routing fees, the nodes it runs on, where its code is and Kortik; a place in the hangar is a trip there and back; anything else goes to the Worker's `/api/tess` as about the Lightning Factory (her fact sheet knows the hangar from its route alone). `distraction(sis)` is what the scene lends her to while Kortik's Sis smashes the Jet: she flies over fast and buzzes round over Sis's head, flaring and calling to her (`CALLS`), and says her piece once it is over (`done`). `unsettled(shimmer, camera)` is what the scene lends her to when the faint shape in the air (`hangar-shimmer.js`) shows near her (within 14 m, in her sight past walls, boxes and the Jet, the shape's `seen`) and she is free a moment after it has formed (`due`; not with too little of it left). Most times (a little over half, more when little of it is left) she only glances at it: she stops where she is, turns to face it, now and then drifting a little toward it or aside where the air is open, watches it a few seconds and goes back to work. Otherwise, with at least `stay` s of it left, she looks it over: she stops where she is, facing it, a beat, then comes over unhurried in two moves, rising over what stands between and going round it where the straight way would pass within 2.8 m of it (`around`), the second slower, to hang 3.8 to 5 m off to its side as the view sees it, turned a little toward the view (the side nearer her where the air is open, `UNEASY`), facing it, her rings turning a little quicker (`whirl`); there she watches it, moving a little round it once or twice; as it comes apart her rings wind down and she watches it start to go and eases back a touch, or, still on her way, stops where she is, and watches it go, and a moment after it has gone she goes back to work (`over`, then the scene's `reset`). Rarely (`puzzle`) it puzzles her: she comes over that way or watches it from where she is, her rings quickening faster and faster (`puzzled`), and once makes one small, uncertain movement (a tilt to one side, `tilt`; a tip back, a share of a `flip`; or her rings faltering and slowing, `uneasy`); as it comes apart she hesitates, edging a little toward it, and once it has gone she keeps watching where it stood 2.5 to 4 s, looking to either side of it, now and then tilting once more, then drifts back to work. The stares, pauses, speeds, distances and moves are drawn afresh each time. Anything ending it early sends her back sooner, and a tap on her while she watches it ends it at once, so she answers as she would at work (the scene's tap); under reduced motion her one movement is only half a tilt and her rings quicken a quarter as much |
| `ghost-herald-data.js`, `ghost-entry.js`, `ghost-gear.js`; `ghost-sim.js`, `ghost-art-data.js`, `ghost-models.js`, `ghost-audio-data.js`, `ghost-audio.js`, `ghost-font-data.js`, `ghost-hud.js`, `ghost-sync.js`, `ghost-game.js`; `ghost-boss-audio-data.js`, `ghost-track-*.js` | HALLOWEEN, temporary: Ooga Arcade's ghost hunt from OBL-Audio-lab, played in its ghost arena (`scene-arcade.js`), deferred groups `halloween` (fetched by `ghost-entry.js` only on a player's click or an active hunt from the server; an idle visit keeps only the herald's three pictures, four wisps and the room listeners, a failed load retries on its tap, and leaving cancels the handoff) and `halloween-track-<key>` (the hunt's playlist, a deferred group a track: the lab's boss music and five tracks the maintainer supplied, each fetched only as it comes up, the next while the current plays). The playlist plays in a new random order from each hunt's start to its victory, one decoded track at a time, with a mini player at the bottom right (`#ghost-player`: what is playing, a skip button when there is more than one track, and a music switch, remembered in localStorage `ooga-ghost-music`). A white herald ghost circled by green wisps floating over the arena's middle; clicked from inside the arena, in carry or combat view (`clickHerald`), it calls the gorillas' "oogaa" as its lobby opens; once the hunt starts an orange beam comes down on it and the gargoyles' eyes shine green lasers onto it, it shoots straight up while the land quakes, the arcade goes into its GAME MODE (combat on, the iron fence up across the hallway's mouth), the night deepens (the arena keeps its stone floor: no blight, `hub.blight` false), and hunters' rifles fire larger off-white rocks, unlimited, until dawn (crew.js `ctx.shotGeometry`), growing stronger with the rounds into yellow (3 to 5), green (6 and 7) and, for the boss, red laser (ghost-sim.js `WEAPON`, `weaponTier`; a laser round's geometry is `straight`, flown along its line), three random drops fall with round 1 (one that would do the player walking into it no good, a health banana on a full bar or Tess, the monkeys or double points they already have, stays for someone else: ghost-sim.js `wants`), seven rounds and the boss as the eighth come in over the arena's wall top (`hub.spots`) (ghost-sim.js `ROUNDS`, scaled for more players by `PLAYER_SCALING`), with a few drops as each round is cleared (`ROUND_DROPS`; these and round 1's more for more players, `PLAYER_SCALING`'s `drops`), and one every `MID_DROP_MS` (25 s, sooner for more players) while a round or the boss is fought (`midDropAt`, in a snapshot's optional `y`). Green gorillas (the Agent rig in its code form, a quarter larger than the island's, 80 health in round 1; every enemy but the boss grows tougher with the rounds, ghost-sim.js `HP_SCALE` and `hpOf`) are called down by a green beam when a round is down to its last two ghosts, call their "oogaa" as they drop, land shaking the ground for a second and leap at players. A giant spider striped purple and green (a gorilla's slot with `kind` 2, `SPIDER` in ghost-sim.js, 120 health in round 2 and tougher after, as every enemy is, built from ghost-models.js `spiderParts`, one in round 2 and two a round after, sent once that round's gorillas are down, `spidersFor`) drawn at twice its model's size (ghost-game.js `SPIDER_SCALE`) with three-part bristled legs, is called down in a white beam and walks after the nearest player, each foot planted on the floor and its leg reaching it by two-bone IK, a foot that strays from its rest stepping there with the rest of its four, the two fours in turn (`walkSpider`, `STEP_*`); within 7 m it rears and spits a glob of green venom that arcs to where they stood and splashes everyone within the spider's own width of its landing (`venomR`, 2.4 m), a green ring that wide pulsing on the ground there (its flight rides in the gorilla's leap fields, so the snapshot keeps its layout); Shift+V on a debug page calls one. The Halving Hourglass (`kind` 3, `HOURGLASS`, `hourglassParts`), a warded tower of stone and brass round its glass with a green crystal on its spire, comes down in a green beam somewhere open in rounds 2 and 4 and as the ghost boss's fight begins, stands there with its sand running, ten small green orbs circling it in three bands (ghost-game.js `poseGlass`) and a thick green beam held on its crystal while it stands (`glassBeams`), and while any stands every rifle round does half damage (ghost-sim.js `halving`, read by `shotDamage`; a HALVING notice under the scoreboard, `#ghost-halving`); the boss's fall (round 8) is the victory. Their sent counts ride in a snapshot's optional `y` (`STATE_EXTRA`). On a debug page Shift+Z calls an hourglass. A round that brings in new enemies opens with a card for each in turn, its countdown `INTRO_MS` (4 s) longer for every card (ghost-sim.js `INTRO_PAGES`, `introPages`, `introMs`: round 1 ghosts and gorillas, round 2 generals, skeletons, the spider and the hourglass, round 3 wraiths and the Fiat Rush, then the boss; `#ghost-intro`, ghost-hud.js `intro`, ghost-game.js `INTROS`): on the lobby card's wood, what the enemy does under the enemy itself, a ghost its own picture hovering (ghost-models.js `portrait`) and the rest the hunt's own models playing a short loop (`FIGURES`, baked by the HUD's icon renderer, `BL.iconRenderer`, into `CARD_MAX` pooled pictures a frame at a time while the round before ends or the card is up, the "$" painted over them since it draws no pictures), the round's banner raised and smaller over it. Killed in a fight, a spider bursts with a shriek (ghost-audio.js `brood`, which each spiderling's and each ghost's death plays too, one at a time) into fifteen spiderlings (`LING` in ghost-sim.js, their own pool carried in a snapshot's optional `l` rows, so older pages skip them): flung out of it, they scuttle after the nearest player and bite within reach, each one node swapping between two baked strides (ghost-models.js `spiderling`, ghost-game.js `drawLings`); a bomb kills them and the round waits for them all. When round 3's ghosts are down to their last two, a green FIAT RUSH banner stands 2 s and then twenty more (ten more for each player past the first, at most sixty: `swarmFor`) drop in one at a time near the hunters, each called down in a green beam and only while fewer than ten spiderlings are alive (`LING.swarm*` and `drop*`, spiderling phase `DROP`, untouchable until it lands; `swarmAt`, `swarmSent` the count dropped, `swarmNext`). A glowing green "$" floats over every spider and spiderling (ghost-models.js `dollar`), and a spider landing shakes the ground a second while a woman's voice calls "Fiat Rush!" (ghost-audio-data.js `fiatRush`, macOS speech as `network`). A skeleton (a gorilla's slot with `kind` 1, `BONES` in ghost-sim.js, a plain ghost's health, built in bone from ghost-models.js `skeletonParts`) is called down in a white beam at its own point in each round from the second (2 then one more a round, and one more for every two players past the first, `bonesFor`): only its upper body, its jaw hanging open and green fire in its sockets, it crawls after the nearest player, each arm reaching its hand along a planted-then-lifted path by two-bone IK (`CRAWL` in ghost-game.js), its broken spine dragging, and within reach lunges up at them and bites (`LUNGE`, a synthesized `bite`), slamming the ground beside them (every player near the slam is hurt and the land shakes); lightning strikes the arena through the night. Its banners (each round, a round cleared, the boss, the end, a pause) stand large and red in the lab's Creepster (`ghost-font-data.js`, SIL Open Font License, added as a `FontFace` from its bytes, so the content policy needs no font source), the arena darker still while one is up. Once a round (and once in the boss's fight) a white lightning bolt spins over the arena's middle in a blue beam, announced in a woman's voice ("Lightning Network"); the first player to reach it calls a bolt down on every ghost and gorilla in turn, each shaking for two seconds and dying (the boss loses a quarter of his health), and every landed spiderling burns at once. The monkeys are one of the random drops (the lab's monkey sidearm, in its purple light, a clip of a child's Spanish lesson saying "monkey" as they are taken, the lab's shriek as a hit takes them away), and come down on a player by themselves, in a purple beam as they glow purple, every 30 enemies they kill themselves (`MONKEY_EVERY_KILLS`, counted one an enemy per player, `foes`; a gift earned while they still have a pair waits until a hit takes those, `giftMonkeys`): a monkey sits low at each of its player's sides, drawn from the front, side or back as the player faces, and with each of their shots each fires a burst of orange laser aimed a little to its own side of theirs, for a rifle round's damage (aimed and landed on the shooter's page, as their own hits are); they and Tess stay through the first hit and leave once their player's bar falls below four (`POWER_KEEP_SEG`). Waking the herald opens a lobby first (`STATE.GATHER`, 30 s; `#ghost-lobby`, the land's title card; only pages in the arena hear it), with the arena left as it is (no night, rocks or fence until LAP): the players there ready up (the Ready button, R, or the herald clicked again; a follower's `ready` message), the one who woke it marked its host. Nothing starts until the host is ready: then the hunt starts when everyone there is ready, when four have readied (the hunt's most, the arcade's `maxPlayers`, ghost-sim.js `maxReady`; a fifth is told it is full), or at the lobby's end with whoever is. The host not ready by the end (whoever else is) closes it with no hunt, the arena as it was. Only those who readied are in the hunt (`inGame`); anyone else in the arena is walked out to its hallway (the arcade's `spectate`), where the fence keeps them and anyone who arrives meanwhile until it ends, and where, signed in, they keep following the hunt to watch it (only pages in the arena see its lobby). A hunter may leave a hunt under way with Exit hunt (`quit`; ghost-sim.js `leave`, a follower's `leave` message): out to the hallway, the hunt going on for the others and ending at once when nobody is left in it. A signed-out page runs its own private hunt with a "Sign in to hunt with others" line in its lobby. The lobby card also carries how to play (move, aim and fire; touch words on a coarse pointer, a pad's once one is connected), and readying up puts the visitor's Ooga in combat (the arcade's `readyGun`). Nobody has a gun in the arena or its lobby: when the hunt starts each hunter, and no spectator, gets the ghost blaster, a black wand with no magazine, and a black pack strapped on with a hose running to it (`ghost-gear.js`; the arcade's `arm` for the visitor, its remote pool's `posed` through `isHunter` for the others), and the small gun button in its own icon with its ∞ (`hud.setWeaponSkin`), which stays up through the hunt on every pointer. A minimap stands bottom left (top left below the banner and fire button on a touch screen) while the hunt is on (`#ghost-minimap`, ghost-game.js `drawMinimap`): an FPS radar turning with the view, the arena from above, its wall, the middle, the elixirs, a cone for the field of view, the other players and every enemy. A scoreboard stays fixed to the top centre through the hunt (`#ghost-scores`, ghost-game.js `drawScores`; under the title on a narrow screen): each hunter's Ooga face in a circle (hud.js `renderFaceIcon`) with their points under it, the leader's circle glowing green. The boss (400 health, 200 more for each hunter past the first, at most 1,200: ghost-sim.js `BOSS`) shows himself inside the arena for 3 s at a time (`placeBoss`, `bossShown`; only then can he be shot), spits enemies of every kind as he does (`SPIT`, `spitOne`: ghosts, generals, wraiths, skeletons and gorillas), vanishes in a puff and comes again somewhere else, clear of the hunters, while his generals still come in over the wall. Through its night each hunter still standing carries a soft lantern over their head so the others can see them (ghost-game.js `lanterns`, taking the arena's point lights after the herald's glow, `lanternsWanted`). A won hunt's banner is green, smaller and higher, and holds `VICTORY_MS` (7.5 s) over a results card in the lobby card's wood (`#ghost-results`, ghost-hud.js `results`, ghost-game.js `drawPodium`): the ghosts busted, a podium of three steps with the top three by points standing whole and square to the view (hud.js `renderBodyIcon`), the first in the middle, highest and floating a little, a place nobody took left bare, and every hunter's enemies killed and points; the herald is back over the middle `COOLDOWN_MS` (5 s) after the dawn, for the next hunt. After the last round's break the boss gets a countdown of his own, as a round does (ghost-sim.js `bossNext`). On a debug page Shift+9 jumps straight to a won hunt's end with mock hunters from the roster (ghost-game.js `mockResults`). Each player has five hit points, shown while a round is in play as a five-cell bar on a plank sign the overlay holds over their own Ooga's head, or below the crosshair in first person or when the head is off screen (ghost-game.js `drawHealth`); a round's start makes everyone standing whole again, each glowing bright orange as their bar fills back. A player whose bar empties goes down for the rest of the round (`DOWN_UNTIL`; in the boss's fight, the rest of the hunt) and is back whole as it ends: a gravestone rises where they fell with their Ooga's own head set on its top (one of four lines of "Game over, man!" at random, `gameOver1` to `gameOver4` in ghost-audio-data.js, plays as it rises, or `gameOverLast` when nobody is left standing), their body is hidden (theirs on their page, everyone else's by the arcade's remote players, `down`), and until they are back their view only follows the others (the arcade's `holdView`, the pilot let go of): it rides behind a hunter still standing (one still in the hunt, driving an Ooga in the arena, `watchable`), the turn keys or a stick swinging it round them (`heldAxes`), Next in the spectator bar bottom centre (`#ghost-spectate`, above the sticks on a touch screen, naming the one followed) stepping on to the next, and it moves on by itself when the one followed goes down or leaves; with nobody left standing it holds still before their own grave, nothing moving it about on its own. An elixir (EntropyLab's logo in an orange glow, ghost-art-data.js `elixir`) hangs before each of the arena's four totems, the hub's repository carvings lit always (`elixirSpots`) while a round is fought, set out afresh each round; a player who reaches one empty-handed carries it, bobbing beside their health bar (a choir as it is taken, `choir` in ghost-audio-data.js), and standing by a player who is down for 3 s (`ELIXIR` in ghost-sim.js; the down spot the hunt keeps, `downX`, `downZ`, where their grave stands on every page, however their page reports them meanwhile) spends it to bring them back whole, an orange ring over the grave counting the seconds down for everyone as it does (`drawReviveRings`) and the pickup's choir a fifth louder as they rise, glowing the elixir's orange for 3 s as a drop does where it lies (an aura round them, a pulsing glow and a ring rolling out on the ground, `drawReviveGlow`). Tess is lent to the hunt (the arena's own rig, `arenaTess`, out of sight until then): a drop that, taken, follows its player and zaps the nearest ghost dead every 2 s until their bar falls below four. On a debug page P pauses and resumes it for everyone (a following page asks the server, which takes it only from a `GAME_DEBUG_IDS` account; at most two minutes, `PAUSE_MAX_MS`). `?skip=N` starts at round N (8, or anything past the last round, at the boss's countdown); on a `debug=1` page Shift with the lab's keys (1–6 the lab's drops, 7 the monkeys, 8 the lightning, N, G, J, X, Y a gorilla, H a skeleton, E an elixir and a stand-in hunter down in front of you to revive, O, B, U, K) drives a hunt on a page alone. `ghost-sim.js` is the rules (pure, loadable in Node and the Worker, seeded, snapshot/load), with the arena they run in (`ARENA`, checked against the arcade's) and what a shot meets (`targets`, `resolveShot`, `resolveBash`, `hitBy`, `shotDamage`, the monkeys' `monkeyOrigin` and `monkeyAim`). The server runs it for everyone signed in (`worker/src/games/ghost-hunt.js` on `game-host.js`, `docs/game-host.md`): a page in the arena or its hallway holds a socket to it (ghost-entry.js `channel`, over `game-net.js`) and follows it, sending where its player stands, its start, ready, leave and pause, and every rifle round and bash with the hunt time it showed and the target it saw them meet (`ghost-sync.js`); the server rewinds each to that moment, decides what it met and its damage, and sends the hunt's state four times a second and every enemy's place each tick (`game-wire.js`), which a following page draws 120 ms behind the newest, eased between ticks (ghost-game.js `smooth`, `lag`). A page shows its predicted hits at once; kills and health come from the server's state. A page alone (signed out, no backend) runs the same rules itself. The arcade reaches it through one adapter (`arenaHunt`), and the hunt works about the arena's middle (`origin`: it draws under a group stood there and brings the scene's players, camera and hits in to it). Removing it: delete these files, `worker/src/games/ghost-hunt.js` and `worker/test/ghost.test.mjs`, then every line `grep -rn HALLOWEEN src worker test docs AGENTS.md` finds (the hunt's checks in `worker/test/game-host.test.mjs` among them; the game host itself stays) |
| `factory-audio.js` | The Lightning Factory's sound, on Ooga Mine's pattern: one context after a gesture (a pointer or key press also wakes one the browser left suspended), pooled tone and noise voices gated by gain and panned, the page-wide mute, background playback across tab changes, closed in `dispose`. Its bed is a factory at work, shaped ten times a second by `update(dt, room)` from what the scene writes into `room` in place: the mains hum and the air everywhere, and by distance the core's thrum (dark with the node, swelling with a surge), the forge breathing and roaring with its anvil ringing while it works, the rebalancer spinning up and down, the cooker's churn, the nearest shield's shimmer, a cart on its rails, the coin's roll and Kortik's board buzzing with its relays, which comes up only close to it. `listen(x, y, z, fx, fz)` takes where the visitor hears from (the driven Ooga, else the view's target) and the view's forward once a frame, and `cue(name, x, y, z, loud, n)` plays an event's own sound where it happens, quieter with distance (half at `REF`, Kortik's at `NEAR_REF`) and panned to its side: the node starting, ready and stopping; peers; a line built, switched on, taken down and off; carts, the forge's whoomph, the mint's wind-up and ching, the coin down its shaft; a forward's swish off its line, each featured line's own soft note (`NOTES`) as the first of its sats arrives, the Routing Console's relays, a failure's low bump and a surge, every event at `EVENTS` of its level under the bed; a fee into the treasury and the crate filling; a rebalance, its settling and a failed one; the hourly summary's teleprinter; a tip's sparkle, its cubes, the jaw's gulp, each chomp and each banana spat; a peer shield crossed; and Kortik's (above), his voice through a throat of its own, on which his sister answers him from behind the hangar's door (`sis`), higher and muffled. An unknown cue throws. `heard` counts every cue asked for, heard or not. Created in the Factory's `enter`, disposed in its `leave`; the banner's speaker (or M while voice is off) mutes it with every other environment sound (`sound.js`) |
| `kortik-models.js`, `kortik.js` | Kortik's Switchboard, the Lightning Factory's manual patchboard beside the Routing Console: a deck at the console's height forward and to its left (`LAYOUT.kortik`, with a stair up from the pit and a level bridge onto the console's deck in `LAYOUT.stairs`), the board along its west edge facing into the hall (`KORTIK`: where its face stands and what blocks the walkers). Its geometry in the wall's frame (`BOARD`: a grid of turned brass jacks with a lamp over each, the face laid in tiles and its border and strips in short lengths so the Canvas 2D sort keeps the jacks in front, cords already patched across the jacks out of reach, a U desk with the plugs and keys on the shelf over his knees and wings either side dressed as the concept is, in lathe, tube and prism parts: hardbacks with round spines and gilt bands, his lightning mug (its own node, `MUG`), a tote of tools, the open routing log (`LOG`), the red phone, papers, a side cabinet with a toolbox, crates and cord, lanterns whose two lights (`BOARD.lights`) join the hall's lantern pool, the notes and the sign), and Kortik, a fictional samurai in his own voxel rig under a name no login can have (`kortik/factory`, so he never joins the roster): kimono, hakama, sash, topknot, beard, arms jointed at the elbow, and a katana in two quaternion-turned pieces, the scabbard and a sword whose faceted blade lies hidden in it (`KATANA`). `kortik.js` runs him on a fixed set of nodes, allocating nothing a frame: lamps flash as calls come in, and his fists reach by two-bone IK to carry a cord's plug from the shelf and push it into the jack, throw its key, pull it when the lamp flickers out and move plugged cords between jacks. When the view is near he acts out one emote at a time with a quiet between (`EMOTES`, one line each in `LINES`: hands up, a facepalm, a yanked repatch, a fist on the desk that makes the mug jump, the same cord in and out, a search under his mug, a slow clap for a red lamp, a wagging point, a shout for Tess, a suspicious lean back, a glare at the Routing Console, a tapped lamp), and now and then the katana: up on his stool, the sword drawn into both hands, three cuts leaving a trail and sparks, every channel force-closed, sheathed, and back down to rewire. A tap on him, or Space from an Ooga beside his stool (the act button reads POKE), pokes him (`poke`): he flinches and answers back at once, angrier a poke (`anger`, one to five, cooling a level every `CALM` seconds left alone, and his tooltip says how he is), and acts on it once his hands are free: a glare, a wagging finger, fists and steam from his ears, red-faced on his stool throwing a plug that his cord yanks back, his sword drawn on the visitor, then the silent treatment until the `PATIENCE`-th ignored poke sends him at the board with the katana. His board is a station like the others (`kortikBoard`, the `kortik` view), and Tess's payments tour ends on his deck, where her first line there has him greet it (`tourHere`: swivelled round, arms folded, one of his tour lines) unless he is fuming. Each of his actions reports its sound through `onSound(name, amount)` while the view is near (a plug pulled, lifted, pushed home or stood back, a key, a call ringing in, a red lamp's buzzer, the fist, the mug, the stool, a thrown plug, a poke, the steam, the sword drawn, each cut, the force-close, the sheathing, and his voice under each line, muttered or yelled, `amount` its syllables), which the scene plays at his board. `__ooga.factory.kortik.emote(name)` plays one next. Where the hall has the Lightning Jet Hangar's door (`onSis`, given by the scene only while `hangarEntry`), he turns toward it now and then while someone is near, a hand to his mouth, and shouts a line of his quarrel with his sister (`RIVALRY`, `QUARREL`, the `sis` emote), and she answers through the door (`onSis(i)`: the scene's `sisAnswers`, her line over the door and `factoryAudio`'s `sis` cue); the hangar plays the same quarrel the other way round. Comedy, not routing: nothing reads the node. Created, updated and disposed by the Factory scene |
| `jet-models.js` | Lightning Jet v2, docked in the hangar, in big voxels (its parts drawn on a quarter-unit grid and built `SCALE` times that, `U` metres a voxel), so the renderer's block detail gives it its concept's blocky look: each part shaped as its own cells (`PARTS`: the body with its livery, pinstripe and roundels, the canopy, wings, tail, engines and gear), merged per construction stage into one cached mesh (`jet(stage)`, and `jet(stage, true)` the same with its gold trim and glass lit as bright as the node's `glow`; the engines' nozzles turned and shaded smooth instead, `nozzles`, which `pieces(stage)` fits with the engines; `STAGES`, and `STAGE`, the one the hangar shows, bumped by hand; `?debug=1&jetstage=N` previews another); `SIZE`, `POINTS` (the cockpit's seat and eye, the canopy's hinge, the engines), `EXHAUST` (each nozzle's exit and radius, where the static fire's plumes start), `columns(stage)` (its cells seen from above for walkers, each column's bottom and top over a grid `U` metres square, from the same cells as its mesh, cached) and `LIVERY`, in metres in the jet's frame (nose to -z, the origin on the floor under its middle) |
