| `jet-data.js`, `jet-feed.js` | the Lightning Jet Hangar's boards' reading of drneski/lightning-jet (stats, releases, milestones and Jet 2.0's branch; the schema in `jet-feed.js`): `jetFeed.load()`, called by the hangar on its first visit a page life (off under `nosim` and `jet=0`), sends eight unauthenticated api.github.com calls one after another (the last two Jet 2.0's own branch, `V2`, its commits and this week's, a 404 there only leaving the reading's `v2` null), unless localStorage holds a reading younger than `CACHE_MS` (30 minutes), stops at the first refusal and shares the totems' poller's `limitedUntil`; `reading()` is the best there is (`source`: `fetch`, `cache` or `bake`), `parse` checks every field at the boundary and loads in Node, and `subscribe` hears a change. `jet-data.js` is the fallback snapshot `scripts/jet-data.mjs` bakes through that same `parse` (committed; with `GITHUB_TOKEN` when set, or from a directory of saved responses), keeping the committed file on any failure |
| `hangar-window.js` | The see-through both ways at the Lightning Jet Hangar's door, on `bifrost-window.js`'s pattern: a light stand-in of the room beyond (`hangarModels.doorWindow` from the Factory's walkway, `factoryModels.hubWindow`'s hall, chamber and fire from the hangar's landing), built once at full size in its own frame with its light baked in, pulled each frame along the eye's sight lines into `DEPTH` metres just behind the shield, projective and clipped to the door's passage (`clipSlab` across it, `clipSlab2` through the band, `clipMinY`/`clipMaxY`), the door's glimpse its backdrop; `across` maps the room beyond's frame to this side's door frame. `create({ group, pieces, beyond, plane })`, `update(camera)` once the camera is final, `dispose`; WebGL only, Canvas 2D keeps the glimpse |
| `hangar-audio.js` | The Lightning Jet Hangar's sound, on `factory-audio.js`'s pattern: one context after a gesture, a bed built once and only re-levelled, the page-wide mute, background playback across tab changes, closed in `dispose`. The hall hums: the mains, a deep drone beating and swelling slowly and the air everywhere, the launch tunnel's neon buzzing round a visitor inside it, and the launch shield's and the door's shimmer close to, and a faint breath of air where the hangar's air shimmers (`glint(x, y, z, level)`, from `hangar-shimmer.js`: noise through two bands, no tone). Over it the static fire test, `test(AT)` scheduling the whole sequence at the press on voices built with the bed: the booth's two-tone siren to the blast, the turbines' whine and whoosh rising through the spool-up, the ignition's whoomph, the exhaust's loud rocket roar (its full band and body through a soft clip, a crackle of sparse clicks, a rumble under it), the malfunction's coughing roar, clanks, pings and alarm beeps, the blast's falling boom, rumble and crack and the iron's clatter, and the presser's hearing muffled from the blast to the result with a ringing over it (past the muffle); everything through a limiter. `setVolume` sets its level (localStorage `ooga-hangar-volume`). `listen(x, y, z, fx, fz)` once a frame from the driven Ooga or the view's target, `update(dt)` levels the bed and pans the booth's and the engines' voices ten times a second; created in the hangar's `enter`, disposed in its `leave`. It also gives Kortik's Sis her voice (`voice(text, style, pos)`: a buzz and a breath through three formants, a syllable for each run of vowels with that vowel's formants, pauses at the punctuation, a stressed last syllable before a "!" or a "?", the censor's bleep over each grawlix; muttered, yelled, screamed or whispered (`SIS_STYLE`), each line at its own pitch and pace; placed where she stands while she talks, a yell never quieter than its `least` anywhere in the hall; it returns how long she sounds, ready or not), her boots (`boot`), a poke's boink (`poke`), a warning light's beep (`beep`, a door keypad's keys at their own pitches), the forklift's button's click and its denied buzz (`denied`), the forklift's run (`forklift(on)`: a relay's click, its electric drive spinning up and a ready chime, then its whine and the pump's hum, placed where it stands, its whine climbing with its speed and the pump's hum while its forks move, `forkliftWork`, or all of it running down) and its horn (`horn`) and her hammer's clang (`clang`, louder by `loud` in her rampage), all on voices built with the bed; Kortik shouting through the door from next door comes on her voice too, a man's formants muffled by the wall (`kortik`). The film show's projector has voices of its own: `projector(on)` runs its motor's hum and whir, the gate's clatter (a saw's edge 24 times a second through a highpass, wavering) and the lamp's buzz where it hangs, spinning up as it starts and running down as it stops, with the soundtrack's crackle and hiss from the screen while the film runs; `clunk` is a picture dropping into the gate, and `roll(seconds)` the screen's roller ratcheting as its case winds down or up, or it unrolls or rolls back up. The engines acting up on their own (`hangar-anomaly.js`) sound on the test's voices: `misfire(M)` schedules a failed start at once, an electrical hum rising, three tries whining higher and dying in a sputter, the last a short burst, a dead stop and the metal ticking as it cools; `revs(R)` the two engines revving by turns from a script, each on a voice of its own panned to its side, farther apart than they stand (`REV_SPREAD`, from `spots.engines.gap`); `zap(Z, from, to)` lightning over the Jet, the hum rising, a sizzle panned along the Jet from nose to tail, then a crack with a thump under it; `blackout(B)` a heavy relay's clunk as the power dies, a low hum in the dark and a clunk as each bank comes back on, the mains hum and the neon following `power(k)` (0 off, 1 on); `shuffle(S)` the Jet shifting in its bay: its brakes letting go with a clunk, tyre scrubs, creaks of loaded metal (the clank's ring gliding up) and the engines' short bursts; `lowrider(L)` a sledgehammer's deep, heavy thud as the nose gear comes down on each beat (`BANG`: the hit, a burst of low noise closing down fast, and a dull knock, panned to the nose gear, `spots.gear`, over a loud deep body, a sine dropping fast with its octave, driven into a clip so small speakers carry it). The overhead crane (`hangar-crane.js`) is heard from its trolley, whose state it reads live (`watchCrane`): its travel drive's whine climbing with its speed over its gearbox's hum and its wheels' rumble on the rails, and its hoist's higher whine and hum; and its cues (`craneCue`): the brakes' clunk and click as a motion starts and stops (`brake`), the sling's chains clinking as it is hooked on or off (`rattle`), the ropes' creak as they take the crate's weight (`lift`), the crate's thud on its stand (`land`) and its two-note warning horn (`horn`), each placed where it is apart from the motors (`cuePan`); an unknown cue throws; and its travel alarm, beeps scheduled a little ahead while the state's `alarm` holds. The control room's fire (`hangar-room-fire.js`) is heard from the room, read live (`watchRoom`): its roar and a crackle of pops as big as the fire, and the extinguisher's hiss; and its cues (`roomCue`, on a panner of their own): the bolt's `strike`, the fire going `out` and the extinguisher's `clank` on its bracket. Its Sound button (`hangar-mute`) opens the hangar's sound panel (on or off, the volume and the pad's vibration); the banner's speaker (or M while voice is off) mutes it with every other environment sound |
| `hangar-fire.js` | The Lightning Jet Hangar's engine static fire test, from its concept: the booth `hangar-models.js` builds beside the runway behind the Jet's left engine, its back to the Jet, and the test its big red button starts (`AT`: armed, spool-up, fire, warning, boom, the presser out, the result, then a 20 s cooldown before it is ready again). `create({ root, jet, nozzles, fx, audio, hold, toast, busy })` builds it once a visit from fixed pools, its cables' droops hung on the Jet's node: the booth's moving parts, a plume at each nozzle's exit (`jetModels.EXHAUST`: a white core in a blue mantle inside the orange envelope, shock diamonds, the throat's glow, the heat's rings), the blast's fireballs, smoke, shockwave ring and shell, and the overlay's vignette. `press` (Space beside the button through `useNear`, the act button reading DON'T PRESS, or a tap there) starts it, holding the presser's controls and the view (`hold`, the scene's `setActive` and `setExternalControl`) and schedules its sound (`audio.test`); the view glides to the show (`LAYOUT.fireTest.view`). `update(dt, driven)`, after the crew moves, runs the lights, screen (at rest turning through its ready, pre-fire checklist, days-since-the-last-explosion and safety-briefing pictures, a blank flicker between, `IDLE`), countdown and meter lamps, the plumes (the Jet shuddering, dust blown aft, the malfunction coughing and spitting sparks), the blast behind the nozzle nearest the booth (sparks and iron from the scene's particles, the nozzles sooted), and the knockdown: the presser thrown backward by the crew's own throw (`leap.thrown`), laid on their back by a root quaternion with the lowest part set on the floor, the stun birds circling and soot on their parts fading, smoking; at the result the controls and the view come back under shut eyelids and a toast says how it went. The pad rumbles in step (`gamepad.rumble`). `view(camera)` after the pilot and `unshake(camera)` before it shake the view; `lights(Lt, slot)` writes the booth's, the plumes' and the flash's lights first after the fixed ones and returns how many; `overlay(ctx2d)` draws the flash, the dimmed view and the eyelids; `dispose` stands the presser up and hands the controls back; `t` (the test's time, -1 between tests) and `blast` (where it goes off) are what Kortik's Sis reads to panic. Between tests the engines burn as `engines(thrust, heat, each)` last asked (`each` a throat's own share, in the nozzles' order; cold unless an anomaly drives them), and while `busy()` says something else has them (an anomaly playing) the button only buzzes no (`hangarAudio.denied`); the Jet shudders on its gear only while the plumes burn, set back once, so between tests its position is left to whatever else moves it. Allocates nothing a frame. Only the presser and Kortik's Sis are knocked down; others seeing it is a later room message |
| `hangar-terminals.js` | The Lightning Jet Hangar's terminals on stands by the runway and the film show they start, on `hangar-fire.js`'s pattern, allocating nothing a frame. Each terminal turns through its pages (`TURNS`, `HOLD` s each with a blink of blank screen between, the three a page apart; a page's frames at its own rate, `PAGES`), painted once a page life and laid on its screen (`hangarModels.KIOSK`): the Jet v2 build board by the stage `jetModels` has reached, the channels routing a payment through the Jet, a rebalance evening their liquidity out, the forwarding log scrolling, the chief engineer's mood (Kortik's Sis's `mood`, read live) and the film archive's DISPLAY card; the dock's terminal shows the Jet's blueprint, its systems and its launch countdown instead of the build board, the rebalance and the log. The crew's desks (`hangarModels.DESK`) show their own pictures, a desk's two monitors a pair that goes together (`PAIRS`: a channel's liquidity and the rebalancer's runs, the fees and the fee policy, HTLCs and the forwarding log, the peer map and the routing's success, the mempool and the new channels, the routing and the fees; the Jet's engines side by side, its wiring and a unit's gauges, the static fire log and the chief engineer's orders): the front row's desks turn through the node's pairs and the back row's through the Jet's (`DESKS`), each pair held `DESK_HOLD` (20) seconds and swapped once the next has loaded, with no blank between, each desk `DESK_STEP` (1) second after the one before, so no two desks show one pair but as they change over, each under its own titles and from that desk's own numbers. Standing at a desk in front of its monitors (`DESK_REACH`), Space (the act button reading NEXT SCREEN) turns one of the two, picked at random each press, on to its next picture after a blink, with a click (`turnMonitor`), so the pair need not go together after; a tap on a monitor from there turns that one (`tapMonitor`). Space beside a terminal (`useNear`, the act button reading DISPLAY, or STOP while it runs, from `actLabel`) or a tap on it from there (`press`) starts the show (`SHOW` holds its beats): the screen's case winds down from where it hangs stowed out of the hangar's sign's way, the screen unrolls, the projector strikes its lamp and its reels turn, and the film (`reel(reading)`, from `jetFeed.reading()`) runs: the countdown leader with its 2-pop, the title card, the plebnet era, the month the repository began, a card for each year and that year's releases oldest first, each a sepia picture of what it brought (`KINDS`, by the words of its tag, with its line) under its date and version, a card for the quiet years between, Jet v2 ahead and THE END; then the tail flaps through the gate on bare light, the lamp goes out, the motor runs down, the screen rolls back up and its case winds back up. The cards are black in a silent film's ornate border, each picture spliced in with a flash of bare light and a clunk; all of it flickers (a black sheet over it at a varying `smokeOpacity`), weaves in the gate (`WEAVE`), runs with grain from a fixed set of frames, and lights the floor in front of it (`lights(Lt, slot)`, after the fire test's). Frames are painted one a frame a little ahead of the one showing and kept for the page life. Pressing again stops it, and every terminal says NOW SHOWING meanwhile. Its sound is `hangarAudio`'s `projector`, `clunk` and `roll`. `debug` (`__ooga.hangar.terminals`) presses, skips to a frame (`skip`), sets the terminals' clock and says the phase, the frame and the film |
| `hangar-anomaly.js` | The Lightning Jet Hangar's engine anomalies: now and then, with nobody at the booth, the Jet acts up on its own, one at a time, at random, a minute to five apart (`GAP`, from the end of one to the start of the next; each page its own), each kind taking its turn from a bag shuffled in place so none comes round again before the rest have played. Never during the static fire test or for `AFTER_FIRE` seconds after it hands the controls back, and while one plays the test's button only buzzes no (the test's `busy`). The failed start (`MISFIRE`): the hall's lights dimming (`dim`, which the scene applies to its fixed lights, lanterns, floor lamps, glass and neon, and to its own light half as much) and an electrical hum rising, blue arcs crackling round the nozzles' rims (a fixed pool of `ARCS` bolt nodes, blue sparks off each, a light while one strikes); three tries at turning the engines over, the throats glowing hotter each time and dying in a sputter, the last coughing a short flame; then a dead stop, the lights back, a wisp of smoke and the metal ticking as it cools. The phantom revs (`REVS`): no lights or arcs, the two engines revving by turns from a script, back and forth as if talking, each throat glowing as it revs and the Jet's node rocking toward it and shuddering, put back at its rest pose at the end. Lightning through the Jet (`ZAP`): arcs (bigger, up to `ARCS` at once) gathering at its nose and crawling over its top and down its flanks to the tail (`onJet`, `onFlank`, from `jetModels.columns`), the hall browning out, the runway's marker lamps' pulse running the wrong way (`rabbit`), its gold trim and glass lighting up as they go (`jetModels.jet(stage, true)` swapped onto the Jet's node, its `glow` rising); then a crack and a flash, the Jet plain again and Kortik's Sis zapped if she stands near it (`sis.zap`); now and then (`ZAP.room`, or `debug.play("lightning", true)`) the crack ends in a bolt leaping off the Jet's back across the hall to a room's console a kinked length at a time (`JUMP`, its bolts turned along the path by quaternion), the room picked at random as it cracks (`room.pick`: the control room or one of the crew's rooms on the right wall; `debug.play("lightning", true, i)` picks room `i`), which catches fire there (`room.ignite`, `hangar-room-fire.js`), never while one already burns. The blackout (`BLACKOUT`): a flicker and every light dead, the hall's own light nearly gone (`ambient`), the hall's hum cut (`hangarAudio.power`), only the nozzles glowing in the dark like eyes, breathing and blinking once, and Kortik's Sis's eyes glowing too (`sis.dark`); then the banks clunking back on one by one. The lowrider (`LOWRIDER`): the Jet bouncing to a beat, its nose lifting on each about its main gear (the node raised so that gear stays on the floor) and its nose gear banging back down (`slam`) with a bounce, harder and softer by turns; it starts only with nobody on the Jet (`riders()`, `standsOn`) and stops at once at its rest pose if someone gets on or a frame runs longer than `HITCH`, and Kortik's Sis bobs along (`sis.groove`). The hangar shuffle (`SHUFFLE`): the brakes letting go and the Jet shifting in its bay from keyframes, a lurch toward its nose and back, a sway from wing to wing pivoting on the trestle under the wing that stays down (`pad`), a last lurch, the engines' short bursts, settling exactly on its marks; at most `along` (0.2 m) and `roll` (0.0105 rad), so it stays in its bay clear of the gangway, the booth's cables riding with it. It starts only with everything clear of where it may reach (`near`, `blocked`: any walker within `SAFE.margin` of the Jet's cells at that walker's height, grown by the motion, or of its nozzles) and fails closed, ending at once at its rest pose, if anything comes that near or a frame runs longer than `HITCH`; while it plays the hangar's own walkers keep out (`keepsOut`), and Kortik's Sis yells at it to stay put. While the blackout has the power off, from its kill until its last bank is back, `powered` is false, and the overhead crane stops (`hangar-crane.js`). Each drives the engines through the static fire's own (`fire.engines`, the revs a throat at a time), their sound is `hangarAudio.misfire`, `revs`, `zap`, `blackout`, `lowrider` and `shuffle`, and Kortik's Sis reacts at the first try (`sis.spook`) and hears when the engines die (`sis.settle`). `lights(Lt, slot)` writes each hot throat's and an arc's light after the test's; `dispose` ends one cut short. Off on a test page (`nosim`) unless `anomaly=` asks; `debug` (`__ooga.hangar.anomaly`) plays one by name (`play`) and says which plays, how far in, the wait, `dim` and `heat`. Allocates nothing a frame |
| `hangar-crane.js` | The Lightning Jet Hangar's overhead crane at work: LJ-02 (`hangarModels.CRANE`, `crane`) moves its crate between the two laydown stands (`CRANE.stands`, each in a painted keep-out square that walkers stay off, as high as a crate on it) as an operator runs a crane, one motion at a time: over the crate, the hook down to just above it, the rigger hooking the sling on (`RIG`), the hook rising slowly as the legs draw tight, held taut (`TAKE`), the crate lifted off at a creep (`CREEP`) and on to its carrying height (`CRANE.carry`), across the hall only at the door end (`CRANE.park.z`) and down it only over the crew's side, so the load never passes over the Jet, waiting over the other stand for the swing to settle (`SETTLE` at most), crept onto it, the hook lowered on until the sling lies slack (its taut and slack meshes swapped, the taut one shortened as the hook comes down to the crate), unhooked, and the hook up and home to park; a while later it fetches the crate back (`WAIT`, sooner the first time a visit). Each motion eases up to speed and down (`SPEED`, `ACCEL`), and the load swings as a pendulum from the trolley's sheaves as the trolley and the bridge speed up and slow down, damped, steadied by hand near a stand (`STEADY`). It works safely: while a job runs the warning beacons on the bridge's ends turn, each flashing as its reflector passes the view (`BEACON`), and its blue safety spot follows the hook along the floor (`SPOT`, `beam`: its place under the hook, the carried crate's bottom and its level, which the scene turns into the renderer's second spot light, or on Canvas 2D its `spot` picture); it sounds its horn before it sets off and its travel alarm beeps while the bridge or the trolley moves; in a blackout its brakes set at once and everything stops where it is (`BRAKE`), the load swinging on and the beacons and spot dark, until the power is back; and it never moves its load over anyone: while anything that walks stands within `HOLD.reach` of the hook's line, where the load hangs or where it would come to rest if it stopped now, lower than the load's bottom, it stops and holds, its horn sounding every `HORN.every` seconds, and carries on once they have been clear `HOLD.clear` seconds. `create({ C, nodes, root, cue, powered, under })` takes the crane's nodes (its `beacons` and the floor's `spot` among them), the scene's root (the crate is moved between it and the hook), whether the hall has power, whether anything that walks stands under the load (`under(ax, az, bx, bz, reach, bottom)`) and where its cues go (`brake`, `rattle`, `lift`, `land`, `horn`), and returns `update(dt, eye)` (the view's position, for the beacons' flash), `sound` (its trolley's place, how fast its travel and hoist run, 0 to 1, and whether its travel alarm sounds, for `hangarAudio.watchCrane`) and `debug` (`__ooga.hangar.crane`: the phase, the step, the jobs done, whether it is tied or loaded, where it stands, the rope, the swing, the spot's level, whether it has power, whether and how often it has held, and `run()`, which starts the next job now). Allocates nothing a frame |
| `hangar-keypad.js` | The Lightning Jet Hangar's door keypads: the control room's and each crew room's on the right wall, each a node of its own on `hangarModels.keypad()` where its room's `keypad` says, and the command room's security panel by its door, whose own lit parts and their red twin it brings (`commandRoom().keypad`: `body`, `denied`, and `bias` for the Canvas 2D sort). Beside one, in front of it, the act button reads KEYPAD; Space there, the act button or a tap on it opens it on screen (`#hangar-keypad`, a `<dialog>` that keeps the focus while open, so the director and Tess leave its keys alone; on a wide screen on the side away from the keypad on the wall, `across`): the room's name, a screen and twelve keys, tapped, clicked or typed (0–9, Backspace, Delete, Enter; Escape closes it), each with its beep (`hangarAudio.beep` at its own pitch). Every code is refused: ENT checks it a moment, then the screen says ACCESS DENIED with `hangarAudio.denied`, the panel shakes (not under reduced motion) and the keypad on the wall turns red (its `denied` twin); three refusals in a row lock that keypad out for `LOCK` seconds, counting down, red until it lets go, open or not. While it is open the Ooga stands still (`hold`), and Tess goes back to work and holds her greeting (her `busy`). `create({ audio, hud, hold, across, pads })`, `debug` (`__ooga.hangar.keypad`: `press`, `key`, `close`, the state, code, screen, refusals and lockouts). Allocates nothing a frame |
| `hangar-meltdown.js` | A worker in the Lightning Jet Hangar who has had enough: now and then (`GAP`, from the end of one to the start of the next, each page its own), with nothing else going on in the hall (`busy`: no anomaly, static fire, room fire or Kortik's Sis's rampage, Tess not lent), the gorilla working one of the crew's rooms on the right wall, picked at random but never the last one's, pounds his console and yells, runs out through the room's door (`hangarModels.roomDoor()` on a node of the scene's, banging open), along the right catwalk and down its stair to the Jet, and pounds on it with both fists at a spot picked at random, never the last one's (`SPOTS`: the nose gear's leg, a main gear's leg or a wing's trestle, each with its own way over the floor from the stair's foot, round the nose and under the gangway for the left side, where Sis squares up and where Tess hangs clear of the wings and the gangway), yelling, each blow a `clang` and sparks. Kortik's Sis, if she is free, notices (`NOTICE`), runs at him screaming and squares up to him on his far side (`sis.brawl`, the spot's `sis`); they trade a line each (`FACEOFF`), then fight in a cartoon dust cloud (`CLOUD`: the island's `hubModels.puff` balls swirling and pulsing round them, both hidden inside, her hammer, a hard hat and fists flying out of it, stars, POW!s and grawlix, blows by `boot`, `poke` and `clang`, yells in both their voices) until Tess, if she is free (`tess.free`: not lent or touring), flies over (`tess.lend`, the scene's `tessFor` saying who has her) and breaks it up with a beam on each of them; with nobody to come, the cloud blows itself out (`FIGHT_MAX`). Dazed, they hear her out (`SPLIT`), Sis blames him and goes back to her routine, and he walks back to his post, the door swinging shut behind him. With Sis busy, he pounds a while, calms down and goes back. His lines are `LINES`, said through `fx.say` and `hangarAudio.voice`'s `worker`. It holds him while it runs (`holding`, which the scene leaves out of the crew's update), posing him itself as the room fire does, his feet at the catwalks' height in the room and on the catwalk and where `supportAt` has them on the stair and the floor; the visitor's Ooga in his way stops him (`blocker`), and he yells at it. The anomalies wait for it (their `busy`) and the static fire's button buzzes no meanwhile. Not on a test page unless `meltdown=N` asks (debug), which also sets the wait, s. `create({ root, fx, audio, rooms, sis, tess, busy, blocker, auto, gap })`, `debug` (`__ooga.hangar.meltdown`: `play(room, spot)`, the spots, the beat, the room, where he is, the door, whether Sis came and Tess is lent). Allocates nothing a frame |
| `hangar-room-fire.js` | A fire in one of the Lightning Jet Hangar's rooms on the catwalks' level and its operator putting it out. The lightning anomaly lights it now and then as its bolt lands on the console of the room it picked (`pick`, `ignite`, `at`); flames of the arcade's smooth blaze rise off the console with embers, smoke and a flickering orange light (`FIRE`), and the operator, the gorilla at that console (`holding`, which the scene leaves out of the crew's update while it runs), goes through his routine on its beats (`AT`): jumping back with a shout, walking to the extinguisher on the room's end wall and taking it off its bracket, spraying the fire from its side (`SPRAY`, a white mist, the fire dying in a white cloud), "...all clear.", hanging the extinguisher back and walking home, posed by the routine itself (his root, chest, legs and arms, `WALKER`) and handed back where he stood (`place`). Its places are each room's `spots` (`hangarModels.controlRoom()`'s and `sideRooms()`'s, the right-wall rooms' at their far ends behind the glass), an extinguisher on each room's bracket (`hangarModels.extinguisher`, its nozzle `NOZZLE`); its sound `hangarAudio.watchRoom` and `roomCue`. `create({ root, fx, audio, rooms })` (each room its `spots` and its operator, `gorilla`: the control room's the crew's last post, the right-wall rooms' the posts before it) returns `pick(i)`, `ignite`, `update(dt)`, `lights(Lt, slot)` (the fire's light and the strike's flash, after the anomalies'), `holding`, `burning`, `at`, `dispose` and `debug` (`__ooga.hangar.roomFire`: `ignite(i)`, the room, the time, the fire's level, where the operator and the extinguisher are). Allocates nothing a frame |
| `hangar-shimmer.js` | A faint shape in the Lightning Jet Hangar's air: now and then a faint cyan disturbance in the air where the visitor is looking gathers into the outline of a standing figure made of light, a little over three metres tall, and comes apart again, for 8 to 12 seconds (`SHOW`): a slim figure three-quarters on, featureless, its hands rounded (`OUTLINE`), traced in a thin glowing line, white at its middle and cyan at its edges, with sparks along it, in runs that glint brighter and dimmer (`EDGE`; the line never thinner on screen than `thin` a metre off); glitter within it, thickest toward its edges and over its upper body (`GLITTER`); from the thighs down it comes apart into a stream of sparks falling to the floor (`FALL`); short bars of light tearing across it, mostly over its head and shoulders, some slipping a thin band of it sideways (`TEARS`); motes drifting round it (`MOTES`); and a faint column of light it stands in (`SHAFT`); facing the view, one way or mirrored. It gathers head first from a loose column of sparks, its line showing as each part arrives; as it dissolves its line crumbles a run at a time and its sparks drift apart, rising, and streaks of light stand where it stood, shimmering, with motes drifting in them, a faint afterimage for 15 to 20 seconds (`RESIDUE`). A very faint breath of sound comes with the figure, not the afterimage (`hangarAudio.glint`); under reduced motion nothing gathers, drifts or falls, its sparks glint slowly and no bars tear across it. It lights nothing. Now and then, with the visitor's own Ooga within 5 to 6.5 m of it while it holds (`NEAR`: most showings, not all; after a moment; at most twice, the second weaker), a share of its outline's runs hold steady at their usual glint and nearly still a second or two, so it reads steadier rather than brighter, the outline brightens a few per cent and fewer bars tear across it, then it goes on as before, even if the Ooga stays; closer still, within 2 to 3 m (`CATCH`: about half the showings, once at most), after a moment a few other runs catch whole, still and a quarter brighter for a fraction of a second, then break apart over 0.3 s, dimming and straying, sometimes with a bar tearing across as they do; and its afterimage stirs a little as the Ooga passes within 3 to 4.5 m of where it stood (`STIR`: most showings), its streaks and motes up to a third brighter and shimmering quicker, the motes pushed a little aside, away from the Ooga, settling again once it has gone by, the figure never coming back; it lasts no longer for any of it and answers nobody else. Tess, if she is near it, in sight of it and free to notice it, watches it (`hangarGuide.unsettled`), and nobody else in the hangar reacts; each page has its own, nothing of it sent. The first comes 30 to 60 s into a visit and each next 3 to 5 minutes after the last has gone (`GAP`); once due it waits for a good moment and place: none of the hangar's other events for `CALM` s (`busy`), the visitor's Ooga on foot, no dialog open, and the view not turning faster than `CHOOSE.turn`, and a spot in good view (`pick`: floor spots laid out once a page with room for it and nothing over it, from the floor's middle out to the launch tunnel's mouth, 7 to 22 m off, 16 on a coarse pointer (`VIEW.coarse`), on screen (clear of the buttons and sticks on a coarse pointer), not behind the visitor's own Ooga, in sight past walls, boxes and the Jet, and clear of the guide and anything that walks), looking again every `LOOK` s, a few frames to a search (`SCAN`), and further out and across, and less strictly, once it has looked for `VIEW.relax` s; of the spots that will do it takes one at random, weighted toward those a visitor would notice (off the screen's middle but not at its edge, nearer, not over the launch tunnel's neon) and away from the last few it came to this page life (`CHOOSE`), so it turns up somewhere new each time. While it shows, for `AFTER` s after and for up to `CLAIM` s while one is due (`holding`) the engine anomalies and a worker's meltdown wait; anything else starting, the tab hiding or leaving ends it at once. Soft pictures painted once a page (a spark, a line, a bar, a glow) drawn additively and turned to the view in four fixed-capacity instanced batches, written in place; the Canvas 2D renderer, whose batches draw no such pictures, gets fewer plain glowing diamonds in their place, without the glow. `dissolveSoon(lead)` has it start dissolving `lead` s on, leaving only a short afterimage (the network's start, `hangar-network.js`). Not on a test page unless `shimmer=` asks (debug). `debug` (`__ooga.hangar.shimmer`) plays one now (`play(spot)`), ends it and says its state, place, timeline, the wait, the calm, how many pictures it draws and how loud it is. Allocates nothing a frame |
| `sis-models.js`, `sis.js` | Kortik's Sis, the Lightning Jet Hangar's chief engineer, from her concept (`untracked/kortiks-sis-plan.md` holds the steps). `sis-models.js` is her rig and her hammer, built as her brother's (`kortik-models.js`): voxels `U` a little under five centimetres, a big head on a sturdy body, arms jointed at the elbow and legs at the knee, the root at her hip (`HIP` above the floor). His heavy slanting brows on her own face: big eyes whose whites glow a little so they read under the brim, a small nose, a frown or a yell; messy long brown hair framing it and down her back; the yellow hard hat with the big blue goggles on its brim (or down over her eyes); blue Lightning overalls open over a white shirt with the bolt on her chest, rolled sleeves and cuffs, leather gloves, laced boots, the belt with its bolt buckle, a pouch, a wrench and pliers. Her head's faces (`heads`: open, blink, yelling, goggles down, each furious red) cast no shadow, so the brim never darkens her eyes. `hammer` is the oversized hammer, turned and bevelled, the bolt on both sides of its head, its grip at the origin along +y. `NAME` (`sis/hangar`) is a name no login can have. `sis.js` runs her, always on the move all round the Jet between her spots (`LAYOUT.sis.spots`, each with what she does there, the point she looks at and, up the stair, the height she stands at), on the floor on both its sides and up its stair along the gangway by the cockpit, walking or often running, never to the spot she just left: fixing the Jet with her hammer (swung up over her head and down onto the gear, a wing, the nose gear, an engine or the canopy, sparks from the scene's particles and `hangarAudio.clang` each hit), inspecting with her goggles down, admiring the Jet then going red and shaking her fist at it, glaring at the booth or the door, pacing and muttering, and now and then running at the visitor's Ooga wherever it stands (`VISIT`), red, fist up, screaming a `charge` line as she sets off and yelling a `caught` one when she gets there. Her lines (`LINES`, by pool, each with its `STYLE` of `hangarAudio.voice`) are the spec's and a few rare ones (`RARE`), every swear a grawlix her voice bleeps, a yell now and then screamed; a quiet is kept between ambient lines, her mouth runs as long as her voice sounds, and her boots thud each step (`hangarAudio.boot`). She walks a grid of her part of the hall built once a visit, each cell with two places to stand, the floor's (or a stair's foot) and one up the Jet's stair or along its gangway where those stand over it (`UP`, under the catwalks), each open from `hangarModels.clearAt` and `supportAt` (asked as a step from outside the hall, so nothing blocked reads open, the floor shut under a stair lower than she is), off the runway, out of the booth's box and clear of the crew's posts; each place links to its neighbours' nearer her height (across the flat floor by the cells alone, on the stair and the gangway by `walkable`, `RISE` at most), and a trip is A* over those links in preallocated arrays, pulled straight where `walkable` allows from the height of the last waypoint and the walk ends on the next place's own level (so a walk across the floor never stands in for the climb to the gangway over it), her feet on `supportAt` as she goes. The visitor's Ooga stops her and she goes elsewhere, as do the circles the scene hands her as `blocks` (the forklift's); she is a walk block for the Oogas (`actor`, joined to the scene's `outsideActors`). When the static fire starts (`create`'s `fire`, the scene's `hangar-fire.js`), whatever she is doing she yells WHO PRESSED THAT?!, and near the blast runs flailing for the nearer of `FLEE` (beside the booth, by the far wall's crates: both kept in frame by the test's show view); there, or wherever she stands when too far off to run, she crouches facing the engines with her arms over her head, shouting at the malfunction. The blast throws her flat on her back, slid a little away from it and out cold, sooted (`scorch` on her parts, fading as she smokes); at the result she gets up red-faced and shakes her fist at the visitor's Ooga (or the booth), blaming everyone, then goes to hammer the engine that did it (`PANIC` holds the timings). When the Jet acts up on its own (`spook(x, z)`, from `hangar-anomaly.js`) she drops what she is doing and reacts, a different way each time (`SCARES`, never the same twice running; `SPOOK` holds the timings): she jumps out of her skin and screams at the engines; hides behind the nearest stack of crates from them and peeks over it until they die (`settle`), then whispers; charges an engine with the hammer up and lowers it slowly once it has died; slaps every switch on the booth and protests she touched nothing; yells through the door at her brother, who yells back that it wasn't him (`KORTIK_ANSWER`); creeps up goggles down and prods the Jet at arm's length; or freezes, turns her head very slowly, whispers "...nope." and walks off the other way. Those that walk go to a place of their own (`scareSpot`), then she goes back to her routine; smashing the Jet or storming off she only jumps or yells at her brother (`IN_PLACE`); it ends her quiet moment, and not while the static fire has her. When lightning over the Jet cracks (`zap(x, z0, z1)`), she is zapped if she stands within `ZAP.reach` of its length: stiff and shaking with sparks off her, then dazed, sooted and smoking, muttering. In a blackout her eyes glow (`dark(k)`, her head's `glow`, `EYES`). When it shifts in its bay she jumps and yells at it where she stands, "HEY! STAY WHERE I PUT YOU!" (`spook(x, z, "stay")`). Walking or pacing she keeps out of where `avoid` says (the shuffling Jet's reach). When the Jet bounces to a beat (`groove(beat)`) she bobs along before she catches herself, red: "I DID NOT JUST DO THAT." (`GROOVE`; not while she smashes or storms off). `liveGeometry` keeps her other faces on the GPU, and `mood` says how cross she is (0 to 4 by her anger, 5 while she smashes), which the hangar's terminals show, and `rampaging` whether she smashes the Jet. When a worker pounds the Jet (`brawl(at, stand)`, `hangar-meltdown.js`) she drops what she is doing, runs at him red with the hammer up screaming a `brawl` line, and stands squared up to him (`brawling`) until let go (`brawl(null)`), when she blames him (`started`) and goes back to her routine; pokes and her brother's quarrel wait meanwhile. While Tess buzzes round her in a rampage (`distract(at)`, Tess's position read live, null once she goes) she swings slower and every few seconds stops to turn on her, red, fist up, yelling a `tess` line (`DISTRACT`). The poke game: Space beside her (`useNear`, the act button reading POKE from `actLabel`), a pad's A, or a tap on her from within `POKE.reach` (`poke`) turns her on whoever did it, a boink (`hangarAudio.poke`) and a pulse in the pad, angrier a poke (`anger`, cooling a level every `POKE.calm` s left alone): a startled hop and WHAT?!, a wagging finger and STOP POKING ME!, a stomp and DO I LOOK LIKE A #@$%ING BUTTON?!, the hammer raised over her head and I'M GONNA REBALANCE YOUR FACE!, now and then chasing the poker a few seconds on her own ground (`chase`). The next poke sets her smashing the Jet: running spot to spot round it (its `fix` spots, the cockpit's up the stair among them) and swinging fast, each blow throwing sparks, iron and the Jet's paint from the scene's particles, a puff of smoke, a clang twice as loud and a thump in the pad, rocking the Jet on its gear (its node's rotation, `jet`) and leaving a smoking dent (`MARKS`, each mended after `MARK_LIFE` s). Then each poke counts toward stopping her (the tally at her feet, `overlay`, STOP HER! over `RAMPAGE.stop` pips, draining between pokes), and a second at one spot sends her running to the one of two farther from the poker, so the visitor has to chase her; left alone she keeps at it until she calls it fixed (`RAMPAGE.max`). The last poke melts her down: a tantrum, GO POKE MY BROTHER! HE'S NEXT DOOR!, a toast, and she storms off to sulk red, arms folded, by the door, telling anyone who pokes her to leave her alone; she won't go off again for `POKE.cooldown` s. The static fire ends it all. Now and then, while she is free, her brother's shout comes through the hangar's door from the Factory (`kortik.RIVALRY`, his line and her answer), in a bubble over the door and on her voice lowered and muffled (`hangarAudio.voice`'s `kortik`), and she turns to the door, cups her hands at her mouth and shouts back (`QUARREL`). And rarely (`HUSH`), at a spot where she would hammer, she lays the hammer on the floor at her feet instead (`props`, which the scene stands in the hall) and looks the Jet over gently, a hand on it, whispering "Huh... maybe this thing might actually work."; a warning light (`BEACON`) blinks on the Jet where she would have hammered, beeping (`hangarAudio.beep`), she jumps and screams "OH, FOR #@$%'S SAKE!", turns all round herself hunting for the hammer, snatches it up from her feet and whacks the light until it goes out. `debug` (`__ooga.hangar.sis`) sends her to a spot, running at the visitor (`charge`), pokes her or sets her smashing (`poke`, `rampage`), starts the quiet moment at a spot (`hush`) or Kortik's shout (`quarrel`), spooks her with any reaction (`spook(kind)`, `settle`, `scare`), and says what she is doing, where, how angry, which fit she is in, whether she is smashing or storming off, panicking or down |

shooting stars, and its ringed giant and red world hung past the end, turning with their moons (`SPACE`, `heavens`), hangs a cool haze down the runway (`fog`; space past the launch shield and the shield's shimmer show through it, `fogPierce`), lights the Jet with its spot from under the roof over its left flank (`KEY`, dimmed with the hall), shares the crates' lanterns' point lights by wherever it reaches a foot's breadth over them, so Oogas get up on it from crates and the like, a jump clearing 1.18 m), and by Kortik's Sis, the crew and its own drive as circles that move with it (`BLOCKS`: the crew's clearance and Sis's `blocks`); its forks a deck to walk onto or jump up on, at whatever height they stand (`forkTop`), all in `groundFor`, `walkableFor` and `flyableFor`), which an Ooga gets into with Space beside its cab (the act button reading GET IN, then GET OUT; the crew's `sitPlayer`, standing up on the side it got in from), sitting in first person looking down the forks (the pilot's `navigate`, then `enterClose`) and getting out, by Space or walking off, back into the view it got in with (`SEAT_VIEW`), which Space in the seat (the act button reading START) or a tap on its key starts (`powerOn`: the key to START and back to ON, the charge needle sweeping up from E, the speed needle reading its speed in km/h as it drives, the drive spinning up, then its lit parts and screen coming on and its beacon turning, flashing as its reflector passes the view and sweeping a light round, `POWER`), and getting out or the key switches off (`powerOff`); while it runs R raises its forks and F lowers them (`FORKS`, the keys borrowed from the view's pitch), the inner mast rising half as far and the chains running over their sheaves (`forkLife`, `layChains`), or a tap on the fork stick sends them all the way up or down (`liftTo`); while it runs the seat holds the Ooga (`lockMovement`) and A and D steer, the steering wheel turning on its column (`helm`) and the rear wheels, which steer (`STEER`, `steerLife`); and W and S drive it (`DRIVE`, `driveLife`): its front axle on along its heading, the heading turned by the rear wheels, stopped dead where its body's circles or its fork tips would not stand clear of the hall (`clearAt`) or of whoever walks it (`clearFor`), the Ooga riding in its seat with the view turning with it, and getting out beside the cab wherever it stops (`standSpot`); H or a tap on the steering wheel sounds its horn while it has power (`honk`); the controls dialog lists its driving, fork and horn controls while the visitor is seated; and whose big red button, tapped from the seat or beside the cab, flips its cover up and buzzes that it does nothing (`BUTTON_PRESS`), hires the crew (the Factory's gorillas in hard hats, in the hangar's vests, each pacing its post from `LAYOUT.crew` at its own breadth, `CREW_BODY`, only to a spot it can walk to all the way, and standing in the Oogas' way as they stand in its), paints the boards from `jetFeed.reading()` and again when it changes, opens each in the board dialog on a tap with links out to the repository and to Jet 2.0's `v2` branch, answers an Ooga at the hold-short bar once an approach, runs the static fire station (`hangar-fire.js`: Space beside its button, the act button reading DON'T PRESS, or a tap from there), lets the Jet act up on its own now and then (`hangar-anomaly.js`, dimming the hall's lights (its fixed ones, the lanterns, the glass, the neon, the floor lamps, the runway's markers, the door's glow and the booth's light) and its own light as it says (`dim`, `ambient`), running the runway's marker lamps the wrong way (`rabbit`), told whether any Ooga stands on the Jet (`jetRidden`) and whether anything that walks the hall comes near it (`nearJet`: the driven Ooga, the other players' Oogas where shown and where the room last put them, Kortik's Sis, the crew and the forklift), the crew and Sis keeping out of the shuffling Jet's reach, and keeping its lit Jet on the GPU), the terminals and the film show (`hangar-terminals.js`: Space beside a terminal, the act button reading DISPLAY, or a tap from there), lets Kortik's Sis roam all round the Jet, poked from beside her or with a tap within reach (`sis.js`, her tally drawn in the overlay), has Tess greet whoever walks in, show them round and answer their questions about the Jet (`hangar-guide.js`, her light after the film's), and lends her to distract Sis while she smashes the Jet (`sis.distract`) and to the faint shape in the air, which she glances at, looks over or is puzzled by (`hangarGuide.unsettled`), and is walked into through the door's shield from the hall and left by it (the walk taking the Ooga through either way as the Factory's gate does, `factoryWindow.GLIDE`, `crossing` and `arrival`: down the door's middle, a beat in the shield, an instant switch under the last frame of the place left, and on to where an arrival stands, at the landing's front edge with the view behind it framed by the screen's shape (`ARRIVAL` on a wide screen, `ARRIVAL_TALL` turned to the Jet on a tall one, `arrival()`), as a direct arrival's is; the door answering whoever comes near as ₿IFRÖST's windows do: its arch's glow brightening, its light swelling and its field humming faster, `DOOR_*`), Escape or Leave, back onto the walkway at the door as the same Ooga, in voice zone `factory.hangar`. On the Factory's side (`factory-models.js`, the door's geometry in `factory-scene-models.js`), only while the scene is registered (`hangarEntry`, decided once a page): `LAYOUT.walkLeft`, the walkway straight from the balcony's left side along the front wall, the front wall set back over it (`HANGAR_SET_BACK`), to the landing before the door (`LAYOUT.hangarLanding`: a deck as wide as the door's frame, railed but where the walkway arrives and along the door, a lantern post at each outer corner, dressed with hazard stripes across the threshold, iron-banded crates and a cable drum (`hangarLanding`, `HANGAR_LANDING`), and the door's posts and arch legs in walkers' way); `LAYOUT.hangar`, `hangarDoor` and `HANGAR_DOOR`, a peer tunnel's arch in the hall's cyan with the hangar's sign, a planked passage and a glimpse of the runway (`glimpse`) behind the hangar itself through the shield (`hangar-window.js`, WebGL), and its lamps (`hangarLamps`), the door's arch brightening and its field humming faster as someone comes near, as the hangar's own door does (the scene's `DOOR_*`); and the front-left outcrop lowered to two tiers under the walkway (`OUTCROPS_OPEN`) |
| `bifrost-models.js`, `bifrost-gate.js`, `bifrost-isle.js`, `bifrost-heimdall.js`, `bifrost-window.js`, `scene-bifrost.js` | ₿IFRÖST: the chamber's geometry and its `WINDOWS` (a travel window per open world, a mirror for the rest), with the night sky (`sky`: stars that twinkle a group at a time, the big ones in halos of their colour, glints, the galaxy band's soft clouds, shooting stars, and planets that turn under glass shells of day and night that stay toward the sun, with their ring bands and moons going round; on Canvas 2D the planets stay still) and its ₿ ship (`starship`), the mechanism and the kit the others share (`facing`, `archOutline`, `word`, `fieldSparkles`); the islet's gate and its field, the kit of rune banners, lanterns and braziers, and `landmark()`, the ₿IFRÖST arch that stands in the old gate's place over the north pass while the game is open; the islet, its terraces and the crystal bridge (`spot`, `site`, `update`); Heimdall; the relief window into the chamber through the field, which draws `gateWindow` (WebGL only); and the chamber scene, which takes one picture of each open world a page for its window (the poker room's once for each cave theme it shows), charges its mechanism in the colour of the window it answers, sails the ₿ ship from the ringed giant out to the glinting stars round it and back, and hangs the island's view back out, snapped by the hub as an Ooga walks in. While `BL.scenes.bifrost` is registered the hub builds the arch, the islet and Heimdall in `enter`, and the window from its frame update once the camera comes within its reach of the field (60 m). With it registered the HQ basement's Ooga Portal is retired, its controls saying DSB Land is through ₿IFRÖST, and DSB's way home leads into the chamber |
| `block-wire.js`, `block-live.js`, `block-viewer.js` (deferred), `worker/src/block.js` | Block Frenzy live rounds, following Ghost Hunter: the Worker keeps one match a room (registrations, ready, random even seats, host, watch deck; `MAX_SIDE` in `block-wire.js` is the one cap), the host's page runs `block-sim.js` and relays binary snapshots at 10 Hz and takes guest inputs at 20 Hz over the room socket. Own voice zone `block-frenzy`, blended in down the zipline. See `docs/block-frenzy.md` |
| `hockey-wire.js`, `hockey-session.js`, `hockey-players.js`, `arcade-ambience.js`, `worker/src/hockey.js`, `worker/src/hockey-limits.js` | Air hockey pilot: bounded 10 Hz host snapshots and 20 Hz guest inputs on the existing authenticated room socket; two-seat lobby, solo moving mallet, hall spectators, fixed table coordinates and shared Arcade voice/ambience. Hibernation socket attachments retain sessions; a bounded durable ledger limits admission/replay across reconnects, with no per-frame database writes. See `docs/air-hockey-pilot.md` for authority, lifecycle, measured limits and staging gates |
| `zipline-models.js`, `zipline.js` | The ziplines: a line surveyed over a scene's own queries, its Ooga hung from the bar and ridden down by `pose`, other pages' riders shown through `poseRemote`. Only Ooga Arcade uses it here: Block Frenzy's entry hall off the deck's back wall (`arcadeModels.BLOCK_HALL`, where the Rally poster used to hang) ends in an open-air cliff ledge and this line, out into blue sky and clouds; before its far end the Ooga fades to Block Frenzy's start menu (`go("block", "zipline")`, no title card), which sits up in the clouds on every arrival, and Play drops it by parachute onto the map; its return lands at the hall. Reduced motion rides slower at a steady speed. |
| `arcade-cabinets.js` | The fixed registry of twelve playable video cabinets: `BL.arcadeCabinets.entries`, `get(id)` and `FEATURE` (`arcade-cabinets-v1`). Each entry supplies its player standing anchor and heading in the hall. Loaded by the page and imported by the Worker's `protocol.js`, so exclusive seat claims and visible player anchors use the same IDs and coordinates; physical tables and out-of-order Rally are excluded. |
| `arcade-doors.js` | The double doors between the island and Ooga Arcade's hall (#554), one rig in each scene's copy of the same doorway, in the door's frame with +z out of the hall: `create` builds the frame and the leaves, which swing by themselves, always away from whoever is walking through (into the hall going in, toward the mainland coming out; `away`); `walk` keeps the doors shut while what lies beyond readies (the visitor still driving outside it, with a retry if it fails) and takes the visitor through as the Timechain Sphere's doorway does: the view swoops into the Ooga's own eyes and the other scene takes over from that same eye. Going in from the island it does so with the doors still shut (`shut`): the hall has its own copy of the cave's side of them, so the hall opens its own doors and all that comes into view is the real hall. Coming out, the hall opens them and hands over as the eye reaches them. On the far side the Ooga walks `ARRIVE` metres on, the doors closing behind the view, before the view glides back out and the controls return |
| `arcade-entrance.js` | The cave in front of the hall, under the 3 o'clock mouth's node on the island: the doors at its back (with a wall round them; the terrain carves the cave's room back to 7 m), and the four most played cabinets (`arcade-popular.js`) recessed two a side into the terrain's alcoves near the mouth, well short of the doors, (`ARCADE_CAVE` in `terrain.js`, the `slots` of `arcade-cabinets.js`, checked in the suite). Each is the game's second machine: its own seat (`<id>@out`, held at its slot, in zone `outside`), beside the hall's cabinet at once, on the same shared board, through the same `arcade-view.js` glass menu with `home: "hub"`; Cloud Hopper's stays on the island scene (hub to hub). The cave wears the hall's own crazy paving (`caveFloor`), in the colours the hall's firelight gives it (measured on screen). Past the hall's doors stands a true-to-scale copy of the cave (`caveRoom`, `caveFloor(1)`, `yard`: the same four cabinets), `HALL.exit` long, with the picture of the mainland in its mouth, where a walk in starts and a walk out ends, so either covers the real distance and sees the real place. `arrive`, `standAt`, `nearby`, `use`, `tap`, `tip`, `prompt`, `liveGeometry` and per-visit `bind`/`unbind` are the hub's only touch points. The hub photographs the cave and mainland from the doors looking out each time a visitor goes in (`world.arcadeView`), which the hall hangs past its doors (the jungle painted simply on a page that opened on the hall) |
| `arcade-popular.js` | Which four cabinets the cave shows: `pick(plays)` (most played first, ties in `DEFAULT`), `top`, `load` (one public read of `/api/arcade-plays`, at most every 30 s), `subscribe`, and `count(id)`, one anonymous `POST` when a cabinet leaves its menu for a run (`arcade-view.js`). Score records cannot say this: a machine keeps only its record and a board only its best finishes. No backend or no reading means `DEFAULT` |
| `arcade-view.js` | One page-wide cabinet visit, created by the director and shared as `ctx.cabinet`: approach the glass, show the real game menu inside it, expand the game to the viewport, then reverse the zoom and return the same Ooga to the cabinet. `begin`, `route`, `arrived`, `update`, `afterDraw` and `failed` own the visit; `active`, `phase` and `held` describe it. One captured hall image and a joystick foreground surround the existing renderer, with responsive menu layout, reduced-motion handling, cancellation and lost-seat recovery. Cloud Hopper keeps a fixed viewport aspect, letterboxed in the glass, and uniformly scales the game and captured hall together; its expansion lasts `cloudHopper.LIFT_SECONDS` and leaves scene updates running so the ascent proceeds simultaneously. Its result card hides during withdrawal. Public game links visit the cabinet first. The eleven non-Cloud-Hopper video menus show their attributed top ten here; Cloud Hopper supplies its own board. |
| `arcade-models.js`, `arcade-games.js`, `scene-arcade.js` | Ooga Arcade: the hall and its zones (`LAYOUT`) on two levels, a round `STAGE` in the middle and a `MEZZ`anine along the back wall with a stair up each side wall, built from the interior kit. It is a cave arcade the Oogas built and run, never a modern arcade with jungle trim: packed earth and flagstones under reed mats and animal hides (`hide`, `mammothPelt`, `shaggyFur`), rough timber with bark edges, stone slabs and boulders, rope-lashed bamboo, logs, dry frond roofs, bone and tusk, a coconut wherever a ball or knob would be, and cave paint in ochre, red earth, charcoal and bone (handprints, spirals, zigzags, dots, stick-figure Oogas, mammoths) on the rock, the cabinets and the machines, drawn with the file's paint helpers (`daub*` strokes, the cabinets' `pictograph`); lianas and leaves grow into each piece's own mesh through the hub's jungle kit. Light is fire: `lanterns` holds carved gourds on lianas, tiki torches on the side walls' trunks, carved tiki poles and stone fire bowls (the poles and bowls are walk blocks), each flame a node on the one `blaze` geometry that the scene flickers by scale and glow, and the fires nearest the view take the point lights left after the fixed lamps (`glowNear`); `ceiling` hangs stalactites, roots, a few teal crystals and the hero, a mammoth skeleton on lianas over the stage (`MAMMOTH_AT`); fire baskets (`SPOTS`) hang burning over the stage and the spirit rattle (`discoBall`) turns under the mammoth; the island's carved signs (`caveSign`) name the hall, the cabinets, the snack bar and the prize counter, every other sign outside the screens and scoreboards is brushed in earth paint (`brushWord`), and celebrations throw ember `spark`s. The game screens and a few glowing mushrooms and crystals are the only cool accents: no neon, chrome, glossy plastic, LED or bulb strings. No dressing covers a screen, marquee, scoreboard, glass or a game's view. Walkways are kept at least 2.3 m wide, any narrower gap is closed off, and dressing below 2.3 m (2.6 m under the mezzanine) stays inside its machine's walk block. They hold twelve playable cabinets plus the out-of-order Rally cabinet (`CABINETS`, each with its `y` and timber or `stone` body: the four with a `game` stand on the stage and play its attract loop, drawn in `DRAW` and baked into `FRAMES` cached geometries plus a PRESS START blink the scene swaps; the eight on the mezzanine loop their retro games (`RETRO`, drawn in `RETRO_DRAW` and baked into `retroFrames`, with `retroStart`'s PRESS START and the best in the signs' 3x5 alphabet, `retroLine`, so ten characters fit)), the jackpot `wheel` (a game at its station on the stage's front, `LAYOUT.wheel.play`, where the whole wheel shows over the cabinets: Space spins it; a spin costs `WHEEL_COST` tickets through `game.spendTickets`, draws its wedge with `randomInt` before the face moves and saves the win with the charge, eases out to stop on it by `wheelStop` and shows it on the boards only at the stop; `WHEEL_VALUES` pay back 85% of the cost on average with one jackpot at ten spins' worth; coming back with more tickets only lights it up), the claw machines, pinball and dart boards under the mezzanine, air hockey on the floor, the carnival machines, the snack bar, the prize counter (`PRIZES`), the game-room staff's uniforms and what they hold (`staffHat`, `staffVest`, `staffProp`: uniform C: dyed red and banana gold, curved layered cap visors, connected shoulder yokes, charcoal facings and gold piping, with the arcade's token for a badge and each role's own piece), the room's signs, posters and planters, and the sandstone scoreboards with brushed titles (`board` plus cached brushed `digit`s; both pinball machines have six digits through 999999); every ball is a coconut (`coconutBall`: husk, young, golden, cue, charred or painted); the carnival games' rules (thro-ball, hoop shot, coconut toss: allocation-free state machines over pooled nodes, each writing a `status` object for the HUD); and the arcade scene. The Ooga's outside weapons and ammunition are parked for the visit: ordinary clubs and rifles stay hidden and unavailable in the hall, including on remote visitors, while combat mouse-look remains usable. Entering the hall and returning from a game request native mouse capture once when control resumes; menus release it, and an explicit cursor release stays released. Only the Ghost Hunter arena lends a rifle and unlimited rounds; leaving Arcade restores the outside loadout and ammunition. A jump carries on in the air as on the island (`flyable` holds it off walls, rails and blocks but not over a drop). Nothing is hovered, tapped or played through rock, a wall or the deck's floor (`sightClear` over the open air of the hall, the haunted hallway and the arena), and what the overlay draws at a place (an NPC's line, a player's name, the hunt's marks) shows only in sight (`pointHidden`), nothing from outside the arena while the visitor is in it, without a test. Space/PLAY prompts and actions require grounded feet and a heading within 60 degrees of the station's playing direction; turning away or jumping removes the prompt and leaves Space available to jump. Video cabinets use `arcade-view.js` for their glass menu, fullscreen expansion and reverse return, replacing their old entry/exit animations. A physical carnival machine opens its own scene, and each game's exit comes back to its station as the same Ooga (`lastPlayer`); the prize counter spends tickets (`game.redeem`). With nobody walking in, the first Ooga the visitor may drive (`net.mayDrive`) does, and the hall reports the Ooga driven to the room (`net.setBody`), so players in it share voice. Walkers stand on `supportAt(x, z, feet)` (the cave floor, stage rings, deck, stair ramp) and are held off things by the torso, never the arms: a square `BODY` m either side of the middle, square to the hall like the walls' faces (`wallDepth`), the stair and deck `RAILS` and the `blocks` (boxes with the feet range they stand in the way of), so the crew's step slides along any face and round any corner. The stage's low decorative roots can be stepped over from every approach; cabinet collisions remain. A step never takes the body further into a wall, rail or block than it already is, so an Ooga can always walk away; a block covers what stands in the way at a walker's height (a palm's stump, a post's log, a bush's leaves), not a model's whole reach. Every machine, table, board and ride is a station that opens its game at that machine (`LAYOUT.frames` is where each is played from, shared with the game scenes), and so is every cabinet: a stage cabinet opens its Ooga game, a mezzanine one its retro game (`retro` stations on the deck at `MEZZ.y`, which the game's exit brings the Ooga back up to). Six maintenance Clankers (Agents), all walking and waiting upright between their reaching and crouching job poses, are the staff, a gamemaster, a token attendant, a mechanic, a snack runner, a prize clerk and the deck's attendant, each working a routine of upkeep jobs picked at random: routes planned by A* over a lattice of the floor (or the deck) laid round the `blocks` each visit, the job done with part rotations and its prop. They avoid each other and scenery, but do not reroute around players, greet them, call them over or react to their actions, and are nobody's walk block. The hall creates no roster Ooga crowd, strolling visitors or machine-playing bots; only live players supply visitor avatars. The separate games' enemies and air hockey's playable solo opponent remain. Every carnival machine, the counters, the totem and the muncher stay alive (`stationLife`): lit parts breathe in a chase along their row (glow on their existing lit node, never a new one), an idle machine runs its game's `attract` and now and then its `tease` (a coconut up a lane, a swish, a claw's dip, a dart, a flip, a beast's dance: the game's own pooled nodes, scoring nothing, `attract(dt, 0)` before any game starts there), a visitor within Space's reach lights the station warm; its action prompt appears only while grounded and facing it, without NPC calls or crowd reactions. The hall sounds (the carnival audio's room bed and cues placed by distance and side, `hear`), powers up in a wave on arrival from the island, hangs a hall of fame over the stage (each game's best, the roster's hashed from their names, beaten by the visitor's own), stands prizes won on a shelf by the counter, and keeps a locked back room door under the mezzanine. Off the mezzanine's right end a haunted hallway (`HAUNT`, its mouth cut in the right wall under the carved "Ghost Hunter" sign, `haunt` in arcade-scene-models.js) runs out to the ghost arena (`HAUNT.arena`): a round of dark stone the island's size, open to a starry night (the scene swaps to `NIGHT` while the view is in the hallway or the arena, its fires taking every light slot), torches all round its wall, walls of the hallway's rock with cobwebs in their bays' corners (`cobweb`), five shut steel doors (`steelDoor`, each leaf a node for later) with the hunt's enemies painted in the bays between them (`ARENA_PAINTINGS`), gargoyles on the wall top spelling FREE SAMOURAI a white letter each (`gargoyle`, `gargoyleLetter`), the hub's four repository totems with their name boards, eyes burning and glow running always, fed no data (`totemModels`, `lightTotems`) and the ghost hunt's herald over its middle (HALLOWEEN, ghost-game.js). Walkers on the deck's level go on into both (`wallDepth` with `feet`, `supportAt`'s haunt floor); a hunter of a started hunt gets the ghost blaster in hand (`arm` draws it through `pilot.weaponMode`) with unlimited rounds, and leaving the hunt restores its earlier ammunition policy and puts it away while preserving the view; the arena reports zone `ghost-arena` (Ghost Hunter's Arena, one voice region of its own whether a hunt runs or not; an older page's `arcade.arena` and `arcade-hunt` are the same region, `voice-blend.js` `ALIASES`), and in the hallway `arcade.haunt` on its Arcade half and `ghost-arena.haunt` on its arena half, flipping `HALLWAY_HOLD` past the midpoint, with a voice crossing between the two lands by where the driven Ooga stands along it (#422, `voice-blend.js`); the room hands both halves the hunt, so a spectator there watches it through the fence and hears its music and effects at the arena's equal-power share of the walk (`hearing`, `ghostAudio.level`): the snapshot-driven cues, arena players' rifle shots (relayed by the room from the arena to both hallway halves, sounded once from `net.subscribeShots`) and, for a page not in the hunt, any hunter's hurt (`hearHurts`), a catch-up frame after a gap hushed (`ghostAudio.hush`); the iron fence (`ironFence`) rises across the hallway's mouth through a hunt, the ghost's chalk ring opens to walkers while one runs, the gargoyles' eyes shine green lasers down onto the herald as it launches (`gargoyleLaser`, `shineLasers`, from the hunt's `launch`), and Exit hunt (`#ghost-exit`) walks a hunter out to the hallway |
| `retro-games.js` | Ooga Arcade's retro games, one on each mezzanine cabinet, `invaders` (Coconut Invaders, the reference), `snake`, `pong`, `stampede`, `flap`, `breaker`, `dash`, `stacker`: factories with the carnival games' interface plus `draw(ctx)`, which paints the one shared 160x120 screen canvas the carnival scene lays over the cabinet's glass, and `coach`. The shared `kit`: the screen, preset inks, the jumbotron's 5x7 font pre-rendered into an atlas in every ink (`text`, `centre`, `number` through a preallocated digit buffer), baked sprites (`sprite`, `blit`, `quarter`), a seeded `rng` per run, pooled pixel `sparks`, screen `juice` (shake, flash), cached popups (`worded`), the 1/120 s `stepper` and the scene's CRT helpers (`crt`). The file's header is the contract a game keeps |
| `scene-carnival.js`, `carnival-audio.js` | Every game in the hall that is not a stage cabinet, each a scene of its own (`skee` Thro-ball, `hoops` Hoop Shot, `shy` Coconut Toss, `claw`, `hockey` against an Ooga, `billiards`, `darts`, `pinball`, `ride`, where the visitor's Ooga (`world.pilot`, else the roster's default pick) sits the game's `rocker` at `arcadeModels.RIDE_SEATS[place]`, its arms re-aimed at the grips each frame; and the mezzanine's eight retro cabinets, `retro-games.js`, whose machine is the cabinet where it stands on the deck and whose game the scene draws over its glass every frame, `drawScreen`: the glass's corners projected, one affine map, unsmoothed pixels, a glow, scanlines and the glass's sheen, from a view square onto it, `retroView`, the screen filling most of a tall screen's width), opened at the machine the visitor walked up to (`go(kind, index)`, `ctx.place`) and leaving back to it, with a steering `aim` (drag, arrows, A and D; for the retro games also `hold` and a `steps` count of presses and drag notches, and down and up, W, S and the up and down arrows, the same way as `y` and `stepsY`) for the games that read it: the machine in the arcade hall with the hall's floor, rock, dressing and fires (the flames only sway on the GPU; four fixed lamps, all firelight, light the machine) and every other machine round it (a machine's dressing stays off its own camera's line from `eye` to `look` and out of the lower half of that view), physical games' cameras dollying in through a 3-2-1 countdown under a hand drum's roll; active video-cabinet visits skip that countdown and replay dolly in favor of the shared cabinet zoom, juice that never touches a score, timing or input (the games call the host's optional `crack` on a big moment, which splits a coconut from a fixed pool of husk halves and sprays milk, chips and leaf through the fx particle pool, punches the camera in and chants OOGA!, or throws husk chips on an ordinary score, and `flop` on a miss; the hall's hosts leave `flop` out and take only `crack`'s big moments, to cheer them), two roster Oogas (never the visitor) watching from each machine's `crowd` spots at the view's edges who lean in, cheer, clutch their heads and dance at three stars, the mobile-first HUD in `#carnival` (score, what is left and the round, the power meter, callouts, one action button), pause, and results with stars, the watchers' line on them, the best (`game.recordArcade`) and tickets (`game.addTickets`); and the pooled synthesized sound they share with the arcade hall (`cue(name, level, pan, pitch)`, `room`, `tick`). A retro cabinet sounds through that sound's chip instead (`create(true)`: two pulse channels with switchable duty, a triangle and a noise channel), its game's cue names mapped per game in `CHIP_CUES` onto `chip(name, level)` cues, and its tune (`SONGS`, sixteenth-note bars read into typed arrays at load) looping on the same four channels from Go (`music(id)`), halted with the pause (`pause`) and stopped the moment the game decides the run (the host's `over`), so the end's jingle plays alone; a cue takes a channel from the tune while it sounds, and the big ones duck it, notes already sounding included. The Sound button in each game's nav (or M while voice is off) mutes it all. All carnival scenes keep `voiceZone: "arcade.hall"`, the driven body and proximity position at their hall machine; same-hall handoffs retain the body and existing voice peers, while hockey keeps its shared seats. | `pool-layout.js` | the Mempool island's one layout, pure and loadable in Node: every dimension (a 44 m top, a 16 m lake over a transparent `membraneY`, a 24 m chamber at `FLOOR` round an 8 m shaft, the 100 m `RAMP` at a 10% grade with its recessed `RILL` following `rillRadius`, the `LEDGE` and its `DOORS`, the two `LINKS` (a mouth, a gallery cut into the cliff behind a pier, a second mouth), `NESTS` and their `SLOTS`, `CHANNELS`, the chamber's flat `STOPS`), and `body()`, the half-metre voxel grid cut from them once a page. Terraces half a metre apart: court and shore 0, lowland 0.5, forest floor and ring path 1, nests 1.5, the ridge to 4.5, channel beds -0.5. `groundAt`, `solidAt`, `covered` (rock or the lake overhead), `sightClear`, `boxSolid` and `boxClear` (exact walks of the grid, for the outlines), `onIsland`, `keptClear`, `rampPoint`. The wall behind each painting is filled in metre squares of twin tones (`PALETTE` holds every colour twice), so the mesher leaves small faces there for the Canvas 2D renderer's depth sort. Local frame: y = 0 at the bridge's top, +z toward the home island, a bearing from +z toward +x |
| `pool-models.js` | the Mempool island's geometry: `islet` (the layout's grid through `terrain.gridGeometry`, also its `cutawaySource`), `rampFloor` (smooth floors over the voxel steps of the descent, the doors and the ledge; `supportOnly` faces, or everything under a sheet counts as inside rock), `membrane` (a smooth spherical cap drawn with the translucent moving lake material, clipped at the waterline, with `twoSided` for Canvas 2D; walked on as the same cap in a thin closed shell), `chamberBacking` (a dark sheet a little inside the rock behind the chamber's floor, wall and roof, laid only where the layout is solid: a pixel that falls between two of the mesher's faces shows it instead of the sky), `crossing`, `NEST_BEDS`, `wallTorch`, `VEINS`, `roots`, cartoon flora, animals and the bridge from the hub kit, solid pieces walking on their first block build (`collisionGeometry`). `merge` does not carry `lineWidth`; set it on the merged geometry |
| `pool-water.js` | the island's water: `levelFor(vsize)` through `HYDRO.POINTS` (20 MvB normal, 60 MvB spill, 200 MvB full flood), bounded easing and stage hysteresis; lake and flood use bright translucent blue blocks, drifting foam and grain (`face.lake`, on both renderers and from below), with gentle swells that taper at the lake rim plus bounded rain/wake packets displacing vertices and their lighting normals. Nine outlets use 36-degree slots, omitting the bridge-facing slot: five reach the cliff and four pass under the raised forest through culverts, then descend the inner ramp wall. Streams fill the stepped trench as overflow rises; stream and fall share the `channelOutlet` plane; a precomputed mouth height profile limits the fall to the contiguous wet opening between the voxel banks, capped at Bifrost's 1 m width. Inner feeder streams match the width and offset of their falls. The collector’s recessed receiving pockets extend back to each inner fall using `rillInner` and shared `RILL_STATIONS`, without separate landing sheets. A 15 cm collector follows a recessed groove down the ramp’s inner edge, turns around the lower dividing wall and circles the chamber perimeter (`RILL_TAIL`, shared with the floor recess), using `lakeFlowCurve` for motion along each arc on both renderers. `lakeFlow` unfolds the animated water texture across the join; `lakeWaveEnd` tapers displacement to zero at the lip. `CHANNELS.length` varies the maximum drops; exterior falls have seven thick strands, while inner falls begin as one continuous pane and split only below 72% of the drop; staggered tips each have a pooled droplet, and `fallGap` keeps the outer ledge rock and walking floor clear. `levelAt` follows the wet footprint. The hanging bowl carries the same moving translucent material below the waterline and stays collidable when empty. `previewFill(0..200)` pins depth from the bowl bottom through full overflow (100 is half depth; around 176 begins overflow). Falls use the moving lake material; the inner outlets show thin residual streaks when dry, replacing the old decorative wall veins; a block's cube uses `SEQUENCES` pooled sets with a `QUEUE`. Never use generic `face.water` here: it is a different material and absent on Canvas 2D, which gets two-sided sheets instead |
| `pool-paintings.js` | the chamber's four wall paintings, set in the jumbotron font through `poolModels.panelFrom` with no backing, each a canvas-backed `imageSurface` repainted in place: each reading bound to its `chain.snapshot` field with its own category's freshness (LIVE under its source, STALE with its age, HELD from the last visit, NO READING; an observed zero stays zero), rebuilt only when what it prints changes. Each stop keeps a `board` for `hud.openBoard` |
| `pool-wildlife.js` | the Mempool island's animals, alive: `poolModels.beastRig` faceted rigs driven by a small state machine (idle, walk, rest and sleep, glide for climbing, the log and flight) on the gorillas' pattern; an animal's `y` is its height over `base`, the ground it stands on or the foot of what it climbed, and the hub passes `groundAt`, `spotOk`, `waterAt` for neck-deep monkey swimming and `terrainClear` for swept body checks before movement. Monkeys use `wallUp` and `wallOver` to climb blocked terrain before resuming a walk; blocked tree glides and ground settling also respect the rock. A tree reservation keeps ground tracking active until arrival; failed approaches release it, and climbing follows the trunk's lean and taper; `create` returns `list`, `update`, `startle`, `dispose` |
| `totem-models.js` | four repository carvings, each sized by `totemFeed.REPOS[i].importance`: `layout(i)` gives its face stack, crest, eyes, band heights and collision boxes; `body(i)` builds the wood, paint, stones and name board, `glyphs(i)` the runes and throat ember, `ringSector(i)` one shared painted-band facet, `featureHighlights(i)` the painted face and crest details, and `hit(i, …)` the picking ray. Their eyes burn on the arcade blaze geometry only during the day after a main/master PR merge; that merge animates the runes, eye fires, bands and painted details, and one seen during the visit triggers the cheer, speech and a notice The same kit carves Repo Island's totems from a design spelled out (`layoutOf(design, importance, girth)` (`girth` a scale across of its own, kept within 0.8 and 1.3 times the height's, so the carving is `w` across and `s` up; without it, as for the four, `w` is `s`), `carve(L, word, seed, opts)`, `glyphsOf(L, word)`, `hitOf(L, …)`): `opts.detail` scales every turned part's segments (1, the default, builds the four exactly as before, a golden the suite keeps), under 0.45 drops the small carvings and under 0.2 keeps only masks, eyes, bands and crest; `opts.board` takes a lighter board or none, and `opts.highlights` false skips the overlay. Its crests add a key, a gear, a pickaxe, a scroll, an eye, a chain, a crescent moon, a shield, a flame, a stepped tower and a star (`CRESTS`, `KINDS`) |
