A repository merged in the last day lights as the meadow's do: each chunk's lit runes (with an ember in each crest eye) merged into one mesh a brightness band, and under the island a glowing core inside each crystal of its cluster, seen through the glass (`gem(k)`, merged into two island-wide bands: bright where at least half the cluster's totems are lit, dim where fewer are, with no highlight, which would turn them gold), (within 8 h, older), glowing in its section's rhythm, and fires in the crest eyes of chunks carved finer than distant (pooled `blaze` nodes); a merge seen during the visit flares its runes and, for a visitor on the island or its bridge, says so. Runes and fires animate only within 30 m of the island. Pick owners `repo-totem` (tooltip; a tap opens the merge's PR, else the repository), `repo-sign` and `repo-bridge`. Arrival is on the lip at the bridge's end. The rock, bridge and every chunk are hub solids; zone `repos` on the island, `between` on its bridge; the hub's `repos` place (route `/repos`). Canvas 2D gets the island and bridge without signs or totems. `__ooga.repoIsland` (`stats` with its `angle`, `spot`) |
| `timechain-rooms.js`, `deferred.js`, `timechain-models.js`, `timechain-gate.js` | the Timechain Sphere's outside, always on the page: the ten rooms' registry (`ROOMS`, each its `kind`, name and radius, `lockable` for the Conference Room alone (`LOCKABLE`), `parse` validating a `roomId` 0–9 at every boundary; `layout(id)`: each room a true sphere (`R`, its centre `c`, its floor `yMin`, the screen's lower `edge`, a height or a raked hall's tilted plane, `edgeEl`, `onSphere`, `radiusAt`), the doorway in a bay where the sphere is narrowest over its height, the arrival, Exit button and, in the Conference Room, the lock at human size in every room, the raked halls' rows, seats and stage (`rake`, `floorAt`, the theater's aisle in half steps so no step is over a walker's), `wallAt`, `clearAt` against a room's solids, `ceilingAt`, the sphere's facets over a point, which bounds the view, and `fill`, the picture that fills the view from a crossing's handover point), Node-loadable; `BL.deferred.load(group)`, which answers at once where a group's code is inline (the portable page, the source tree) and otherwise fetches the one same-origin file `npm run site` moved it to (`BL.deferredScripts`); the walk-in LED ball (sixteen screen lunes and a logo layer lit by `show` through `glow`/`highlight`), its bridge, the doors whose outer faces are the ball's own turning strips moved with each leaf (`setDoors`), the keypad (`padRig`: `choose` lights a key, `lock` hangs a padlock over a locked room's number, `say` writes its window, scrolling what will not fit) and, on a plate under the doorway's, the DIRECTORY button (`directoryKey`), which, tapped or with D at the keypad, opens the room directory (`directory`, every number and its room, the chosen one lit and a locked one padlocked) in the board dialog, where a tap on a row or a number key chooses that room as the keypad does (`onChoose`, from beside the keypad only, `canChoose`); the keypad's window runs the chosen room's name across it, the dim passage behind the doors ending in a hanging curtain with a hidden pocket past it (`PASSAGE`, `SPOTS`), and the plug across the doorway that no walker, jumper or jetpack crosses; and the doorway's controller (`create` from the hub, after the crew), every frame of it from the Ooga's own eyes: keypad engagement (the view glides into first person as the Ooga turns to the physical keypad), single selection, the account's answer, the room readied only after Enter while the Ooga turns to the doors (the deferred code, `timechainInterior.prepare` a slice a frame, its picture hung where it fills the eye's view from the handover point, the island's view out of the passage for the way back, the sphere's heartbeat while that runs long; Back cancels it), the reservation (docs/timechain-sphere/doorway-contract.md through net.js's `askDoor` and friends), the doors parting, a walk along a curve onto the passage's axis and an inbound hand over without a fade (`go(..., true)`) from the same eye; the return also preserves that eye under `keep`, while `release` fades out over rebuilding the island, then walks back out from the room's own hand over, spectator doors from the room's `transit`, and the jetpack and the ordinary camera given back once the doors close. The view and mode the visitor walked up to the keypad in (`pilot.viewState`: first person, shoulder, orbit or bird's-eye, carry or combat, the look from the Ooga's heading, pitch and zoom) ride `world.sphereVisit.view` into the room, which gives them back once its guided arrival is done (`pilot.adopt`); the room's own at Exit ride `world.sphereExit.view` back out, and the bridge gives them back once the doors close. Solids count crossings on an upward ray, so a collision shell must be closed: the screen stays off it and the doorway's cut between the skins is walled in the shell |
| `scene-timechain.js`; deferred: `timechain-data.js`, `timechain-blocks.js`, `timechain-charts.js`, `timechain-room-models.js`, `model-viewer.js`, `timechain-party-radio.js`, `timechain-room-*.js`, `timechain-interior.js` | the rooms' scene: the shell, always registered, whose `prepare(place, ctx)` resolves the visit (the keypad's commit on `world.sphereVisit`, else this tab's last visit in sessionStorage repeated only as it was, else solo when signed out, else the keypad with the room chosen) and loads the deferred group; and the room itself: the timechainindex.com holdings feed (off under `nosim` unless `timechain=1`), the mempool.space block and mining reader (`create` per room, a page-wide `cache`, one request in flight per dataset, backoff, Retry-After, hidden-tab pause, STALE kept, the 144-block window chained by parent hash, `summarize`, strict `parse`; off as the holdings feed is), the native chart painters (`PANELS`, the ribbon's cards, the holdings; source, window, units and freshness on every panel), the shell every room shares and the kit each kind builds with (`timechain-room-models.js`: the sphere's wall and its screen, one canvas wrapped round the dome by its curve with its seam over the door, a node a metre back so the curve's axis is the sphere's; the floor's canvas or a raked hall's terraces; the door bay, vestibule, Exit button and lock; `plan` per room, cached: a kind's merged static parts, named nodes, solids, seats, lights, its `dome` paint (or, for a dome a kind repaints as it plays, `dome(paint, { w, h, smooth })`: a small canvas drawn up smooth, which `build` hands over as its `dome` node) and `floor` paint (`floor(paint, { scale, emissive })` for one a kind repaints, `scale` pixels a metre so a grid of that pitch falls on whole pixels, handed over as `build`'s `floor` node) and its `live` pictures on the dome; `build` a visit's nodes, `prepareSteps` its construction a step a frame), each kind in its own file (`timechain-room-<kind>.js` on `BL.timechainRoomKinds`: `plan(K, L, room)`, optional `steps` and `door`, and `animate` for what moves, allocation-free, with optional `tap`, `near` and `dispose`; Satoshi's Temple's synthesizes its bell, gong, singing bowl and wind bells in pooled voices, and the Mining Museum's exhibits, a tap or Space in front of one, open in the model viewer), the model viewer (`model-viewer.js`, `BL.modelViewer.open({ title, help, count, index, item(i) })`: one `#model-modal` dialog drawing an item's geometry in Canvas 2D, lit as the icon renderer lights, on a canvas the visitor turns with a drag or the keys and zooms with the wheel or a pinch, beside its kicker, name, account, stats, note and links, paged with the arrows; its faces drawn in the order a BSP tree built once a model gives from the eye, exact from any side, where a sort by depth lets a small part on a big face fall behind it at some angles; it draws only on a change, never on a timer; the room kit's `solo` merges a builder's parts into the one geometry it shows. The museum's models keep every part laid on another at least 2 mm proud of it, so no two faces looking the same way share a plane where they overlap and the hall's depth test never flickers between them; on its plinth each exhibit is plugged in: its cords (`lead`) run over the plinth's back edge and down to plates on its back, the outlets the machine really takes (`POWER`: NEMA 5-15 at 120 V for the early rigs, a wall wart's for the Bitaxe, 6-15 or 6-20 at 240 V for the Antminers' C13 and C19 cords, a 240 V PDU's C19 sockets for the S21's P13 cord and the Proto Rig's three C20 cords, a red IEC 60309 three-phase socket for the hydro) beside an Ethernet jack, each plate under its cord where it comes over the edge (two 120 V cords that come over side by side share a duplex, as the pools' rigs on each side do) and in the cords' order, so none crosses another; in the viewer its plugs lie unplugged behind it), the Block Party's radio (`timechain-party-radio.js`: commercial-free channels (`STATIONS`: EDM Sessions from radio.co, then four of SomaFM's) whose streams answer cross-origin, the DJ changing channel from behind the booth (`tune`, `next`; this page only), played through a gain and read through a low-pass by an analyser, and, whole, by a second analyser whose spectrum in log bands and wave (`spectrum`, `scope`) the booth's screen and the LED wall round the room show; each frame `meter` cuts the newest samples into blocks of a hundredth of a second by absolute sample number, so the frame rate never shapes the beat, and the pure `tracker` finds the tempo by autocorrelation (each lag scored with its double and quadruple, leant toward 125 BPM) and the phase from the onsets' Fourier component, trusted from a confidence of 0.25 until it falls under 0.18; a context only after a gesture, the page-wide mute on the gain while the analysis goes on, a watchdog and backoff, and a plain element where Web Audio hands a live stream silence or the stream stops answering cross-origin; off with the room's other feeds under `nosim` and `timechain=0`; the room eases its own beat onto the tracker's tempo and phase, never backward, falling back to 124 BPM, and starts each drop on a bar), and the visit (`create`): first person, shoulder and orbit, no weapons or jetpack, guided first-person arrival and exit, shared presence in `sphere-<id>` or none at all, solids to stand on (stepped up onto from `STEP_UP` down, the taller ones shouldered round as on the island through the crew's `shoulderObstacle` hooks, only where the pass can be made) and seats to sit in (Space facing one, `crew.sitPlayer`), the kind's tap targets (one whose spec names its meshes, `pick`, is hit only where one of them is the nearest thing drawn under the pointer, the room's body and its live pictures in the way: `sharpPicker`, a coarse grid of triangles a mesh, made a mesh a frame from a second into the visit; a kit node marked `proxy` is never drawn and stands in for a part too fine to test) and its own Space action where the Ooga stands (`near`), where it hears from (`built.ear`), each room's own door (a kind's `door`: the bay's, frame's, header's and leaves' colours, its leaves' marks through `leafFace`, its trim in its plan, `doorway` and `doorBulbs` for strings of bulbs it lights), and in the Conference Room a keypad beside the doors that locks a shared room with a 4-digit PIN and its LOCK key (`net.lockRoom`; the Worker's `roomLocks` enforces it, docs/timechain-sphere/doorway-contract.md), opened again by anyone inside with UNLOCK or by the last one leaving, its PIN shown on that keypad, while in use, to those inside alone (`net.state.roomPin`), and a ROOM LOCKED sign across them while it is, the room's hum, its live pictures repainted when what they print changes, block ages ticking each second, on retained canvas textures (one picture repainted a frame at most, rotating through them), `prepare` (the room readied at the island's doorway after Enter, built and painted over frames, seen once from its handover point and kept on the GPU, which the visit adopts so arriving builds, paints and uploads nothing), and the guided walks starting and ending at the crossing's handover points |
| `routes.js` | `BL.routes`: the site's address and name, `title(entry)` (the tab, search and card title) and every route (`path`, `scene`, optional `place`, `title`, `description`, card `image`), home first. Plain data, also read by `scripts/site.mjs` and `scripts/cards.mjs` in Node |
| `router.js` | `current`, `arrive`, and Back and Forward through `go`, over `routes.js`. Every scene change pushes a history entry carrying the scene, so scenes sharing an address still step back and forth. The address is a path when served at a directory and a hash (`#/oogarally`) from disk or a named file |
| `deferred.js`, `scene-deferred.js` | code a hosted page fetches when needed: `load(group)` (one same-origin script per group, a base group first, the scene's chunk preloaded beside it), `scene(id, null, wip)` stable shells whose `prepare` fetches `<id>-scene` and which a second definition never replaces (a chunk also returns at its head when its group is already present), only an error (never a timeout) moving a retry to a fresh address, a failed chunk probed with a HEAD whose 404 sets `stale` and calls `onStale` once (the director's `hud.offerReload`: "Ooga Booga Land was updated · Reload"), `prefetch`/`prefetchScene` for an approach or a hover that leads into a scene (a mouth, its sign, a cabinet, a ₿IFRÖST window on arrival), never for a camera view. The groups are the Timechain rooms, DSB Land, the arcade's games, Cloud Hopper's code and art (`cloud-hopper`, fetched by the hub's `prepare` only for a cabinet arrival; only approaching or hovering that Arcade cabinet prefetches it, with speculation off under Save-Data and deliberate play still allowed), the poker room, Ooga Drop's diver and sound, Rally's, Mine's and Orbit's bases and scenes, the Lightning Jet Hangar, HALLOWEEN's hunt and its tracks, the terrain cache, the lab's wallpaper and the Canvas 2D renderer. The portable page and source tree carry every group inline and fetch nothing |
| `build-tasks.js` | `run(steps, scope, signal)` drains a construction generator one slice per task (`SLICE_MS`, 40, behind a black fade); a builder checks in with `yield* pause(slice)` once per row or model piece, and with `if (due(slice, 63))` in front of it in a tight per-face loop (the builder file's own shorthands, which reach this file only when sliced, so unsliced builds run without it); `measure` and `cpu` count only synchronous construction for the boot tier |
| `door-ready.js` | a place behind a door, readied while the visitor is still this side of it (the hub's Factory shield): `create({ code, extra, scope, steps })`, then `approach()` to ask speculatively (`deferred.prefetch`), `commit()` to ask for real (a failure stays `failed` until the next `commit`, so nothing retries by itself), `step(budget)` once a frame for the construction, `ready`, `working`, `extraDone` (code it opens without has come or will not) and `reset`. Pure, loaded in Node by the suite |
| `icon-renderer.js` | the HUD's portrait, gear and locker icons, the Canvas fallback's face path without the rest of that renderer, which loads only on fallback |
| `factory-scene-models.js`, `arcade-scene-models.js` | the Factory's and Arcade's scene-only geometry, deferred with their scenes; the shared hub pieces stay in `factory-models.js` and `arcade-models.js` |
| `director.js` | the app: renderer, frame loop, governor, keys, donations, routing, transitions. A throw in the frame loop is reported once and, if it goes on, offers Reload (`FRAME_ERRORS_MAX`) |

Load-order constraints beyond "after what it uses":
- `chain.js` loads after `mempool.js`, whose socket it subscribes to, and before any scene reads a snapshot.
- `emoji-data.js` then `reaction-policy.js` load before `net.js` and `reactions.js` (after `players.js`, under whose list its first bar stands); the Worker imports the same two files in `protocol.js`.
- `game-wire.js` and `game-timeline.js` load before `net.js` (the Worker imports both too); `game-net.js` loads right after `net.js`, whose `state` it reads.
- `weather.js` (after `models.js`), `pool-layout.js`, `pool-models.js` (after it and `terrain.js`), `pool-water.js`, `pool-paintings.js` (both after `pool-models.js`; the paintings also after `jumbotron.js` and `game.js`), `pool-wildlife.js` and `solid-props.js` load before `scene-hub.js`, which reads them all at load.
- `rocket-parts.js` and `mine-rigs.js` load before `game.js`, which validates saves with them. `mine-models.js` loads after `hub-models.js`.
- `pad-focus.js` and `pad-settings.js` load after `controls-help.js` and before `director.js`, which creates the screen; `gamepad.js` reaches `BL.padFocus` only at run time.
- `retro-games.js` loads after `jumbotron.js` (its font) and `arcade-games.js`, and before `scene-carnival.js`, which stages it.
- `factory-kiosk-core.js` (the kiosk's square glass size) loads before `factory-models.js`, which sizes the kiosk's glass from it. `factory-kiosk.js`, the kiosk's flow, loads after `jumbotron.js` (its font) and stays inline on every page: it is the payment path, so a tab left open across a deploy can still donate.
- Game scenes load after the hub and the lab, so the hub stays the landing scene, and before `director.js`.
- The ₿IFRÖST files load after `scene-hub.js` in the order models, gate, isle, Heimdall, window, scene: each reads `bifrost-models.js` at load, and the isle also reads the gate. `scene-hub.js` loads before them, so it reaches them only at run time: the arch, the islet and Heimdall in `enter`, the window and the islet's `update` from its frame update.
- `repo-island-data.js` loads before `repo-feed.js`, and `repo-island-models.js` (after `totem-models.js`, `hub-models.js`, `pool-models.js`, `timechain-models.js`, `build-tasks.js` and `compact-geometry.js`, which it reads) before `repo-island.js`; the hub reaches both only at run time. The Worker imports the data file directly.
- `tess.js` loads before `hub-guide.js` and `factory-greeter.js`, which read it at load. `scene-hub.js` loads before them all, so it reaches `BL.hubGuide` only at run time, in `enter`.
- `timechain-rooms.js` and `deferred.js` load before `timechain-models.js` and `timechain-gate.js`, which the hub reaches at run time. `scene-timechain.js` loads after the hub (a game scene's place) and the deferred group (`data-defer="timechain"`) right after it, rooms' modules in the order data, blocks, charts, room models, the ten `timechain-room-<kind>.js`, interior: the room kinds and the interior read the others at load. On a hosted page the group runs only once fetched, after the director has booted, so nothing outside it may read it at load.

**The visibility stack.** The hub's outlines and cover are the largest system after the scenes. `rock-guides.js` owns the stone the camera looks through and builds `slopeGuides`, `caveGuides`, `holeGuides` and `wallApertures` itself; nothing else creates them, so they load before it. `object-guides.js` is the registry every outline goes through (`pileGuides` and `mirrorGuides` join as providers), `sight-guides.js` draws the result (the hub runs two, one per camera view), and `camera-cover.js` and `banana-cover.js` paint interiors the camera ends up inside. Geometry is built once per island and memoised; fade and camera state belong to the visit. A build-side change must hold for every later visit, and state must return to base in `leave`. `enter` builds `rockGuides` through `ensureRockGuides` and it stays there: deferring it past the first paint stalls the loading curtain. Its shared wall build (`rockGuides.prepareSteps`) is a generator a released hub's return runs in slices before `enter`, with the island, the Mempool island's meshes and collision trees, ₿IFRÖST's islet meshes (`bifrostIsle.prepareSteps`) and the crew's rigs (`prepareHubSteps`); the rest of that return's `enter` runs in slices too (`enterSteps`, pausing at its measured costly statements), and its first covered-view pass spreads its outline bakes over slices (`objectGuides` `collect(..., deferContours)`, `bakeNext`, `ensureContours`). A new statement in the hub's `enter` that costs more than a few milliseconds gets a `yield* pause(slice)` before it. Anything new that reads `headquarters.rockGuides` must guard, as `resetSurface` and `dispose` do, or call `ensureRockGuides()`.

Both renderers implement the same surface: `render(root, camera, opts)` returning whether a frame was drawn, `project(x, y, z, out)`, `ray(px, py, camera, out)`, `resize`, `setQuality`, `prepareScene(liveSet)` (behind the entry fade, before first visible use), `releaseGeometry`, `prepareGeometry(geometry)` (uploads ahead of a first draw, true once it can draw; always true on Canvas 2D), `prepareSteps(root, rigs, slice)` (a generator uploading, a slice at a time, the meshes a first frame of `root` would draw; empty on Canvas 2D), `releaseUnused(liveSet)`, `dispose`, and the getters `kind`, `quality`, `ready`, `failure`, `stats` (`records`, `active`, `mirrorResources`, `culled`, `drawn`), `size`. Gameplay code stays renderer-agnostic; anything new goes into both.

## Scenes and the director

One scene is active at a time. `director.js` owns what outlives a scene: the renderer (WebGL2, or Canvas 2D when the context or programs fail), the overlay canvas, the frame loop, governor, quality auto-tier, minute housekeeping, keydown routing (Shift+Delete and Right Shift+R there, M to the microphone in voice where the scene sets `micKey`, L to the room's voice links where it takes them, every other key to `active.onKey`, then H to the raised hand when the scene did not use it), the visibility pause, `game`, `world`, the donation subscription (opened once the account's look at `/api/me` has answered, so the Worker can turn real mode on first), routing (`router.js`), transitions, the `[data-scene]` HUD sections, the banner's line under its title wherever a section carries `data-zone-line` (`zoneMap.line`), the header logo (`.home-link`, which goes to the hub from any other scene, as a website's logo goes home), `__ooga`, and `destroy` on pagehide. A scene builds its root, camera, input, HUD and systems in `enter` and drops them in `leave`. `world.mine` holds Ooga Mine's whole run across visits; it is cleared when a finished run is left or played again. `world.factoryNode` is the page's one Lightning Factory node (`factoryFeed.node`), made by the first scene that needs it and ticked by whichever is active, so walking in from the island carries on the hall's show; it is never disposed. Its `cooker` holds the page's tally for the cooker's donations board (tips cooked, bananas out, the last tip, shown rounded), and a visit left mid-show settles its tips and bananas into it. ₿IFRÖST keeps its pictures on `world` across visits: `world.bifrostView`, the island's view out of the portal, retaken each time an Ooga walks in (absent until one has), and `world.windowViews`, the chamber's window pictures by their looks' key (`dsb` for DSB Land's, `poker:<theme>` for the poker room's in each cave theme), each taken once a page. The Timechain Sphere's doorway hands its traveler over on `world` too: `world.sphereVisit` (the keypad's commit: room, shared or solo, the Ooga, its jetpack as it was), read once by the rooms' `prepare`; `world.sphereExit` (the same on the way out, with the eye's `pose` at the hand over), read once by the hub, which walks the Ooga out of the passage; `world.sphereKeypad`, a room chosen in advance for the keypad when a signed-in arrival at a room's address is sent outside; `world.sphereKeep`, the island's meshes kept on the GPU while a visit lasts, so walking back out uploads nothing; and `world.sphereOutView`, the island's view out of the passage, retaken on each crossing in, which the room hangs at the end of its vestibule.

The quality auto-tier only steps down. It judges the interval between delivered frames, never the cost of issuing one, because GL calls return before the GPU draws. A window closes at 45 frames or 900 ms, and two slow windows in a row must agree before it steps. Unfocused frames, transitions and the debug `advance` are not evidence. `tierFromBoot` starts the page a tier or two down when the first scene's build passes `BOOT_MEDIUM` (3600 ms) or `BOOT_LOW` (5400 ms), set against the hub building in about 2.35 s on an M4 Max. An island restored from the terrain cache counts as the build it replaced: the director adds the cache's `credit`, the build CPU its Worker measured less the restore's own. Initial GPU upload time is excluded from this input so a shader/upload delay cannot change the opening tier. Check anything that slows the hub build against them with `__ooga.timing` (`ready` minus `renderer`).

The scene contract, as `scene-lab.js` and `scene-hub.js` implement it:

| Member | Role |
|---|---|
| `id` | the key in `BL.scenes` and in `?scene=` |
| `prepare(place, ctx)` | optional; runs before `enter`, at boot and in every transition: null to go ahead, `{ scene, place }` to arrive there instead, or a promise of either (deferred code loading), while the screen holds dark; a rejection fades back to the scene being left (`router.abandon` restores the address) |
| `stay()` | optional; a transition this scene started was abandoned because the next scene's `prepare` rejected, and the visitor stays: put back what the departure set up (its leaving flags, the pilot it stopped, a crossing that would at once start again). Called once the fade back has finished, when `go` accepts again |
| `enter(ctx)` | build the visit; set `root`, `camera`, `input`, `debug` on the scene object |
| `update(dt, elapsed)` | per frame, allocation-free; `elapsed` is scene time, restarting at 0 on every enter |
| `overlay(dt)` | draw the frame's 2D overlay (`fx.drawOverlay`); the director paints the fade after it |
| `onDonation(donation, bananas)` | a donation event while this scene is active; `bananas` is the API's `{ exact, rounded }` in real mode, null when simulated. Every scene counts `exact` into the tally and names `rounded` in its toasts, while its show keeps the game's one to twelve. In real mode the director sets `world.level` every step from the API's pile (its count less `eatPerHour` since it arrived, whole bananas), so every browser shows the same one |
| `onSeen(id, sats, due, left)` | optional; an on-chain payment the API saw before its block, while this scene is active: true when the scene showed it (the Factory's kiosk, for its own invoice), and otherwise the director toasts one the visitor left waiting |
| `onKey(e)` | keys the director does not handle itself |
| `micKey` | legacy scene metadata, no longer read by the director: M controls environment sound everywhere; microphone and remote voice muting stay in the Oogas panel |
| `onLootCleared()` | the locker was emptied with Shift+Delete |
| `renderOpts` | any of `clear`, `sky`, `ground`, `sun`, `light`, `shadowCenter`, `shadowExtent`, `bloomStrength`; with `horizon` and `zenith` the sky pass draws (plus `moon`, `stars`, `time`); `clouds` (0 to 1); `windTime` (seconds; holds the WebGL2 wind sway where it stands, the Canvas 2D renderer has no sway); `lights`, a `Float32Array` of up to `POINT_LIGHT_CAPACITY` `x, y, z, radius, r, g, b, 0` entries in priority order with `lightCount`, clamped to the tier; `spotLight`, up to two cone lights of 12 floats each (position and reach, direction and the cone's outer cosine, colour and its inner cosine; WebGL draws both, Canvas 2D the first); `fog` (rgb) with `fogNear` and `fogFar`, off when absent; `pictureDim` (0 to 1, 1 when absent), how bright every picture drawn from an image is but light added to the view (canvas-backed pictures keep theirs), on both renderers |
| `leave(destroy)` | tear the visit down; returns `{ targets }`, the input target count read before `input.dispose`. The hub under the keep policy suspends instead (its graph, guides and solids stay) unless `destroy` is true |
| `buildSteps(ctx)` | optional; a generator of pure construction the director runs in slices once the fade is black, before `leave` and `enter`; publishes only finished caches |
| `enterSteps(ctx)` | optional; `enter` as a generator, which the director runs in slices after `leave` (then `renderer.prepareSteps` for its first frame): scene updates and draws pause, scene HUD controls are inert and input is held (except the recovery Reload button), `__ooga.scene` is null, and the screen holds (black, or an instant crossing's last frame) until its last slice. One that finishes in its first slice completes in the same frame. Every hub arrival through `swap` uses it: a kept hub resume finishes in its first slice; a rebuilt hub or its first visit after a non-hub boot is sliced under either policy. Cold hub boot uses the same sliced path, including its preparatory builders and initial renderer uploads; scenes without `enterSteps` keep the ordinary `enter` path |
| `retained` | the hub under the keep policy: `enter` resumes the kept visit instead of building one |
| `retainedRoot` | the hub's suspended graph, or `null`; read-only diagnostics can traverse it without invoking cache readers |
| `stats()` | `visibleNodes`, `allNodes`, `tweens`, `targets` plus the stats of fx, crates, crew, the pile and, in the hub, the critter counts |
| `liveGeometry(set)` | add geometry kept off the graph but wanted on the GPU (the cavemen's swapped heads) |
| `keeps` | optional; ids of scenes whose builders stay warm while this one runs, so the director's release on a switch spares them (the island keeps the Lightning Factory's, which it walks straight into); released with this scene when it is left for one that does not keep them |
| `root`, `camera`, `input`, `debug` | set in `enter`; `input` and `debug` nulled in `leave` |
| `agent`, `agentView`, `agentControls`, `agentHandoff` | the Agent hooks the director reads; the hub and lab set all four, the rally, drop and orbit only `agent` and `agentControls` |
| `inMotion` | getter, true while the pile or fx animate; the governor keeps full rate for it even unfocused |
| `warming` | optional getter, true for a frame the scene spent part of readying another behind a door; the quality auto-tier does not judge those frames |
| `cachesReleased()` | optional; the director has just dropped the builder caches another scene kept warm for this one (`keeps`), so what this scene holds for a quick return no longer applies (the Lightning Factory's `world.factoryKeep`) |
| `cabinetMenu` | optional getter, true while a custom starting menu is showing (Mine); the director combines it with `[data-intro]` visibility to keep the menu within the glass and expand only when play starts |

Both renderers expose `geometryRecords()` as an on-demand, read-only iterator of resident GPU geometry keys; Canvas 2D returns an empty iterator. Diagnostic traversal reports graph visibility, not frustum or draw visibility; it must not invoke tracked cache readers or change ownership.

`ctx` is one object for the page life: `{ renderer, canvas, overlay, game, world, go, lootEnabled, testBananas, agentPlay, hubPolicy, from, place, signal, cabinet }`. `cabinet` is the page-wide `arcadeView` controller; games use its `active` state to hand it return travel and, except Cloud Hopper, replace their old entrance animations. Cloud Hopper retains the ascent on every attempt. `signal` aborts when the visit ends, for listeners and fetches; `hubPolicy` is `keep` or `release` (`?hubcache=`). `go(id, place)` starts a transition and returns true (throws on an unknown id; ignored, returning false, while one runs); `from` is the scene being left, `null` on boot; `place` is the routed place to arrive at, or `null`. The hub ignores `from` and starts on its landing view, or on the `navigate` view `place` names.

An ordinary transition fades to black for 0.25 s, then in one frame (or, for a scene with `enterSteps`, from that frame on in slices): `leaving.leave()`, `clearTweens()`, `enter(next)`, `renderer.releaseUnused(live)` with every geometry under the new root plus its `liveGeometry` and any retained hub geometry, then 0.25 s back in. An `instant` transition keeps the left scene playing while the next prepares and holds its last frame while the next builds/enters, without a fade; a wait past one second shows a spinner. The Timechain return under `release` deliberately uses the ordinary fade. While no scene is active during sliced entry, donations are recorded in the tally but have no toast or delivery, and simulated donations do not grow the pile. Under `?debug=1` the director throws if the leave contract is broken: children left on the old root, input targets reported by `leave()`, tweens surviving `clearTweens`, or more GPU records than that combined live set. The director aborts the old visit signal after `leave`; each arrival gets a fresh one. `leave` must also clear timers, dispose its systems, input and HUD, and null the visit's module-level references; a kept hub instead suspends its controls and systems while retaining its graph until resume or `leave(true)`.

## Engine patterns to keep

- **Allocation-free frame loop.** Nothing in `frame`, `updateCaveman`, `drawOverlay` or the renderers allocates per frame. Hoist literals, write into scratch objects (`setVec`, `SCREEN`, `MUZZLE`), reuse arrays. Director simulation, input and overlay work goes in `step`, shared by `frame` and the debug `advance`, so fixed-step checks exercise the displayed-frame path.
- **Pool and cap.** Particles, bullets, bubbles, sleep marks, crates, inventory and the pile have fixed capacities. Anything spawned repeatedly needs a pool or a cap; if in doubt, spawn it in a loop and read `stats()`.
- **Instance by geometry.** Nodes sharing a geometry object are one draw call. Reuse geometry; cache builders with `cached()` or a `Map` keyed by parameters. Cached character clones share immutable geometry but own their nodes, quaternions and part arrays; remap every array member to the clone.
- **Shared builders and index sorts.** Voxel props use `makeVox`, `voxCoords` and `voxelGeometry`. Bounding-volume indexes use `BL.math.sortByKey` with one `sortScratch` allocation, never comparator sorts. Geometry and island-query changes claimed to preserve behaviour need before/after golden hashes.
- **Release what you stop using.** Nodes removed from the graph leave picking and the GPU at the next housekeeping pass. Call `input.remove` for anything you registered.
- **Deterministic cosmetics.** Contributor traits hash from the handle, loot from the donation id via `fnv1a`. `Math.random` only for throwaway effects; `randomInt` (crypto) only where fairness matters, such as the die.
- **Frame rate is sacred.** Full rate whenever focused. Reduce work through the quality tiers (pixel density, effects), never by throttling frames.
- **Camera moves only on input.** No auto-orbit, drift or inertia. Held keys and sticks move the target or the driven caveman while held; damping settles within a few frames.
- **Games run on a fixed step.** The rally, the drop and the orbit accumulate frame time into 1/120 s substeps (at most four a frame), so handling is identical at any frame rate; `pose` and the camera run once per frame. Track data lives in typed arrays or preallocated objects, decor bakes into one merged geometry per sector so the renderer culls a sector at a time, and dynamic batches are fixed-capacity `instanceData`. The drop's course is laid once per visit by flying the diver's own physics without frames.
- **Sound is synthesized and pooled.** Two live radio streams are exceptions, each an `<audio>` element on origins the content policy names in `media-src`: DSB Land's (`dsb-radio.js`) and the Timechain Sphere's Block Party's (`timechain-party-radio.js`, its channels on radio.co and SomaFM), which also goes through Web Audio so its lights can follow the beat, so its streams must answer cross-origin. DSB's supplied entrance recordings are the other exception: embedded in the portable build, same-origin MP3 assets loaded on activation in the Cloudflare and GitHub Pages builds. Every node is built once; a cue only schedules automation on a pooled voice. The context is created only from a gesture the browser has activated (`navigator.userActivation`), and `leave` closes it.
- **The clock is the hub's.** `daylight.sample` writes the hub's `RENDER_OPTS` in place every frame; its phases drive lamps, critters, quotes and toasts. The lab passes no sky; the drop samples the same clock so its sky matches. Working, chilling and sleeping come from contribution age, never from the clock.
- **Free rotation is a quaternion.** Bodies that turn about all three axes (the plane, the diver) carry `node.quaternion` and integrate a world-frame angular velocity with `quat.integrate`; Euler `rotation` stays for anything that only yaws or swings.

## Checks and trust

Validate at boundaries: localStorage on load (`game.load`), donation text (`donations.sanitize`), browser capability (renderer fallback, context loss, program failure) and pointer input (cancellation, second finger, stale targets). Inside those boundaries trust the code. No guards for states our own code cannot produce; a thrown error beats a silent fallback.

## Adding things

**A contributor.** Add one file, `src/characters/<handle>.js`, and nothing else: no shared file, list or test changes. A plain IIFE (`const BL = window.BL;`) calling `BL.characters.add({ handle, joined, lastCommit, look, voice, github, display, dress })`:
- `joined` (Unix seconds) orders the roster, which sets digit keys, grid slots and the default pick. `lastCommit` is the activity until the backend refreshes it.
- `look` overrides the hashed `skin`, `hair`, `fur` and `height` and carries body flags: `build: "slim"`, `bald`, `cleanShaven`, `wideEyes`, `hairless`, `noBrow`, `noPupils`, `face` (`"nose"`, `"smirk"`, `"beard"`, `"none"`), `eyeColor` with `eyeGlow`, `hatY` (sixteenths of height). `crew.js` reads the behaviour flags (`gasMask`, `cigarette`, `skater`, `pumpkin`, `stoneAxe`, `nunchaku`, `recipe`, `petRidesHat`); `contributors.js` reads `maintainer`, which keeps that character working at every project cave until the backend reports its commits. A character that builds `parts.jetpack` (with `parts.jetFlame` under it) flies for good: `crew.js` drives it as it drives the jetpack, and `removeJetpack` only cuts its thrust. A character that builds `parts.dog` (a pet, placed where it stands by the Ooga) has it walk at its heel (a pet whose node lists `legs`, its four leg nodes hinged at the hips, fore left, fore right, hind left, hind right, and optionally a `tail`, walks on them in DSB Land's dogs' gait, `crew.QUAD_GAIT` and `poseQuadruped`, which `rescue-dogs.js` reads too; `packAt` places its little jetpack; timechainb's lion cub is one): `crew.js` lifts it off the body, checks its own floor/clearance and follows on foot or with a scaled existing jetpack while its owner flies. Authored and adopted dogs share `crew.flyCompanion` steering and `COMPANION_FLIGHT` constants; `rescue-dogs.js` adapts its retained array slot to the helper. Landing sweeps clearance under gravity and only recovers onto checked ground. Hidden owners hide the pet/equipment, NPC pause holds it, and disposal removes it. With `petRidesHat` the pet rides the hat instead of flying: the moment the owner puts a jetpack on, grounded or not, it leaps up onto the top of the head and what it wears (a loop measured once per head, `crew.hatRide`: on a brim round the crown, or a bare head's top), trots round it facing its way, held to the head's transform by `crew.poseHatRide` and hidden with the head in first person, wears no jetpack, sits still under Calm, stays up through landings while the jetpack is worn, and when it comes off hops down beside the owner, clear of walls, into the same checked landing. Remote-player model copies use the same `crew.createPets` follower through the scene crew's `pets` controller: each page animates the dog against its own ground and clearance from the existing owner pose and `jet` state. The pool removes the detached pet when its owner leaves, and hidden owners hide their pets. No pet messages are sent. The same opt-in puts the pet on the torso's front while settled in the hammock (ahead of the hat); `poseStomachRide` inherits the torso's current sway, stays visible in first person, and on exit or a tip hops back to the hat or, off the bed, to the seat's checked stand-up spot (`hammock.landing`, through the crew's `petLanding`). `pet-reactions.js` makes that resting pet face the nearest other visible live body within four metres, including the viewer's own live body and excluding NPCs and ghosts. Each page selects locally and synthesizes a pooled high-pitched double yap only after a browser gesture and while environment sound is enabled; leaving range, hiding, exiting rest, muting or disposing stops the voice. No additional transport is used.
- `voice` is `{ poke, idle: [...] }`. `github` is the login behind the handle; without it, stats keyed by a different login never reach the Ooga, who then sleeps in the HQ forever. `display` is an optional in-game name; matching and hashes still key on `github || handle`.
- `dress` hooks run inside `models.buildCaveman` with the build kit `k` (`h`, `u`, `rand`, `P`, `color(hex)`, `jit`, `skinJ`, `hairJ`, `leopard`, `vg`, `root`, `parts`, `legH` (the hip's height over the soles, for anything stood on the ground beside the Ooga), `fine` (the refined grid's cells to an authored unit, for a `refine` hook), `armX`, `eyeCells`, and the settable `nose`, `lid`, `headEmissive`): `torso(k, v)`, `club(k)` (returns `{ voxels, palette, goldPalette }` or `{ default, gold }` geometry, plus `rest` and `carry` angles, `{ x, y, z }` in YXZ order with `y` optional: `{ x: 0, y: Math.PI / 2, z: tilt }` turns a blade a quarter about its haft, as w-s-bitcoin's axe and MrHodlX's stick do), `gear(k)`, `skull(k, v)` (true replaces the head block), `crown(k, v)`, `eyes(k, v)` (true replaces the eyes), `mark(k, v)`, `hatY(k)`, `headgear(k)`, `extras(k)`, `tint(k, palette)` (return a second palette and every voxel part bakes again under it; `crew.js` switches between the two on its own timer), `refine(k, part, v, side)` (paints a refined part's fine map, `k.fine` cells to an authored unit, before it bakes: `head`, `closed` and `portrait`, `torso`, `arm`, `fingers` (one hidden cell), and `leg` with its side; hotpixelgroup's wrist bands are one). Every other hook draws on the authored grid. Keep accessory builders and their caches in the file. Hooks draw from the shared `k.rand` in build order, so never reorder another character's draws.
- Run `npm run build` to regenerate `characters.gen.js`, then check the "characters" unit check. `npm run characters` lists the cast one line each.

`npm run characters:json` prints every character as the row a backend would hold (`handle`, `github_login`, `display`, `joined_at`, `last_commit_at`, `look`, `voice`). Keep the field names in step with that table. `dress` has no column: geometry stays code. When the backend arrives it owns the dates, as `applyActivity` and `applySnapshot` already overwrite `lastCommit`.

**A prop in the lab.** Build geometry in `models.labRoom` from `box`, `lathe`, `tube`, `ring`, `polyline`, `merge`, or voxels. Return the node in `equipment` if interactive, register it in `scene-lab.js` with `addProp` (which unregisters it in `leave`) using an `owner.kind`, then handle that kind in `tooltipFor` and `onTap`.

**A swag item.** Add an entry to `SWAG` in `models.js`: `id`, `name`, `tier`, `slot` (`head`, `hand`, `face`, `gun`), optional `offset`, `rotation`, `float`, `spin`, and a `build` returning geometry. Weapons are skins: `skin: "club"` or `skin: "gun"` turns the caveman's own club or rifle gold, and `build` only feeds the locker icon. Sizes are fractions of caveman height; the head is about 0.28 wide and 0.22 deep at hair level, so anything wrapping it needs radius 0.38 or more. Stacks cap at `STACK_MAX` (9): `game.lootFor` rolls only items under the cap and `addItem` refuses the tenth.

**A caveman behaviour.** Add state to the `cave` object in `crew.js`, drive it in `updateCaveman`, reset it in `resetPose` if it changes limbs. One-off arm animations must settle back (see cheer and catch). Workers use `cave.work.phase`: `outbound`, `station`, `shoot`, `return`, then `reload` at the pile; `act.kind` tracks meals, builds, wandering, idling and player control. A donation pauses worker movement, bursts and reload timers while the free hand cheers; keep the work phase, ammunition and rifle grip so work resumes. Strolls run only when the scene passes `wanderSpot`; `walkToSlot` leaves a stroller alone unless forced (`rush` forces). Walkers stand on `groundAt`, so a scene with terrain passes its `heightAt`. The hub supplies `bedRoute` and `bedRouteClear` together; shortcuts reuse `headquartersSleep.clearSegment`, and swept walking handles props and other actors.

**Set dressing.** Dress a place from `BL.dressing`, never with one-off props: make a `set()`, `put` kit pieces and `cable` lamps, `build` once and cache it, then add its `solid` (registered with the scene's solids or obstacles), `hang` and `glow` nodes; only `solid` collides. A new piece is a function in `KIT` writing voxels into the three layers; `crate`, `coalCrate` and `barrel` are `MESHES` instead, the hub's cartoon crate and barrel fitted to the piece and baked into `solid` (their `keep` voxels, the coal, still write). Every placed piece is pokeable: `build` also returns `picks` (kind, variant, pick sphere), the hub turns them into off-graph pick targets with `addPieceTargets`, and a new piece gets its tooltip, particles and lines in `PIECES` in `scene-hub.js`. Each mouth's facade comes from `THEMES` in `scene-hub.js`, its sign from `caveSign(name, icon)`. Horizon scenery, palms and the dressing's hanging and glow meshes carry `sightHidden`, because the outline registry bakes every other node under the root and would otherwise cost hundreds of MB and most of a second of boot. A set's `lights` go into the scene's point lights after its own lamps, so the tiers keep torches and fires first. `mineModels.LAYOUT.DRESS_AT` is also the mine walkers' obstacle list. The hub's instanced lawn is relaid when `island.path.version` moves and skipped on Canvas 2D.

**Cave banners.** Choose `[side, variant]` through `THEMES.banners` in `scene-hub.js`, never arbitrary coordinates in `pieces`. The shared slots place the whole banner behind and outside the lantern posts, with a gap from their bases, arms and glass. Every corner of its footprint must be closer to its own mouth than every other mouth, including sealed and unused caves; `checkCaveBanner` enforces this when the facade is baked. Keep its bounds in step with the kit's crossbar and foot if the geometry changes. Check the rendered cloth and emblem clear the rock and nearby props; do not move a banner in front of a lamp to make it visible. A banner's emblem is `BANNER_ART` in `dressing.js`: 48 x 72 rows of quarter-block pixels, baked once as row runs into the hanging mesh, or the glow mesh for its `glow` inks.

**A lamp in the hub.** Build a geometry whose flame faces are the only emissive ones, then `addLamp(node, LAMP.kind, x, y, z)` in `scene-hub.js`: `node.glow` follows the dusk ramp in stagger order and lit lamps fill the point-light array in registration order. A critter kind is a batch in `critters.js`: fixed capacity, homes seeded by `mulberry32`, motion written straight into `instanceData`, population eased toward a phase- and tier-scaled target.

**A rally track.** Add a definition to `TRACKS` in `race-track.js`: `id`, `name`, a `theme` from `THEMES`, `laps`, `seed`, `hazard`, medal `targets` in milliseconds and `points` through `P(x, z, y, { w, bank, surface, wall, curb, pad })`. `slabY` is the driving surface and `roadY` the centreline profile launches read. `build` takes `rain` (greys the outdoor sky, wets the slab, raises `slipAt`); the scene rolls it with `randomInt` per load and `?debug=1&rain=1` or `rain=0` pins it. The spline must not cross itself at the same height; a bridge (`surface: SURF.board`) may cross a lower road. A `SURF.gap` point opens a jump fed by a `pad: 1` on the point before; keep the run-up straight. Then read `spawns` and `checkpoints`, run the `race tracks` check, and make sure every racer finishes under the autopilot.

**A mount.** Add an entry to `MOUNTS` in `racers.js` (top speed, acceleration, brake, turn, drift turn, mass, hop, off-road factor, body radius, garage bars) and a builder in `race-models.js` returning `{ node, seatY, seatZ }` plus the parts `pose` animates. Dinos draw the three shared `DINO_HIDES` so each part is one instanced draw per hide, and their legs pivot at the hip.

**An item.** Add its name to `ITEMS` and `ITEM_NAMES` and its odds to `ODDS` in `race-items.js`, handle it in `use`, and give the AI a rule in `drive`.

**A donation-driven event.** Hook `onDonation` in the scene module. Never change the event shape `{ id, sats, handle, message, at }`; the backend will emit exactly that.

**A cave.** The eight mouths exist in the terrain, one per slot in `caves.js`; a slot with a `repo` is also a work site the crew shoots bananas into. Opening one is one line there (`scene`, `status: "open"`, `name`) plus a scene module registering `BL.scenes.<scene>`, and a `routes.js` entry and `npm run cards` if it should have an address. `buildMouth` dresses an open mouth by its `scene`. The new scene needs Escape and a `leave` action back to `"hub"`, and its own `data-scene` sections in `src/index.html` if its HUD differs.

**A scene.** An IIFE loaded after the systems it uses and before `director.js`, registering every contract member. `update` and `overlay` allocate nothing; `leave` clears timers, disposes crates, pile, crew, fx and the pilot, removes every input target it added, empties `root`, disposes `input` and `hud`, nulls the visit's references and returns `{ targets }`. The camera comes from `pilot.create` with the scene's presets and bounds (`clampTarget`, `clampCamera`): call `readInput` before the crew moves and `update` after, spread `pilot.hooks` into the input hooks, route `act`, `reset-view`, Escape and 0 to it, and `bind` it the shared systems. Give the crew `walkable` and `useNear`, and answer `onTap` for every prop worth a reaction. A scene where the visitor drives an Ooga passes `mayPossess` (`net.mayDrive`) to the pilot, picks its starting Ooga by the same rule, declares its zone (`voiceZone` on the scene object), reports the driven Ooga with `BL.net.setBody`, `sendPose` and `setHealth` every frame and clears it in `leave`, gives it its footsteps (`BL.footsteps.create()` in `enter`, `update` with the driven Ooga every frame after the crew moves, `dispose` in `leave`), and shows the others there through a `remotePlayers` pool (crew `outsideActors` and `outsideActorHeight`, nameplates in its overlay): the director ordinarily clears the body on a switch, but synchronous Arcade hall game handoffs preserve it and active live video cabinets keep their room anchor, and the room hears and shows only players with one (see `docs/auth-and-presence.md`). Register it in the suite (see Testing). A game exits to `home`: Ooga Arcade when one of its cabinets started the game, the island otherwise, and its exits say which (`hud.leaveTo(scene, home)` in `enter`). A visitor plays their assigned Ooga (`net.ownAmong`), as they drive only it on the island: an Ooga list holds to their row (`hud.holdRow`) and a pilot picker stops turning. A new game also gets a cabinet: an entry in `arcadeModels.GAMES`, a `PALETTES` row and a loop in `DRAW`, and a slot in `CABINETS` to stand in (the mezzanine's eight play the retro games). A game whose code a hosted page should fetch only when needed is a deferred scene: add its id to the shells in `scene-deferred.js` (with its `wip` there too while it is one), tag its scripts `data-defer="<id>-scene"` in `src/index.html` (a base group its models share with other scenes gets its own tag and a `SCENE_BASE` entry in `deferred.js`), register it with `BL.deferred.scene(id, definition)` carrying `enter`, `update`, `leave` and every other member in the definition itself (a late second copy of the entire chunk returns at its head; the existing Drop and Arcade `prepare` assignments after the definition are safe inside that whole-chunk guard, though new definitions should carry their members together), and put its pure construction in `buildSteps`, not `prepare`. A way out that can be refused (a scene with `buildSteps`) needs the leaving scene's `stay`.

**A HUD element.** Markup in `src/index.html`, styles in `src/style.css` with the existing tokens, wiring in `hud.js` behind a small method, a `data-action` button if it triggers behaviour. Dialogs (the donation popup via `openFeed`, `#recipe` via `openRecipe`/`closeRecipe`) keep their keys from the scene. Every readable board (the jumbotron, the Mempool island's chain board, weather sign and wall paintings) opens in the one `#board-modal` through `hud.openBoard(board)`: a board is any object with `title`, `help`, `canvas`, `count`, `index`, `caption`, `note`, `version` and `go(index)` (plus an optional `wide`, which widens the dialog on desktop, and `links`, a list of `{ href, label }`, links out under its note, each opening in a new tab; `pick(x, y)`, which hears a press on its picture in the canvas's pixels, and `key(key)`, a key it takes for itself, true when it did; either answers "close" to close the dialog), the dialog pages it with chevrons, one dot a page and the arrow keys, and repaints when `version` moves (`hud.updateBoard` each frame). Change a board, never the dialog. Counts from a thousand up go through `game.formatLarge`. Blur controls after use so shortcuts keep working. The director writes the active scene to `body[data-active-scene]` (never `data-scene`, which marks the scene sections); the games use it to hide the sheet and the pile count. A game's side panels fold one way everywhere: the panel carries `fold-panel` with `fold-left` or `fold-right`, a `panel-fold` button with `data-fold="<panel id>"` sits in its head, and a `panel-edge` tab with the same `data-fold` follows as its next sibling; `hud.js` toggles `data-folded` and the stylesheet does the rest. A panel title is `.panel-head h3`; a heading inside a panel is `.section-head`. Accent-coloured text on wood uses `--accent-text`; `--accent` stays for fills and borders.

**Panel text.** Nothing truncates but a player's or donor's name in a list row (the Ooga Boogas and live players rows, the Bananas panel's donors and recent donations), which keeps to one line and ends in an ellipsis, its whole name its `title`; anywhere else no `text-overflow: ellipsis`: size a slot for its longest words, stack a stat as label over value over note (`.mine-row`), and let a name that will not fit wrap (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`). A help or keys line is one line, or two split on purpose at a clause (`\n` under `white-space: pre-line`); headings and labels never wrap. Shorten the words before shrinking the type: labels 8.5–9.5px, notes 9.5–10px, values 14px. Prose in intro and results dialogs may wrap. Every popup closes on a press outside it through `hud.dismissOutside(node, close)`; results and intro screens are game state, and only their buttons move on.

**A game's title card.** A `<section class="race-results game-intro" data-intro="<scene id>">` in the game's div, on the mine card's pattern: kicker, sign title, a 16x16 pixel badge, the goal line, six `.game-rules` with 9x9 icons, a `.game-intro-go` button with `data-action="intro-go"`, and the exit (`data-action="leave"`, labelled by `hud.leaveTo`). The director shows it on every arrival; its button, Enter, Space or Escape close it, and no key reaches the scene while it shows. A `[data-coarse]` line swaps in touch wording.

**A retro game.** Each mezzanine cabinet plays one (`arcadeModels.RETRO`, in the cabinets' order), its id its scene's. A game is its factory in `retro-games.js`, keeping the interface in the file's header: `update` and `draw` allocate nothing and play on the kit's `stepper`, `start(seed)` seeds the run's `rng` so a seed replays it, `status` is written in place, `over()` marks the moment the run is decided, won or lost, and `onEnd(score)` ends it once after the end beat, and `draw` paints the whole 160x120 screen, its attract (PRESS START and the best) while idle. `scene-carnival.js` stages it unchanged. With it go its `stars` and `tickets`, its cap in `game.js`'s `ARCADE_MAX`, its title card, its header line (`Ooga Arcade · <name>`) and its nav (Sound and Arcade, as the carnival games' navs) in `src/index.html`, its hall loop in `arcadeModels.RETRO_DRAW`, its tune in `carnivalAudio.SONGS` and a `CHIP_CUES` entry in `scene-carnival.js` mapping every cue name it calls (a name left out throws); its suite step is `carnivalPlay`, a Space every step, so a run must end under that rhythm within 400 steps.

## Debug

`?debug=1` exposes `window.__ooga`. It owns the page-level handles (`game`, `renderer`, `input`, `scene`, `go`, `advance(seconds, dt)`, `housekeep`, `stats`, `timing`, `frameInterval`, `renderedFrames`, `level`, `transitioning`, `fade`, `cabinet`) and forwards a fixed list of names to the active scene's `debug` object. `__ooga.cabinet` is the page-level visit controller (`active`, `phase`, `held` and its methods), not a per-scene forwarded handle. The `for (const key of [...])` loop at the foot of `director.js` is the reliable inventory of forwarded handles: a new handle is one name there plus the field on the scene's `debug`. Each game's handle (`__ooga.race`, `drop`, `orbit`, `mine`) carries `phase` and `simulate(seconds)`, which runs the scene's own update in fixed steps without frames; `__ooga.racers.autopilot = true` lets the AI drive the visitor. Per-character weapon state is on `crew.cavemen` entries. In a Timechain room, `__ooga.timechain` holds the room, the visit, its `layout`, `built` nodes, the data `reader`, the `guide` and `doors` states, the picture `slots`, `exitEye` (where the walk out hands over) and `exit()`; on the island, `BL.scenes.hub.debug.timechainIsland.gate.debug` is the doorway's state (with `handZ` and `outZ`, its two hand-over points).

Debug keys: B adds test bananas only with `?debug=1` (100, or `&b=`), L a legendary tip (simulated only, and only where the room does not take L for voice links), P fills the pile, Shift+Delete or Shift+Backspace clear the locker (loot on), Right Shift+R resets everything. Right Shift+A plays the active scene's Agent where one is provided; hub companions are selected directly. Left Shift runs while moving an Ooga or gorilla. `BL.agent.createPlay` lives in the director, reaches scenes as `ctx.agentPlay` and takes its keys in the capture phase. The Konami code toggles the feed panel on any scene without debug (`__ooga.feedPanel`); it subscribes and ticks only while open. Keys are ignored in text fields and on auto-repeat.

Every Ooga owns its jetpack; an abyss fall returns the Ooga to the pile and clears its ammunition. Jetpack tuning lives in `crew.js` (`JET_*`); the hub's `jetpackAllowed` refuses it underground except up the basement shaft. The hub's 18 clouds are decorative: unchanged cloud meshes and lighting, nine evenly spaced clouds on each of two authored circular tracks (`CLOUD_TRACKS`, radii 36 and 40), no fades, respawns, walking surfaces, collision, rider state or movement planning. Each lane's clouds share a speed so they keep their spacing; their long axes remain horizontal and tangent to the island. Between each two of the four bridges they cross (the north, Mempool, Repo Island and Timechain bridges, `CLOUD_CROSSINGS`) each lane swings once, sin³ of the way across and as high as its stretch is long next to the longest (`CLOUD_SWING`), so every swing climbs no faster than the three equal swings did and the inner lane, between -4 and 2 metres, dips under every bridge and the outer, 2 to 11 metres, crests over it, rising and dipping between them. Broad crossing shoulders keep the full meshes clear of the structures. The tracks occupy disjoint radial bands; cutaway views preserve their current wave height. They are `sightHidden` so visibility guides do not register them.

URL flags:
- `/<route>` when served (the site, or `npm run serve`), `#/<route>` from disk or a named file (`oogaboogaland.html#/oogarally`), opens a routed scene or place: `oogarally`, `oogadrop`, `oogaorbit`, `oogamine`, `dsb`, `lightning` (the Lightning Factory), `jet` (the Lightning Jet Hangar), `oogaarcade`, the Timechain Sphere's rooms `sphere-0` … `sphere-9` (a signed-in arrival with no visit of its own in this tab goes to the keypad with the room chosen), and the hub's `entropylab`, `bca`, `sphere` (the doors and keypad), `repos` (Repo Island, off the bridge facing the plaza), `mempool` (its `lab`, `bca`, `timechain` and `mempool` views, the last arriving on the chamber floor under the lake; the `lab` scene itself stays debug-only) and `donate` (its landing view with the donation popup open). Every later arrival pushes its address (the bare page for a scene without a route), so reload and Back and Forward follow the visitor; an unknown or closed route lands on the hub. A route wins over `?scene=<id>`, which opens any scene by id (unknown ids land on the hub); served, the router drops it from the address, and from disk a written hash (`#/` home after leaving a game) outranks it. `?canvas2d=1` forces the fallback. `?hubcache=auto|keep|release` chooses the hub policy: auto keeps the built hub at a usable memory report of at least 8 GiB, releases it below that, and uses a fine pointer to keep or a coarse pointer to release when no usable report exists. Keep and release override that choice. A released hub's island is rebuilt behind the fade on return. `?compactGeometry=0` leaves immutable render meshes unpacked (`compact-geometry.js` packs them by default). The island cache, which on the staged site restores the island from IndexedDB at boot and, after a miss, has a Worker store it at idle, follows the same auto rule (and `hubcache=keep` or `release`); `?terrainworker=1` forces it on and `?terrainworker=0` off; `?lazyhub=1` hangs plain bedding in HQ and the basement and swaps in each LifeHash bed in idle time once the view comes within 18 m. `?yaw=` sets the starting camera angle.
- Local animation preview: `http://localhost:8787/?testdonations=1` enables L on the mainland or in the Lightning Factory once the Worker enables real donations. It plays twelve visual bananas through the current scene's delivery pool, without a donation event, payment, saved donation or leaderboard change. It is available signed in or out, only on loopback hosts, and ignores keys in dialogs and fields. The normal donation button still creates real invoices.
- Real donations run locally through OBL's Worker: `cd worker && npm run dev` (`http://localhost:8787`) beside bananapayserver's staging config under `wrangler dev` on another port; wrangler's dev registry joins the `DONATIONS` binding by name, and bananapayserver's `docs/testing.md` has its regtest stack for paying invoices. `?nosim=1` silences the simulator, keeps the mempool socket closed and skips the first visit's Bananas panel (`welcome=1` asks for it anyway, `welcome=0` turns it off anywhere; see `docs/first-visit-bananas.md`); `?mempool=0` closes the socket alone; `?rate=0` keeps the 2140data price socket closed (as `nosim` does); `?oogatron=0` stops the stats poll; `?totems=0` stops the totems' GitHub poll; `?repos=0` stops Repo Island's (`repo-feed.js`); `?jet=0` keeps the hangar's boards from reading GitHub, so they show the cache or the bake; `?net=0` keeps the account module from asking `/api/me`, so donations stay simulated on the Cloudflare site too; `?chain=0` stops the REST polling and the price socket, and `?chain=esplora` or `?chain=https://host/api` pins the provider. `__ooga.mempool` (`emit`, `parse`) drives the weather offline, `__ooga.weather.apply` takes a snapshot's arrivals, `__ooga.poolIsland.preview` stands the lake at a backlog (`lake(mvb)`, null to hand it back), throws a block (`block()`) and puts an Ooga to sleep or wakes it by name (`sleep(name, asleep)`), and `BL.chain` readers take raw payloads.
- With `debug=1`: `prepass=0|1` forces the depth prepass off or on for every tier and GPU; `birdseye=1` offers bird's-eye combat in every scene that does not keep it out (`noOverhead`), for Oogas and gorillas, as the island offered it before the ghost hunt became its only home, so the checks of the overhead camera and the island's cutaway keep running; `bananas=` sets the pile level (clamped to `pile.MAX_BANANAS`, ten million); `hour=` pins the clock's starting hour, `time=HHMM` freezes it, `day=` picks the day of year, `latitude=` changes the test latitude and `daylen=` runs a day in that many seconds (`__ooga.setHour(h, daylen, day)` resets it); `anomaly=N` has the hangar's engine anomalies come by themselves every N seconds, even under `nosim`; `meltdown=N` does the same for a crew-room worker's meltdown; `shimmer=N` has the hangar's faint shape come by itself, N s before each, even under `nosim`; `totemglow=1` holds all four repository totems' animated glow patterns, and every Repo Island totem's, at full strength, including under `nosim`, without changing feed timestamps or triggering celebrations; `repospin=0` holds Repo Island still; `tessnews=YYYY-MM-DD` plays Tess's signed-in island greeting with the news from that day on, signed in or not; `loot=1` turns loot on (it ships off behind `LOOT_DEFAULT`); `gorillamove=1` (also `climbers=1`) enables detached selection and movement orders: click a gorilla to highlight it, then click a world destination to plan walking and climbing from its current position. Further clicks redirect it; Escape clears selection. Blocked climbs stay in place for inspection.
- Fixtures, applied at startup only: `mag=1|2` grants spare magazines; `solo=1&character=<handle>` builds only that Ooga (none without a valid handle, across scenes); `weapon=1|2` holds the primary or secondary; `ammo=N` (0–30) or `ammo=unlimited` sets that actor's magazine; `character=`, `firstperson=1`, `jetpack=1` and `view=`. A signed-out `debug=1` hub that names no `character=` hands out no Ooga, so a fixture starts from a still view (`release` and `pose=` with `mode=detached` set one up too); no visitor ever has that view. In the hub the position readout is on with debug (`pos=0` hides it); clicking it copies a `pose=` URL, and `pos`, `body`, `head`, `camera`, `look` and `mode=carry|shoulder|first-person|birds-eye|orbit|eye-level` set a pose by hand, and `combat=1|0` sets the camera look mode; it does not enable game combat.
- **Unfinished games.** A game still being built sets `wip: true` on its scene object, and `director.js` unregisters it unless the page opts in: `?wip=<scene id>` opens that game without `debug`, `wip=1` opens every one. A closed scene's cave, if it has one, seals to rock, `?scene=` lands on the hub and `go()` throws. Its saves are never touched. Opening a game for everyone is deleting its `wip: true`, and for a deferred scene its id from `WIP` in `scene-deferred.js`.

Ordinary walking scenes hide the shared Ooga and clanker portrait and overhead health bars (`hud.create` defaults `healthEnabled` to false; `remotePlayers.drawNames` can opt in for games). In the hub, local Ooga and clanker names appear above heads only while hovered, including the locally controlled character, with speech above the name. Games keep their own health HUDs. The hub creates no loose boxes, barrels, rubble or breakable rocks and no breakables tracker; the shared primitives and breakables module remain for games. Hub donations and contribution events retain their notices and character reactions without confetti or fireworks.

Mempool island effects resume within 44 m of its centre and pause beyond 52 m, measured horizontally from the camera, its target or the driven Ooga/gorilla. While distant, the island and cloud deck remain visible, but weather simulation and sound, water effects, wildlife and display updates rest. The feed keeps current targets; displays refresh on return, animations advance only for nearby time, and distant block events create no queued lightning or cubes. `weather.pause` keeps the static deck, while game mode's `weather.hold` hides it too. Shared material shimmer and foliage sway still render normally. `__ooga.poolIsland.effectsActive` exposes the proximity gate under debug.

The hub's lab, Factory, B.C.A. and Bifröst portal effects resume within 24 m and pause beyond 32 m of the camera, its target or the driven character, measured in 3D. The Bifröst bridge also wakes from its home end. Portal geometry stays visible; ripple clocks and body sampling rest, and a resumed body atlas samples current poses instead of sweeping across the pause. Entrances, projectile clipping, the Factory feed, office routines and Heimdall stay live. Portal windows keep their eye-dependent projection and visibility current while their decorative animation rests; distant Factory events enqueue no visual bursts. Ambient butterflies, fireflies and embers use the same 24/32 m gate per instance. Their shapes, populations and day/night transitions remain, with local clocks preserving their resting poses until approached. Other scenes opt in through the optional `activeAt` callback on `critters.create`. Gulls and boats keep moving at every distance, because their silhouettes remain visible from the hub and would look frozen under a proximity gate.

## Combat and the mirror

Shared crew and clanker damage simulation defaults off (`combatEnabled: false`). The hub's `setGameMode` enables it only for a running game and clears damage, knockout gear, attacks, fire, rage and captures when the game ends; other walking scenes leave it off. The hub and other walking scenes separately opt into `weaponsEnabled`, which restores Ooga swings, rifle shots, reloads and magazine swaps with normal animations and ammunition use, without enabling character damage, health timers or knockouts. `weaponInteraction(owner, node)` admits selected scenery contacts outside combat: the HUB accepts the Matrix mirror, so mirror contacts still make ripples, and the arcade's ghost arena its idle herald. Other targets and melee scenery probes remain game-only. The one exception is the hub hammock's sway: a banana round crossing it (`hub-hammock.js` `crosses`) rocks it outside combat too, with no damage, nothing kept and the round flying on; it is not a weapon contact, so it is not admitted through `weaponInteraction`. The ghost lobby uses `pilot.setMenuOpen` to release mouse-look and suspend held actions while its buttons are visible, then restores combat crosshairs when it closes; carry remains unchanged. `crew.setCombatEnabled` and `clankers.setCombatEnabled` retain the damage and reaction gates. X still changes cursor-look versus click-and-drag camera behavior, including first person and, where a game offers it (the ghost hunt's `birdsEye`), bird's-eye; Cloud Hopper's run is combat alone through the hub's `combatOnly`, at the shoulder or in first person; `pilot.refreshCombat` and `clankerPlay.refreshCombat` update action availability without changing that view. NPC work shots and work reactions remain decorative; a ghost track's rounds are visual replays in its ghost colour.

DSB Land, ₿IFRÖST's chamber and its tunnel, the Lightning Factory, its Jet Hangar and Ooga Arcade's hall have no weapons, on a direct route too. Each parks the driven Ooga's loadout as it arrives (`crew.parkWeapons`, held on the weapon itself, after `pilot.arrive`) and hands it back as it leaves (`unparkWeapons`, before the crew is disposed); `remember` reads the parked loadout into `carried`, so the rifle, rounds and magazines come back in the next scene with weapons, however many such places lie between. With nothing owned, spare magazines parked too, combat's crosshair is a single dot (`data-unarmed`, centred), the HUD's weapon buttons stay hidden and every weapon key and button is refused, while X, the combat look and DSB's tomatoes stay. A remote pool made with `unarmed` shows other players there with neither club nor gun.

Ooga Arcade parks the visitor's outside primary/rifle ownership and held slot for the visit, so ordinary weapon actions are unavailable in the hall while combat mouse-look remains usable. Only a running ghost hunt's hunters get a gun, the ghost blaster with unlimited rounds, and never a primary; the arena itself, its lobby, spectators and hallway hunt snapshots grant nothing. Arcade finishes live remote poses by hiding clubs throughout the visit and guns on anyone but those hunters; the shared remote renderer keeps its outside weapon behavior. Leaving restores the outside loadout before the crew is disposed. Carried focus restores soft mouse-look across scene loads without requesting pointer lock without a gesture; deliberate cursor release remains released. Both retained and rebuilt mainland returns place the visitor inside the Arcade overhang. The hall creates no simulated Ooga visitors or machine-playing bots; live remote avatars and game enemies remain. Maintenance Clankers ignore players when choosing jobs and routes and do not greet, follow or react to their actions. The central stage's decorative roots do not block its low steps; cabinet collision remains. Air hockey's playable solo opponent is a separate game-mode concern.

The arcade's ghost hunt requires combat camera mode while it runs (its GAME MODE, which the pilot's `combatOnly` follows), until dawn, idle or disposal. The controllers' optional `combatOnly` policy forces combat on game entry, possession and Ooga pose restoration, and consumes X/portrait attempts to enter carry. Readiness still gates sleeping/stunned actors' aiming and attacks. Menus, Tab and Escape may release cursor focus without changing combat intent. Other games' damage capability alone does not require combat camera mode.

GAME MODE temporarily hides the shared sheet and floating Oogatron boards through `hud.setPanelsHidden`, preserving their open and fold states and restoring them on exit.

Local settled character poses use the hub's `animationInterval`: full rate within 20 m of the eye, view target or driven actor, 10 Hz out to 40 m and 5 Hz beyond. Controlled actors, active game actors, movements, contacts and pose transitions stay at full rate. Behavior, work and sleep clocks always run every frame. A live NPC host with followers always poses at full rate because those transforms are the shared snapshot; a follower may sample only its local settled limb interpolation while receiving root and work state every frame.

A ready melee swing deals 5 damage (10 with nunchaku), a full hold charge doubles it, a gun bash 4 and an AK banana 2 (5 on a head hit). A gorilla pound deals 10–20 damage according to charge. The reusable breakables module gives boxes, barrels and rocks 4, 9 and 17 health (the hub creates none); vegetation is not a target. The mirror is unbreakable: no damage, per-panel health, cracks, shards or healing state. Weapon contacts make ripples and shots can cross its plane. The actor barrier and locked gate remain. The glyph room, its wave and lever are retired; no room, doorway or world glyph buffers are built or updated. Free and trailing cameras keep the ordinary scene and overlay rendering when passing over or inside the OBL cave behind the mirror; the cave has no fullscreen blackout. Portal contact glyph effects are independent.


New event geometry and pickup nodes must stay capped and take part in `liveGeometry` and scene disposal. Contributor activity is local snapshot data (`docs/activity-contract.md`), separate from human control presence.

## Testing

**When to test.** The maintainer play-tests every change, so a test run is never automatic. Default to "wait". Run "fast" or "full" only when the task says so. With "wait", a change ends with `npm run build` and `node --check` on every touched file (the build never parses JS, so a stray brace otherwise blanks the page) and nothing else: no scenes, no probe scripts, no screenshots. "fast" means the scenes you touched; "full" means `npm run test:full`. Batch the work first and test once at the end, never after each edit.

**Scenes are the unit of testing.** `npm test -- race mine` runs the global tier plus those two scenes, one Chrome each, side by side. Pick scenes from the files you touched:

For an explicitly scoped browser check that must not rerun the global tier, `node test/run.mjs --browser-only <scene> …` uses the same scene selection, assertions, watchdogs and failure ledger. `ONLY` can narrow those named scenes as usual. The option rejects `full`, `unit`, `perf`, dedicated checks and runs without a named scene; default commands and CI still include their normal global tier. Report browser-only results as partial coverage.
